# Implementation Status

What exists in the code (`src/engine/`, `src/editor/`, `native/`, `src-tauri/`) versus what the decision records in [docs/decisions/](../decisions/) describe, one row per record that has code (0001 is a process record; there is no 0084). Rendering is Babylon.js only (0075, 0081); three.js remains only for the OBJ/MTL/FBX and glTF loaders (0081, 0088). Rows were last audited against the code on 2026-11-07. A row may say **(Proposed)** after its status when the code is built but the record isn't accepted yet. The v1 demo is a sample project in `samples/flappy/` (ADR 0031). User guides are in [docs/wiki/guide/](../wiki/guide/).

Legend: **Done** = implemented as decided · **Partial** = works, with listed gaps · **Not started**.

| Record | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [0002](../decisions/0002-typescript-first-esm.md) TypeScript-first | Done | `strict` TS everywhere; Babel removed. `noImplicitOverride` is off so game scripts can write `update()` without `override`. |
| [0003](../decisions/0003-tauri-platform-browser-runtime.md) Tauri, browser runtime | Partial | Runs in the browser and has a Tauri v2 shell in `src-tauri/`. `tauri.conf.json` is the desktop editor's shell (`frontendDist` `dist/editor/`, dev server :3000): a borderless Projects window (`projects.html`) and a hidden editor window (`editor.html`, ADR 0059, 0082). Scripts: `tauri:dev`, `tauri:build`. Games ship through the generated Tauri app of `npm run export -- --target desktop` (ADR 0041), not this shell. Platform adapter in `src/engine/platform/`: `isTauri()`, and an internal `FileSystem` (`TauriFileSystem` over the fs and dialog plugins; `BrowserFileSystem` is read-only over HTTP) for the editor (0026). In Chromium browsers the editor opens folders writably through its own `FsaFileSystem` (0038). `pickFolder` takes an optional `defaultPath` (recent projects reopen a folder the fs scope no longer allows through the dialog, starting there). Tauri JS packages are imported lazily, so headless tests and browser builds never load them; they're external in the engine library and absent from its declarations. `capabilities/default.json` grants app-data read/write plus the fs commands with no fixed paths: a folder picked with `pickFolder()` is added to the fs scope recursively, and `tauri-plugin-persisted-scope` (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`) keeps it across sessions. Window commands are granted for the Projects and editor windows (show, hide, size, drag, destroy, create). The icons are Myco branding now; the identifier `com.foxfirestudios.myco.editor` is a placeholder. Not done: Linux/WebKitGTK testing, a WebGL2 baseline (the renderer is WebGPU-first, 0075). |
| [0004](../decisions/0004-threejs-render-pipeline.md) three.js render pipeline | Superseded by 0075 | The renderer is Babylon.js only (0075, 0081; `render/babylon/`): WebGPU with a WebGL2 fallback, a frame graph per World (`frameGraph.ts`), orthographic/perspective `Camera`, `SpriteRenderer`, `MeshRenderer`, `Light`, material assets and shader graphs compiled to WGSL. Textures take their filter, wrap and mipmaps from the image's import settings sidecar, and sprites can draw a slice of a sheet (0040). Post-processing is 0051's (frame-graph tasks and the finish pass), skyboxes and HDR environments 0053's, render textures 0109's. three.js remains only for the OBJ, MTL, FBX and glTF loaders (`scene/models.ts`, `scene/gltf.ts`; 0081, 0088). Hand-written shader assets are 0106's. Missing: animation (out of scope). |
| [0005](../decisions/0005-physics-backend.md) Physics | Partial | Rapier 2D behind the backend-neutral Physics plugin (`plugins/physics/runtime/`, ADR 0135, 0137; the Rapier backend is `plugins/physics-rapier/runtime/rapier2d.ts`): `RigidBody2D`, `BoxCollider2D`, `CircleCollider2D`, `PolygonCollider2D` and `EdgeCollider2D` (0039: convex hull, or concave outlines split into convex parts by `physics/polygon.ts` and built as one compound shape; edges are polylines), triggers, collisions, named layers + matrix, raycast. 3D on Rapier 3D (0045), loaded on demand: `RigidBody`, `BoxCollider`, `SphereCollider`, `CapsuleCollider`, `MeshCollider` (exact or convex, from a model mesh or the node's MeshRenderer), triggers, collisions, `CollisionEvents`/`TriggerEvents`, layers, `Physics.raycast`. Since 2026-11-07, for both: `CharacterController`/`CharacterController2D` (0091), joints (0092), shape casts and overlaps, `Stay` events, interpolation and child colliders that follow their node (0093). The backend interface exists since 0137 (`plugins/physics/runtime/backend.ts`; components talk to the shared sync core, which drives a backend). Missing: PhysX (the owner prefers to stay on Rapier; 0005's PhysX plan is unrevisited), snapshots. |
| [0006](../decisions/0006-audio-howler-and-three.md) Audio | Superseded by 0157 | `AudioSource` and `Audio.playOneShot` on Howler. Spatial audio is 0074's (Howler's spatial API; three's `PositionalAudio` is not used). Mixer buses: 0097. Missing: `Audio.music` (0006's music API; use `AudioSource` or `Audio.bus("Music")`). |
| [0007](../decisions/0007-hidden-ecs-with-lifecycle-scripting.md) Hidden ECS, lifecycle | Done | Hooks run in Unity order, indexed by implemented hook, parents before children, `executionOrder`, deferred destroys. Awake waits until a node is first active (Unity semantics). |
| [0008](../decisions/0008-gameobjects-as-node-graph.md) Node graph | Done | Generational handles, hierarchy in typed arrays, active-in-hierarchy, path `find`, optional Transform. |
| [0009](../decisions/0009-components-attached-or-as-child-nodes.md) Component nodes | Done | `addChildBehavior`, `owner`, `getBehaviorInComponents`, multiple components per type, reparent → normal node with a warning. |
| [0010](../decisions/0010-engine-graph-authoritative-over-threejs.md) Engine authoritative; three.js hidden | Done | The renderer mirrors only nodes with something to draw, flat, from the engine's world matrices each frame (Babylon meshes with their world matrix set from the engine's, 0075; `Object3D` mirroring went with three.js's render backend, 0081); no three.js or Babylon types in `src/engine/index.ts`. glTF subtree exception not needed: imported models (0044, 0088) are flat meshes baked into model space. Custom shaders are engine shader assets and graphs compiled to WGSL (0047, 0075, 0106), not the GLSL `Shader` assets the record describes. |
| [0011](../decisions/0011-frame-loop-and-system-order.md) Frame loop | Done | rAF, fixed-step accumulator (1/60, max 5 substeps, 0.25 s clamp), steps 1–10 in order, `engine.step(dt)` for tests. A per-World `input` phase runs after the clock, before `start()` (0335). Each World steps on its own fixed rate (`tickRate`, 0032), every unpaused World once per frame in creation order. |
| [0012](../decisions/0012-ecs-storage-implementation.md) ECS storage | Partial | In-house: generational entities, typed-array hierarchy/transforms, sparse-set data components. Behavior storage is per-entity arrays + per-type lists rather than dense sparse sets. **Benchmarks** (`bench/ecs.bench.ts`, `npm run bench -- --reporter verbose`; `npm run bench:memory`; Node, 2026-11-04; at 1k / 10k / 50k): an empty step 0.003 ms. A seek system over N agents (two data components, 10 f32 fields): 0.42 / 3.5 / 19.4 ms per step with row views, 0.045 / 0.36 / 1.8 ms with 0064's columns, against 0.016 / 0.16 / 0.78 ms for a plain Float32Array loop (views were about 27× that; columns are about 2.3×). Moving N transforms through the Transform facade plus propagation: 0.25 / 2.4 / 13.4 ms. Creating and destroying 1% of N per step: 0.05 / 0.39 / 1.7 ms, under a scene or at the World root (the root case was quadratic, 166 ms at 50k, before `activeScene` stopped listing the root's children). Adding and removing a data component on 1%: 0.007 / 0.04 / 0.18 ms. A query matching 10% of N costs the same as iterating the matches. Memory: about 560–640 bytes per agent node. Not measured: render sync (no renderer in Node), a comparison with bitECS. |
| [0013](../decisions/0013-data-driven-scene-format.md) Scene format | Partial | JSON scenes/prefabs, type registry, standard-decorator fields, `$node` refs, `asset:` paths, nested prefabs, field / name / active / add / remove-component overrides, `worlds` and `tags` overrides (ADR 0042), list fields (`@field([T])`) and `Quaternion` fields, versioning + migrations (format 3: quaternion rotations, ADR 0163). Add/remove child-node overrides (`addNodes`, `removeNode`, ADR 0086; `$node` from an added node can't point at prefab-internal nodes); `{"$node": "/Name/Child"}` references persistent nodes by path from the World root; the TypeScript scene builder (`scene()`, `prefab()`, `ref()`, `asset()`, `src/engine/scene/sceneBuilder.ts`); `npm run rewrite -- <projectDir> [--check] [--dry-run]` rewrites scenes and prefabs to the latest format (`src/rewrite-cli/`). Missing: per-component field migrations for renamed or retyped fields (only whole-file migrations, `registerSceneMigration`, exist); designed in [0123](../decisions/0123-per-component-field-migrations.md), held back by the user until an engine major version. Editor side of name / active overrides on inner nodes: see 0122. Format 5: `BallJoint.swingLimit` → `swing` (ADR 0375, T43). |
| [0014](../decisions/0014-unified-2d-and-3d.md) Unified 2D/3D | Partial | One renderer; sorting layers; 1 unit = 1 m; 100 px/unit default, overridden per image by its sidecar (0040) and per SpriteRenderer; per-scene physics dimension (a scene that doesn't say gets 3D physics when it has 3D bodies or colliders, 0045); meshes depth-test and sprites draw over them. Sprites batch per atlas: thin instances of one quad per run of one texture in draw order, with the tint and slice UVs per instance (0108; with editor tools, in the scene view and editor play mode, sprites keep a mesh each for picking and outlines; games batch). Pixel-perfect cameras (`Camera.pixelPerfect`, `pixelSnapping`; 0108). Missing: the `scale2D` accessor (`position2D` and `rotation2D` exist). |
| [0015](../decisions/0015-vite-build-tooling.md) Vite | Done | Vite 8. `vite.config.ts` runs the dev server (:3000). `vite.player.config.ts` builds the game player (`dist/player/`, used by `npm run export`, ADR 0041). `vite.engine.config.ts` builds the engine library (`dist/engine/engine.js`, with three.js, Babylon.js, Rapier, Howler and Tauri packages external; the nav WASM and the UI font ship beside it) and `tsconfig.engine.json` emits its declarations with `stripInternal`, so no backend types leak (0010). Assets load through `Assets.fromGlob` over `import.meta.glob`. Every config compiles TS with `tsc` (`vite.shared.ts`) because oxc doesn't lower standard decorators. `vite.editor.config.ts` builds the editor (`editor.html`, `projects.html`) into `dist/editor/`; in dev it's served at `/editor.html`. `npm run build` is typecheck, then the player, the editor and the engine library. The multi-bundle layout came from the earlier `game-engine` repo. |
| [0016](../decisions/0016-world-root-and-scenes-as-nodes.md) World root, scenes as nodes | Done | Scene nodes under the root, additive loads, autoload persistence, `node.scene`, `GameObject.makePersistent`. **Scenes inside scenes**: a node whose `prefab` is a `*.scene.json` file is instanced as a scene node (scene-node flag, `SceneInstance` with its source, and a Transform so it can be placed) holding the file's top-level nodes, the same way `SceneManager.load` builds a scene under the World root. Overrides with an empty target apply to that scene node. Only scene nodes directly under the World root count as loaded scenes (`sceneNodes`, `activeScene`). Any other file is a prefab, a single node instanced as itself. Runtime and Editor Worlds exist. |
| [0017](../decisions/0017-user-systems-and-data-components.md) User systems, data, buffers | Done | `defineComponent` (f32/i32/u8), `defineBuffer`, `defineSystem` with phases, `before`/`after`, queries, `ctx.single`. Physics events also exposed as `TriggerEvents2D` / `CollisionEvents2D`. |
| [0018](../decisions/0018-promise-based-waits.md) Promise waits | Done | `wait.seconds/realtime/nextFrame/fixedStep/until`, dropped on disable/destroy, `abortSignal`. |
| [0019](../decisions/0019-public-api-conventions.md) API conventions | Done | `getBehavior` + `getComponent` aliases, degrees, live vectors (`transform.position`, `rigidBody.velocity`). Convention made explicit: `position` is local, `worldPosition` is world. |
| [0020](../decisions/0020-in-canvas-ui-with-uikit.md) In-canvas UI (uikit) | Partial (partly superseded by 0076; the interim `Text` by [0421](../decisions/0421-game-ui-is-built-only-from-ui-documents.md)) | In-canvas UI is built, on Yoga layout and Babylon GUI drawing (0076) rather than uikit (`@pmndrs/uikit` is no longer a dependency): components in `ui/components.ts` (`UICanvas`, `UIPanel`, `UIText`, `UIImage`, `UIButton`, `UIInputField`; 0069), screen and world canvases, UI-first pointer routing, and markup documents with stylesheets (0077, which also answers 0020's theming question). Fonts are project font assets over bundled Inter (`UIText.font`, 0076), not MSDF. The interim `Text` component (canvas texture, screen-space pass) was removed by 0421: its class, Babylon drawing and scene-view picking (the `rect` drawn shape) are gone, and UI documents with `UIText` (now with an outline) replace it. Missing: a Slider and other widgets, keyboard and gamepad navigation, MSDF fonts. |
| [0021](../decisions/0021-particles-with-three-quarks.md) Particles | Superseded by 0081 | three.quarks was removed with the three.js backend (0081). Particles run on the engine's own renderer-neutral simulation, drawn by Babylon.js (`scene/particles.ts`, `render/babylon/particles.ts`, 0075): the ParticleSystem component is 0057's, effects as graph assets and the Particle Graph are 0060's, GPU simulation is 0094's. |
| [0022](../decisions/0022-editor-is-a-v1-goal.md) Editor | Partial | Increments 1–15. **November 2026 editor batch**: the Project panel's settings are a category list (a dropdown under 250 px; labels stack in a narrow column); Add Component opens category submenus (built-ins by `componentCategories.ts`, scripts by folder, Data) with Recent and a flat search; play mode has a Gizmos toggle with per-kind filtering (overlay kinds on `addEditorOverlay`) and a Stats overlay (FPS, frame and step times, per-World update/fixed/render, draw calls, triangles, GPU objects, JS heap, nodes, nav agents); the live selection outline now draws in play. **Appearance and branding** (details in the 0025 row): Settings has General and Appearance sections; theme and fonts are per user; the menu bar, start screen and an About dialog carry the Myco Engine and Foxfire Studios art. **Import settings and the Sprite Editor** (ADR 0040; see that row). **Play mode debugging** (details in the 0032 row): a World picker that shows a live World of the running game in the Hierarchy and Inspector, with live edits; **Keep values** written into the scene on Stop as one undo step; Pause and single-frame Step for the session (Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+P, F10 or Ctrl/Cmd+Alt+P) and per World. **Message log** (`messages.ts`, `ui/StatusBar.tsx`): every status message is logged, as are uncaught errors, unhandled rejections and console errors and warnings, with stack traces (`errorStatus(context, err)` keeps a caught error's stack as the status `detail`). Clicking the status bar (or Log) opens the log, newest first. Errors show their details straight away; there are Copy, Clear and Close buttons, and Escape closes it. An unseen-errors count shows while it's closed, and an uncaught error also says "Something went wrong" in the status bar. **Deleting assets** (`assetDelete.ts`): the Assets panel's Delete button, or Delete/Backspace on a focused tile, deletes the selected file (asset or script) from disk after a confirmation. The confirmation lists the files that still refer to it: scenes and prefabs, `project.json`, scripts that mention its path, and the open scene's unsaved edits. "Don't ask again" is a per-user preference, also in Settings and offered back in the status bar. project.json, the open scene, read-only projects and play mode are refused. After deleting, references are removed (`assetReferences.ts`, ADR 0148): instances of a deleted prefab go with their children, and node references to them become None; component fields and overrides naming the file go back to their defaults; `project.json` autoloads of it are dropped. Scene and prefab files on disk are rewritten (layout kept), and instances in the open scene of a prefab that changed are rebuilt. The open scene is cleaned as one undo step (`ReplaceDocument`), kept when it's saved. Scripts aren't edited; the status bar lists what was cleaned and what still refers to it. In the Editor World an instance of a missing prefab loads as an empty node with a reported problem (after undoing such a removal, say); games still throw. Deleting a script reloads scripts and closes its Code tab. **The scene view is a panel**: there's no fixed middle any more. The Select/Move/Rotate/Scale tools, the 3D/2D toggle and Focus are a toolbar at the top of the Scene panel, so they move with it. The Scene panel docks, tabs and splits like the others, and the innermost dock fills the space the others leave, with no resize handle. Dropping at its edges adds a dock just outside it, and when it empties the next dock out fills. Saved layouts from before get the scene view as a new filling dock. The engine's containers (`#viewport`, `#game-view`) are long-lived elements the Scene panel attaches when it mounts (`viewportElements`), so moving or hiding the panel keeps the canvas and WebGL context. **Adding and removing components** (`src/editor/componentEdits.ts`, `ui/AddComponentMenu.tsx`): the Inspector's **Add Component** button opens a searchable list of every registered component (`componentCatalog` in `src/engine/editor/`), grouped Engine, Scripts and Data; Transform, engine internals and `editor.*` types aren't offered. Types a node can't take (a second SpriteRenderer, RigidBody2D, Camera, Text or data component) are greyed out with the reason. A collider added beside a sprite starts fitted to it (box: the sprite's size; circle: half its larger side; `spriteSize`). Each component header except Transform's has a ✕ that removes it. On plain nodes this edits the node's components list; on prefab instances and nodes inside them it's an `addComponent` or `removeComponent` override (`SetOverrides`). Removing a component an override added drops that override, and field overrides for a removed component are dropped or renumbered. Only the first of a type from the prefab can be removed there, since a `removeComponent` override takes the first. With several nodes selected, both act on every node that allows it. Each add or remove is one undo step. The scene camera's F key no longer fires while typing in a text field. Each script or engine component's header checkbox switches it on or off (`enabled: false` in the file; on instances an `enabled` override, ADR 0028, removed again when switched back). Disabled components are dimmed, and a mixed multi-selection shows indeterminate. Transform and data components are always on. A grip on each header drags the component to a new place, with a line showing where; Alt+↑/↓ on the focused grip moves it too. Order sets hook order within a node. On a plain node the Inspector lists components in file order, and data components move among the rest (file layout only). On an instance a move writes an `order` override (ADR 0029; `World._reorderComponents` renumbers creation order to match). Moving back to the prefab's own order removes it, and an instance's data components stay put. Apply now writes added and removed components, `enabled` and `order` overrides into the prefab file too, directly on plain prefab nodes and as overrides on nested instances; name and active overrides of inner nodes are written too since ADR 0122 (the instance root's own stay on the instance). **Multi-selection** (`src/editor/selectionModel.ts`, `viewportSelection.ts`, `ui/RefineList.tsx`): the selection is an ordered list of path keys (`selectedKeys`); the last is the active node, which single-node tools (Create Prefab, Edit Collider, prefab Apply) act on, and `selection` still reads and sets it. A left drag on empty viewport space draws a selection box and selects every node lying entirely inside it (its sprite or text rectangle, or its origin if it draws nothing; a prefab instance counts everything drawn inside it), live as the box changes; Shift adds to the selection, Ctrl/Cmd toggles, and Escape puts the selection back. Shift+click and Ctrl/Cmd+click do the same for single viewport clicks. Colliders count as part of their node: a click anywhere inside a box or circle collider selects its node (the smallest collider first, ahead of the sprites drawn there; clicking again cycles to them), and collider rectangles count toward the box and the Alt+click list (`colliderShapes`, `worldToCanvas`). Alt+click opens a list of every node within 24 px of the pointer, occluded ones included, plus their children (prefab inner nodes too), with list controls: click, Ctrl/Cmd+click toggle, Shift+click range, arrows, Space, Ctrl/Cmd+A. The selection follows it live; Enter or clicking away keeps it, Escape restores it. The hierarchy takes Ctrl/Cmd+click and Shift+click ranges over the visible rows. Every selected node is outlined. With several nodes the Inspector shows the components they all have: differing values show a dash (vectors per axis), and an edit sets every node in one undo step (a vector axis edit keeps each node's other axes). The transform gizmo sits at the selection's centre and moves, turns or scales the topmost selected nodes around it in one undo step (`setGizmo` takes several nodes; the render backend applies the gizmo's change to each node's start pose). Delete removes them all in one step, and F frames their centre. The scene camera now ignores mouse travel from before a drag's first frame, which made Alt+clicks in 2D jump the view. `screenBounds` in `src/engine/editor/` gives each node's on-canvas rectangle. Dragging several hierarchy rows, and the pivot/centre gizmo toggle, were already in. **Added (proposed ADRs):** OS file drop into the Assets panel (0085), scene tab context menu (Close/Others/All/Right/Left/Saved), middle-click and Ctrl/Cmd+W with one prompt for several unsaved tabs, `*.ui.xml`/`*.css`/`*.xml` open in the Code panel (saving updates the running UI's hot reload), multi-node Create Prefab and multi-instance Apply (0087). **Collider gizmos** (`src/plugins/physics/editor/colliderGizmo.ts`, `colliderMath.ts`): every 2D collider in the edited scene is outlined in green (the selected node's brighter), sized as the physics backend sizes it: box size × world scale, circle radius × the larger scale axis, centre offset scaled and rotated with the node. The Inspector's **Edit Collider** toggle on a BoxCollider2D or CircleCollider2D card shows handles in the viewport and hides the transform gizmo: box edge handles resize from the opposite edge (Alt: both edges), circle rim handles set the radius, and the centre handle moves the offset. Each drag is one undo step of `SetField` edits (overrides on prefab instances), and presses on a handle don't select or orbit. Editing ends when the selection changes or any tool is picked. The render backend draws the overlay from a per-frame provider (`addEditorOverlay`, `overlayHandleAt`, `overlayHandlesOnCanvas` in `src/engine/editor/`). **Polygon and edge colliders** (ADR 0039) are outlined too (edges open) and offered in Add Component; with Edit Collider on, each vertex has a handle (drag; Ctrl or "always snap" snaps to the move step), each edge's middle has one (press to add a vertex there and drag it), and Ctrl+click on a vertex removes it (down to 3 points, or 2 for an edge); one undo step each. Their `points` are edited as a list in the Inspector. 3D colliders have gizmos too (`collider3dMath.ts`, 0045), and joints (`jointGizmo.ts`, 0092). **Scene tools** (`src/editor/sceneTools.ts`, `align.ts`, `cameraPreview.ts`, `ui/SceneTools.tsx`, `ui/scene-tools.css`), in the Scene panel's toolbar and remembered per user: **World/Local** (X) aligns the move and rotate handles with the world or the node (the active node for several); **Center/Pivot** (Z) puts a multi-selection's gizmo at the selection's centre or the active node's origin; **Snap** turns on "always snap" (else hold Ctrl), and its ▾ popover sets the move step (m), rotate step (°), scale step, grid size (m) and grid visibility; the grid redraws at the chosen cell size (`setEditorGrid`). **Align ▾** lines up two or more selected nodes by their bounds (sprite boxes, else origins; `worldBounds`): left/centre/right/top/middle/bottom and distribute horizontally/vertically in 2D, min/centre/max and distribute along X, Y and Z in 3D (distribute needs three; the outermost stay and the gaps become equal). Each action is one undo step of position edits (overrides on prefab instances), on the topmost selected nodes. **Camera preview**: with a Camera node selected in edit mode, an inset in the scene view's corner shows what it sees at the game's aspect ratio, rendered by the backend into that part of the canvas through the camera without the grid, gizmos and overlays (`setCameraPreview`); the **Preview** toggle turns it off. The preview applies the camera's effects and draws the game's screen UI, but doesn't follow a camera's viewport rect; align uses sprite bounds only (no text or collider bounds). **History panel and Settings** (`src/editor/ui/HistoryPanel.tsx`, `SettingsDialog.tsx`, ADR 0027): a dockable History panel (a tab beside the Inspector by default) draws the edit history tree like the undotree plugin, newest at the top with a column per branch, marking the current and saved states. Clicking a state jumps there, and Ctrl/Cmd+Alt+Z and Ctrl/Cmd+Alt+Shift+Z step back and forward in creation order. Saved layouts from before the panel existed get it as a tab beside the Inspector instead of being reset. A Settings dialog (Edit > Settings…) holds the per-user history location (project folder by default, or per user) and the ask-before-clearing-references option. `src/editor/main.tsx` renders the Preact layout (0025) and boots an Editor World (`Engine.create({ kind: "editor" })`). The render backend fills the viewport panel, draws a grid (XZ in 3D, XY in 2D) and axes, and doesn't render cameras inside the edited scene. `OrbitCamera` is a GameBehavior on a yaw → pitch-arm → camera rig under the World root. In 3D, right-drag or Alt + left-drag orbits; in both views, middle-drag or Shift + right-drag pans (right-drag also pans in 2D), the wheel zooms (`Input.mouseScrollDelta`) and F focuses. The 2D view is orthographic, looking down −Z. **Reordering and deleting** (`src/editor/hierarchyEdit.ts`, `nodeDrag.ts`, `deleteSelection.ts`): drag hierarchy rows to reorder, reparent or unparent nodes, using the same indicators as asset drops. A reparented node keeps its place in the world: its local Transform is recalculated, written as overrides on prefab instances. Delete/Backspace or the inspector's Delete button removes the selected node and its children. When other nodes reference it, an in-editor dialog lists those references and offers "Don't ask again" (a per-user preference); confirming sets them to None in the same undo step. After a delete that cleared references without asking, the status bar offers to turn the prompt back on. Nodes inside prefab instances can't be moved, take children or be deleted here; the instance itself can. Each move or delete is one undo step. **Asset drag and drop** (`src/editor/assetDrag.ts`, pointer events rather than HTML drag and drop, which Tauri's webview takes over on Windows): drag an asset tile into the viewport to add it at the top level under the cursor, on the z = 0 plane (`pointOnSpritePlane`), falling back to the camera's focus point. Drag it into the hierarchy to add it under a node (the middle of its row: the row highlights) or beside it (near the row's top or bottom edge: a line shows where); empty space or the scene header means the top level. Images become sprites, audio becomes an AudioSource, and prefabs and scenes become instances, with scenes as sub-scenes (ADR 0016). Other files, a scene or prefab that would end up inside itself, rows inside prefab instances, other panels, and play mode are invalid: the ghost label turns red and says why, and releasing changes nothing. Each drop is one undoable step and selects the new node. **Dockable panels** (`src/editor/layout/`: a pure model in `model.ts` plus the `DockLayout` renderer): docks are listed outermost first, and the Scene panel fills what the innermost leaves (see above). Each dock is on the left, right or bottom and splits evenly into tab groups. Dragging a panel by its header previews the drop and then lands it according to the pointer: on another panel's header it joins as a tab; at the window's outer edge it becomes a new outermost dock that claims the full height or width; at the viewport's edge it becomes a new innermost dock; inside a dock it splits the dock evenly at that position. Anywhere else, or pressing Escape, leaves the layout unchanged. Handles resize docks (clamped so the viewport keeps 200×140) and the splits between groups. The layout is saved in localStorage and validated on load (it falls back to the default), and Reset Layout restores the default. The viewport element stays mounted while panels move, since the engine renders into it. **Projects and saving** (`src/editor/projects.ts`, `session.ts`): the editor starts on a start screen (ADR 0031) with Open Project… (desktop) and the samples served beside it; a sample opens read-only (works in a plain browser), and the desktop editor can copy one into a folder to edit it. In the desktop editor, **Open Project…** picks a folder containing `project.json` through the platform FileSystem: every JSON file is read and binary assets become object URLs up front, since scene loading looks assets up synchronously. Each project gets a fresh Editor World with its own settings (autoloads dropped). The Assets panel lists scenes and prefabs to open, and a status bar reports errors such as unknown script types. **Save** (Ctrl/Cmd+S) writes the open scene through `formatSceneJSON` (`document/format.ts`): unchanged nodes keep their original text and blank-line grouping, and edited ones are formatted Prettier-style at 112 columns, so a one-field edit changes one line. The demo files round-trip byte for byte. Opening another scene or project, or closing the window, asks before discarding unsaved changes (Tauri dialog plugin, `beforeunload` in browsers; the window needs `core:window:allow-destroy`). A folder project's own scripts load from its `scripts/` folder (`scriptLoader.ts`, ADR 0030), and the opened folder stays in the fs scope across sessions (persisted scope). The editor opens a scene file into a `SceneDocument`/`WorldSync` and frames the scene's own camera, switching to 2D for orthographic scenes. The **Hierarchy** shows the document's node tree (collapsible, with prefab badges, inactive nodes dimmed), and clicking selects. The **Inspector** shows the selected node's components with declared fields and live values, and edits them: each field type gets its own input (numbers with arithmetic and label scrubbing, booleans, strings, asset paths, colours, vectors per axis, node references, nested settings objects), plus the node name and active flag. Every edit is a `SetField`/`RenameNode`/`SetActive` command (an override on prefab instances; `src/editor/fieldValues.ts` converts to scene-file JSON). Edit > Undo/Redo (saying what they undo), also on Ctrl/Cmd+Z and Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z or Ctrl+Y, and the hierarchy marks unsaved changes with •. **Viewport selection** (`src/editor/viewportSelection.ts`): a plain left click (no Alt, under 4 px of movement) picks the topmost sprite or text under the pointer (`pick` in `src/engine/editor/`, a ray test against each sprite's world quad, ordered by draw order then distance) and selects its document node, so a prefab's inner nodes select the instance. Clicking the same spot again cycles through overlapping hits, and clicking empty space clears. The selection's sprites, including descendants, get a blue outline drawn on top every frame. F and the Focus button frame the selection, or the origin when nothing is selected. **Prefab tools** (`src/editor/prefabs.ts`): prefab instances expand in the hierarchy to the nodes inside them, read from the prefab files (nested prefabs too), and those nodes are selectable (the selection is a path key such as `pipe/top`). Edits and gizmo drags on an instance or a node inside one become overrides. Overridden fields are marked with a revert button, and instance badges get a dot. Instances have **Apply**, which writes field and component overrides into the prefab file and refreshes every instance (the scene change undoes; the file keeps it), and **Revert All**. Plain nodes have **Create Prefab**, which writes `prefabs/<name>.prefab.json` and turns the node into an instance with the same id; it refuses if outside nodes reference nodes inside the new prefab. Apply and Create Prefab need a project folder. Nodes inside an instance can be renamed and deactivated (overrides), and Open Prefab edits the prefab in its own tab (ADR 0122, see that row). **Play mode** (`src/editor/playMode.ts`): **▶ Play** / **■ Stop** (Ctrl/Cmd+P) snapshots the open document, unsaved edits included, and serves it under the scene's path through a temporary asset source. It pauses the Editor World and boots a Runtime World with the project's real settings (autoloads too) in a game view over the viewport. Edits made while playing go to the document only; even a scene reload in the game (Flappy's restart) uses the snapshot. Stop disposes the Runtime World (`Engine.dispose` now destroys every node, so scripts get onDestroy, audio stops and the Rapier world is freed), removes the snapshot, gives input back to the editor canvas and resumes its loop. If the scene fails to start, the editor resumes and the status bar shows the error. **Asset browser** (`ui/Assets.tsx`, `assetBrowser.ts`, `assetSelection.ts`, `assetOps.ts`): a file browser. A folder tree sits beside the folder's subfolders and files, shown as a grid of tiles (image thumbnails, a tile-size slider) or a list with kind and size; the view and size are per-user preferences. A path bar with Back, Forward and Up (Alt+←/→/↑) moves between folders, and search finds files by name across folders. Click, Ctrl/Cmd+click, Shift+click, a box dragged on empty space (Shift adds, Ctrl/Cmd toggles, Escape restores) and the arrow keys (Shift extends, Ctrl/Cmd+A selects all) select files and folders; Enter or double-click opens. F2 renames in place, dragging the selection onto a folder (in the tree, the grid or the path bar) moves it, Ctrl+D duplicates, Ctrl+C/X/V copy, cut and paste, and Delete deletes, all on several entries at once; a right-click menu offers the same plus New Folder, New Script and Show in File Manager. Renames and moves update references in scenes, prefabs and project.json like deleting does (the open scene as one undo step); scripts that mention an old path are listed, not edited. Scripts stay under `scripts/`, and the open scene (or a folder holding it) and files with unsaved Code panel edits can't be moved. `FileSystem` gained `rename`, recursive `remove` and an optional `reveal` (opener plugin, `opener:allow-reveal-item-in-dir`); `EditorProject` gained `folders`, `sizeOf`, `createFolder`, `move`, `copyFile` and `deleteFolder`. Dragging several assets into the viewport or hierarchy adds them all as one undo step. Earlier: click selects a tile, double-click opens a scene or prefab, and a tile's + adds a prefab instance or a sprite node for an image (at the scene camera's focus point, under the selected node or beside a selected prefab instance) as one undoable `AddNode` with a unique id derived from the file name. **Camera gizmos**: cameras in the edited scene (which don't draw the editor's view) show a camera icon, constant size on screen and clickable to select, plus a wireframe of what they see at the game's aspect ratio, drawn to at most 30 m deep (`render/cameraGizmo.ts`); the selected camera's wireframe uses the selection colour. **Gizmos**: the toolbar's Select/Move/Rotate/Scale tools (Q/W/E/R) show Babylon's position, rotation and scale gizmos on the selected node (`render/babylon/editorGizmo.ts`, 0075; `setGizmo`/`gizmoActive` in `src/engine/editor/`), which game bundles never load. The 2D view limits move and scale to X/Y and rotation to Z. Holding Ctrl/Cmd snaps to 0.25 m, 15° or 0.1 (steps set in the Scene toolbar's Snap settings, see Scene tools). Each drag is one undo step of Transform `SetField` edits (overrides on prefab instances; `gizmoMath.ts` converts the gizmo's world pose to local values), and presses on a handle don't change the selection. **Scene and hierarchy editing (E1)**: the Hierarchy header has a **+** Create menu (Empty, Sprite, Camera, Text at the top level at the scene view's focus point, and Child variants under the active node), and rows and empty space have a right-click menu with the same plus Create beside the row, Cut, Copy, Paste, Paste as Child, Duplicate, Delete, Hide and Lock (`nodeCreate.ts`, `ui/HierarchyMenu.tsx`). New nodes get a unique id and a name unique among their siblings ("Camera (1)"), are selected, and are one undo step each. **Copy, cut, paste, duplicate** (`nodeClipboard.ts`, `hierarchyActions.ts`): Ctrl/Cmd+C/X/V/D (Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+V pastes as a child) act on the selection's topmost subtrees in hierarchy order. Pasting gives every node a new id, re-points `$node` references between the copies, keeps references to other nodes only when pasting into the scene they came from (else None), and goes after the active node (or into it). Prefab instances copy with their overrides. Each is one undo step; cut clears references to the cut nodes like Delete. The clipboard is in memory, so it pastes into any open scene; copies also go on the system clipboard as `myco.nodes@1` JSON. Nodes inside prefab instances can't be copied. **Hierarchy search** (`hierarchyFilter.ts`): words match names, `t:Type` matches component types by short name (prefab instances and their inner nodes by their prefab files' components); matches show with their ancestors (dimmed), and Escape clears. **Dragging several rows**: dragging a selected row drags the selection's topmost nodes, in order, with the usual drop rules, as one undo step (`moveNodes`). **Hide and lock** (`nodeVisibility.ts`): eye and lock toggles on each row, inherited by children. Hidden nodes are switched off in the Editor World only (never in the document, play mode or the file); locked nodes can't be picked, boxed or Alt+clicked in the viewport but stay selectable in the hierarchy. Kept per user in browser storage keyed by project and scene. **New Scene** (`newScene.ts`): the Assets panel's menu and File > New Scene write `New Scene.scene.json` (a `myco.scene@1` file with an orthographic camera) into the folder and open it. **Scene tabs** (`ui/SceneTabs.tsx`, `openScenes` in `state.ts`; `sceneTabs.ts` `createSceneTabs` owns open, switch, close and close-all, tab views, history attach and restore, and scene-open migrations, and is the only writer of `openScene`/`openScenes`, with the Editor World, history platform and confirm dialog as seams handed in by `session.ts`; tested in `sceneTabs.test.ts`): opening a scene or prefab opens a tab at the top of the Scene panel (or switches to it). Each tab keeps its own document, undo history (persisted per scene, ADR 0027), selection, scene camera view and dirty marker; inactive tabs have no nodes in the Editor World and are rebuilt when activated. `openScene` stays the active tab, which Save, Play and every panel use. Closing a dirty tab asks first (the edits stay in its history and are offered back on reopening); closing the project or opening another asks once for all dirty tabs.  |
| [0026](../decisions/0026-editor-scene-document-and-commands.md) Scene document + commands | Done | The editor UI edits through it (inspector fields, rename, active, undo/redo). `src/editor/document/`: `SceneDocument` holds the scene JSON as authored (round-trips exactly, key order included) and indexes nodes. The commands `SetField` (plain data, or a prefab override with an optional inner `target`), `RenameNode`, `SetActive`, `AddNode` (a subtree, at a sibling index), `RemoveNode`, `ReparentNode`, `AddComponent` and `RemoveComponent` each return a `Change` list, and undo (the stack's patches, 0321) restores the document byte for byte; later records added more (`SetComponentEnabled`, `MoveComponent`, `SetScenePreload`, `SetComponents`, `ReplaceDocument`, `WriteFiles`, and the prefab and node-tag commands). `CommandStack` has undo/redo, nested `beginGroup`/`endGroup`, a dirty flag against `markSaved()`, and `subscribe`. `WorldSync` instantiates the document under an edit-mode scene node, then applies changes incrementally: field writes in place, a subtree rebuild for structural changes, and `$node` references re-pointed. It maps World nodes back to document nodes (`nodeIdOf`, `pathOf`, `nodeFor`). Editor-only engine access lives in `src/engine/editor/` (`listNodes`, `listComponents`, `writeField`, `instantiateDocument`, `moveNode`, `destroyNow`, `pick`). It isn't exported from the engine or included in its `.d.ts`. Edit mode (`World.setEditMode`) stops GameBehavior hooks, built-in activation side effects such as audio, and user-system queries inside the edited subtree. Editor tools outside it keep running, and no physics world is created. Several documents are open at once as scene tabs (see 0022). AddComponent/RemoveComponent on instances are prefab overrides (`componentEdits.ts`), and saving to disk goes through the `FileSystem` adapter (Tauri, or the File System Access API in browsers, ADR 0038). Child-node overrides: see 0086. |
| [0025](../decisions/0025-editor-ui-dom-with-preact.md) Editor UI (DOM, Preact) | Done | Preact panels with `@preact/signals` state; docking as in 0025's resolved open question. **Theming** (its other open question; `src/editor/theme.ts`): a theme is a preset's five key colours (background, panels, text, accent, secondary accent) plus the user's overrides of them, kept per preset. Every other token is derived from those five: surfaces, hover and active, borders, muted and faint text, links, selection, focus ring and glow, buttons, inputs, danger/warning/success with strong and soft variants, badges, scrollbars, shadow, backdrop and viewport colours. Text tokens are held to WCAG contrast against the panels (`ensureContrast`, `colorMath.ts`), so custom colours stay readable. Presets: **Myco Dark** (the default, from the brand art: near-black with a teal cast, glowing cyan `#2ad4d4`, moss green `#8bd12a`, warm white `#f4efe6`), **Myco Light** and **High Contrast**. `installTheme` (called in `main.tsx`) writes the tokens to `:root` as CSS custom properties (`--bg`, `--panel`, `--surface`, `--hover`, `--border`, `--text`, `--muted`, `--accent`, `--accent2`, `--selection`, `--danger` and so on) and sets `color-scheme`; `editor.css` keeps Myco Dark as the first-paint fallback. The editor stylesheets use only tokens, with no hard-coded colours, and font sizes scale with `--ui-scale`. The Code panel's Monaco theme "myco" is generated from the tokens (`monacoColors`) and redefined when they change. The viewport's background, grid, selection outline, camera gizmos and collider handles follow the theme through `setEditorColors` in `src/engine/editor/` (`RenderSurface.setEditorColors`, editor-only, not in the public API; the colours come from `viewportColors` in `theme.ts` via `src/editor/viewportTheme.ts`). **Settings > Appearance** (`ui/SettingsDialog.tsx`, now tabbed General / Appearance) has a preset picker, colour pickers for the five key colours with per-colour and all-colour reset, and a live preview. It also sets the UI font family (presets, or Custom… for free text) and size (10–20 px), and the Code panel's font family and size (9–28 px). Changes apply live and persist through `persisted()` (`myco.editor.theme`, `.themeOverrides`, `.uiFontFamily`, `.uiFontSize`, `.codeFontFamily`, `.codeFontSize`). **Branding** (`brand.ts`, `ui/StartBrand.tsx`, `ui/AboutDialog.tsx`, `ui/brand/`): the menu bar's Myco icon opens About (also Help > About Myco Engine). The start screen's header shows the Myco logo, and its footer the Foxfire Studios credit, the version and an About link. About shows the logo, the version (from `tauri.conf.json`) and credits, and Settings links to it too. The favicon and the Tauri app icons come from `branding/myco-engine-icon.webp`, resized in Chromium; Tauri's set was generated by `tauri icon` from a 1024 px PNG. **ADR 0116**: theme import/export (`myco.theme@1`, `themeFile.ts`), per-project `.myco/theme.json` (`projectTheme.ts`; `.myco/` is gitignored, so projects un-ignore it to share), a light logo variant chosen by panel luminance, bundled Inter and JetBrains Mono (`ui/fonts.ts`). |
| [0027](../decisions/0027-persistent-undo-tree.md) Persistent undo tree | Done | `CommandStack` is a tree of states with node-level document patches (`document/patch.ts`). Editing after undo branches; redo follows the last-visited child; `jumpTo`, plus `back`/`forward` in creation order (Ctrl/Cmd+Alt+Z, +Shift); 1000-state limit. History is saved per scene after every change (`historyPersistence.ts`) into two alternating checksummed slots (`historyStore.ts`). Project histories go in `.myco/history/` by default; per-user storage (app data or IndexedDB) is a Settings choice and is always used for read-only projects. Opening a scene matches history to the file by content hash and offers to restore unsaved changes from the last session; history that doesn't match is set aside. The History panel (tab beside the Inspector) draws the tree and jumps on click. Prune history older than N days (Settings > General, `HistoryTree` `toFile`/`load` `pruneDays`, ADR 0267; never cuts the current or saved path; ADR 0116). Not yet: sharing or merging history between machines. Asset documents (0133) keep the same tree as text patches through `TextHistory` and the same `HistoryStore`, in `.myco/asset-history/` (ADR 0141). |
| [0028](../decisions/0028-component-enabled-overrides.md) Component enabled overrides | Done | `OverrideEntry.enabled` in `SceneLoader.ts`; written by `setComponentEnabled` in `src/editor/componentEdits.ts` and applied to prefab files by Apply (`src/editor/prefabs.ts`). |
| [0029](../decisions/0029-component-order-overrides.md) Component order overrides | Done | `OverrideEntry.order` in `SceneLoader.ts` and `World._reorderComponents`; written by `moveComponent` in `src/editor/componentEdits.ts` (removed when back in the prefab's order), applied to prefab files by Apply. |
| [0030](../decisions/0030-in-editor-scripting.md) In-editor scripting | Partial | **Runtime loading** (`src/editor/scriptLoader.ts`): opening a project folder reads the `.ts` files under `scripts/` (`EditorProject.scripts`), and the session loads them before the first scene opens. Each is compiled with the TypeScript compiler (`transpileModule`, the build's settings, inline source maps; loaded lazily in its own chunk) and loaded as a `data:` URL module in dependency order. Relative imports are rewritten to the other scripts' URLs and `"myco"` to a shim re-exporting the editor's engine (`globalThis.__myco`). Syntax errors, import cycles, missing scripts, unsupported package imports and scripts that throw are reported per file, and anything importing them is skipped; the rest load. They show in the status bar and stay as a "⚠ N scripts didn't load" marker with the details. Loading replaces existing registrations with the same id or name, including the editor build's (`withScriptRegistrations` in `src/engine/editor/`; the registry, `defineComponent` and `defineSystem` honour an internal replacing switch). **Reloading:** File > **Reload Scripts** (folder projects) and `saveScript` (for the Code panel) re-read and reload every script (`replaceScripts`). The open scene is then rebuilt from its document, so values set in the Inspector stay and new fields get their defaults; this rebuilds the whole scene rather than only the nodes using changed types, as the ADR allows. Types the previous load registered that this one doesn't (a deleted or renamed script, one that broke) are unregistered, putting back any editor-build type they had replaced (the registry, `defineComponent` and `defineSystem` record script registrations and can undo them). The same happens when another project opens. During play mode a reload goes into the running game, keeping its state (ADR 0125; it waits for Stop only while the game is starting). **Missing scripts:** in the Editor World the scene loader reports unknown component types, and overrides aimed at missing components or nodes, through `InstantiateHooks.onProblem` and skips them; games still throw. `WorldSync.missingComponents` lists them per node, and the Inspector shows each as a "missing script" card with its data kept in the document. The card's ✕ removes one written in this scene (a plain node's entry, or an instance's `addComponent` override and its field overrides); one from a prefab file is removed in the prefab. Playing a scene with a missing script fails to start, with the error in the status bar. **Code panel** (`ui/CodePanel.tsx`, `code/`): a dockable panel, a tab beside Assets by default (older saved layouts get it there too), where scripts open in tabs with Monaco (`monaco-editor`, loaded lazily with its workers on first use). Monaco's TypeScript service knows the engine's public declarations as the `"myco"` package. They're emitted in memory from `tsconfig.engine.json` by the `engineTypes()` Vite plugin (`virtual:myco-types`). Every project script is a model, so autocomplete, errors and go-to-definition work across scripts. Scripts show in the Assets panel (kind "script", double-click opens). Script components' Inspector cards get a { } button that opens their script (`scriptFor` finds it by the id it registers). A dot marks unsaved edits, Ctrl/Cmd+S saves and reloads scripts (the editor's own shortcuts stand aside inside the code editor, except Play), closing a tab with unsaved edits asks first, and read-only projects open scripts read-only. Models without unsaved edits follow reloads. Monaco loads in the background once the editor is idle after a project opens (`preloadCodeEditor`), and with scripts in the project the TypeScript worker starts too, so the first file opens without the wait. An "Opening <file>…" overlay shows until the file has drawn. Scenes and prefabs no longer become Monaco models or TypeScript libraries. Fixed along the way: the engine's `Input` stopped Space (and the arrows) from typing into any text field, and the scene camera's F key fired while typing in Monaco (which types through the EditContext API); script modules load from `blob:` URLs in browsers. `window.openTestProject` opens an in-memory project for end-to-end tests. **JSON, JavaScript and other text files**: `.json` files (except scenes and prefabs, which open in the scene view), JavaScript (`.js`, `.mjs`, `.cjs`) and TypeScript modules (`.mts`, `.cts`), such as the Node tools that generate a sample's assets (`samples/pirates/tools/*.mjs`), and Markdown and plain text (`.md`, `.txt`) are listed in the Assets panel (kinds "javascript", "typescript" and "text") and open in the Code panel with Monaco's JSON, JavaScript, TypeScript, Markdown and plain-text support (JSON gets its own language worker); UI documents, stylesheets and XML open there too (ADR 0077). `projectPaths.ts` (`isTextAsset`, `codeLanguageOf`) decides which files these are and their language. Saving JSON requires it to parse; the rest save as they are. They can be renamed, moved, copied and deleted like other assets; there is no command to create one (New Script makes only scripts). Everything but JSON is edit-only, never compiled or loaded as a script: only `.ts` files under `scripts/` are scripts. Game builds copy them as plain files, as they copy every project file but TypeScript (`isExportedFile`), so a project's `tools/*.mjs` ships unused in its build (inert: the player never loads it); `.mts`/`.cts` are left out. Scripts can import the project's JSON files (`import levels from "../data/levels.json"`): the loader serves each as a module whose default export is its contents, invalid JSON is reported against the importing script, and saving JSON a script mentions reloads scripts. In the Code panel the TypeScript service gets each JSON file as a small module, since Monaco's worker reads `.json` as TypeScript, so imports are typed from the file. The generated project tsconfig sets `resolveJsonModule`, and the repo's tsconfig maps `"myco"` to the engine so project scripts inside the repo type-check. **New Script** (`newScript.ts`, `scriptTemplates.ts`, `ui/NewScriptDialog.tsx`): a **New Script** button in the Assets panel (folder projects) and **New script…** at the bottom of the Add Component list ask for a kind and a name. The kinds are a script (GameBehavior), a data component or a system; Add Component offers only the first two. Typed names become PascalCase, and the dialog shows the file and id (`game.<Name>`) as you type. It refuses invalid identifiers, existing files and ids another script registers. Creating writes `<folder>/<Name>.ts` from a template (under the Assets panel's folder when that's inside `scripts/`), reloads scripts, opens it in the Code panel and, from Add Component, adds the new component to the selected nodes as one undo step. **Editing outside the editor** (`scriptingSetup.ts`): opening a writable project folder creates `scripts/`, writes a `tsconfig.json` if there is none, and writes the engine's declarations under `.myco/types/myco/` (a folder of `.d.ts` files plus a `package.json`, rather than the single file the ADR sketched). The declarations are rewritten only when their hash changes, and the tsconfig maps `"myco"` to them. A unit test type-checks the script templates against this setup with the TypeScript compiler. The desktop editor watches `scripts/` (Tauri fs plugin's `watch` feature, `fs:allow-watch`) and reloads when a script changes on disk, skipping changes that match what's loaded, such as its own saves. The Code panel's **Open Externally** opens the script with the system's app for `.ts` files (opener plugin, `opener:allow-open-path`; granted for any path and the windows have no CSP, a known limitation, see [security](security.md)). `FileSystem` gained optional `watch` and `openExternally`. Checked in the Tauri editor with a real project folder: scripts load, the files are written, and an outside edit reloads. **Game builds** of project scripts: built by ADR 0041 (`compileForBuild` in `src/export/webBuild.ts`). **The ADR's open questions** (npm package imports, type-checking before Play, keeping a running game's state across a reload) are built by ADR 0125; see that row. Samples (ADR 0031) load their scripts the same way. |
| [0031](../decisions/0031-samples-ship-beside-the-engine.md) Samples | Done | Flappy is a sample project in `samples/flappy/` (its behaviors, data components and systems are `scripts/` importing `"myco"`), and nothing in `src/` imports it. The `samples()` Vite plugin (`vite.shared.ts`) serves `samples/` at `/samples/` in dev, untouched, with a manifest at `/samples/index.json` (name and description from an optional `sample.json`, files with sizes), and copies them into `dist/editor/samples/` in the editor build. The editor opens on a start screen (`ui/StartScreen.tsx`): the projects section (`ui/StartProjects.tsx`: New Project…, Open Project… and recent projects, ADR 0038) and the samples, each with Open (read-only, `samples.ts` fetches its files into a `readOnlyProject`; its scripts load through the script loader) and, where folders open (desktop, Chromium browsers), Make a Copy… (writes it into a picked folder and opens that). `?sample=<id>` opens one directly, and File > Close Project goes back to the start screen. The standalone game build (`index.html` game, `src/index.ts`, `build:game`) and the game's Tauri config are gone; `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` is now the editor's, so `npm run tauri:dev` / `tauri:build` are the editor. Editor tests register the sample's types through `src/editor/sampleTypes.ts`. The Arena sample (`samples/arena/`, T16) shows multiplayer. King of Pirates (`samples/pirates/`) is the larger 3D multiplayer sample on ADRs 0067–0074: a menu (a UI document, ADR 0077) and rooms on the lobby, server-authoritative ships with prediction, bots on baked navmeshes, Gerstner water, fog, a `UICanvas` HUD (ADR 0076), spatial audio, procedural assets (`tools/generate.mjs`), a minimap and arena-edge warnings, particle fog clouds; tests `src/engine/tests/piratesSample.test.ts`, `piratesNavigation.test.ts`, `piratesAwareness.test.ts`. Game builds of projects: see 0041. |
| [0032](../decisions/0032-worlds-systems-and-bootstraps.md) Worlds, systems, bootstraps | Partial | Wave 1 of [the plan](worlds-and-netcode-plan.md). **Several Worlds per Engine** (`core/Engine.ts`, `core/WorldHandle.ts`): `Engine.create` runs the bootstrap (`EngineOptions.bootstrap`, else `project.json`'s); `engine.world` is the first World it made. One loop steps every unpaused World in creation order, each with its own accumulator, tick rate, time scale and `maxSubSteps` cap, with `World.active` set to it and its end-of-frame work after its own frame. `Input.beginFrame()` runs once per Engine frame. `engine.createWorld(config | templateName)`, `findWorld`, `worlds()`, and static `Engine.createWorld`/`findWorld` (the current World's Engine) return handles: load scenes, add/remove systems, pause/resume, destroy (the next World becomes the default; destroying the last throws). Handles keep the World in a `#private` field. Autoloads go into the default World. One view per Engine (ADR 0034): the first World that wants it (not `server`-tagged ones unless `renders: true`) gets the canvas and, unless its config sets `input`, local input; when that World is destroyed the view passes to the next one that wants it. `kind: "editor"` or `"server"` without a bootstrap makes one Editor or Server World. **System catalog** (`core/systems.ts`): `defineSystem` only registers. Engine built-ins (script hooks, the four physics steps) are catalog entries with `worlds: ["*"]` (a wildcard in `worldsMatch`). Each World runs the catalog entries its tags match, plus ones added and minus ones removed by name, recomputed when the catalog changes, so scripts loaded later join matching Worlds. The Editor World (tag `editor`) no longer runs game systems; they never saw the edited scene anyway. **Isolation** (T3): `Input` returns nothing in a World with `input: false`. Reparenting, `create`/`instantiate` under another World's parent, `{ $node }` resolution, behavior `init` values and buffer records holding another World's nodes all throw. A `RenderSurface` belongs to one World. Plain field assignment can't be guarded. **Bootstraps** (`core/bootstrap.ts`, `settings.ts`): declarative configs are validated against the catalog when they run (one `BootstrapError` listing every problem, before any World is made; unknown keys are errors), code bootstraps load through `ResolveBootstrapOptions.loadScript`, templates by name (`templateConfig`), `bootstrapLinks` for T10, `validateBootstrap` for the Worlds panel. **Amended by [0034](../decisions/0034-current-world-view-and-async.md):** between frames the default World is current (no throwing); `GameObject.create`/`instantiate` with a parent use the parent's World, and `this.instantiate`/`this.time` keep async behavior code in its own World; physics events go to their own World's buffers. `createWorld` validates a config like a declarative bootstrap before changing anything (`timeScale: 0` allowed), and `Engine.create` disposes what it made if it fails. Membership: a World records only its own include/exclude; tags keep deciding the rest as the catalog changes. **Editor (wave 2).** Play mode (`playMode.ts`, `gameView.ts`) builds an Engine from the project's bootstrap (`EngineOptions.loadScript` runs a code bootstrap from the loaded project scripts); start-up Worlds whose config names no `scenes` play the open scene's snapshot (`scenes: []` starts empty, as in a build). The game view tiles every rendering World (the main view plus one surface per other World, `attachWorldView` in `src/engine/editor/`), following Worlds created and destroyed at runtime; clicking a tile gives that World input (`focusWorldInput`), keeping held keys (`Input.retarget`); a strip lists every World, headless ones too, with Pause/Resume. The **Worlds panel** (`worldsConfig.ts`, `ui/WorldsPanel.tsx`, a tab beside History) edits `project.json`'s `bootstrap` as a graph: Worlds, templates and links; tags, tick rate, time scale, renders, input, start-up scenes; a per-World system checklist with reasons, written as minimal include/exclude. Valid edits save at once (other keys and the file's formatting kept); invalid ones stay unsaved with the problems shown inline. It has its own undo/redo, and read-only projects show it disabled. `EditorProject.settings` follows project.json writes. **Known gaps:** a bare `GameObject.instantiate(path)` or `Time.*` after an `await` in a non-default World still acts on the default World (ADR 0034). **Inspecting the running game** (`runtimeInspect.ts`, `ui/RuntimeHierarchy.tsx`, `ui/RuntimeInspector.tsx`, `playDebug.ts`, `ui/PlayControls.tsx`): while playing, a **Show** picker at the top of the Hierarchy switches the Hierarchy and Inspector between the edited scene and any live World of the play Engine (Worlds created at runtime included; a destroyed one says so). The live tree (refreshed every 300 ms) lists the World's nodes with badges for loaded scenes, prefabs instantiated at runtime, ghosts (mode, mine) and connections; selecting a node outlines it in that World's view (`setSelectionOutline`) and shows live values in the Inspector (every 250 ms; a field being typed in keeps its draft), editable: edits go to the live World only (`writeField`), with active and enabled toggles. **Keep values** on a component header (and **Keep all changes to this node**, every component with the Transform) records the values as scene JSON; on Stop they're written into the document as one undo step ("Keep play-mode changes"), only fields that differ from the scene, as plain values on scene nodes and as overrides on prefab instances and nodes inside them (`docPathFor` maps a live node back by its scene ids). Nodes made or instantiated at runtime, the scene node itself and nodes of other scenes can't keep values, and the Inspector says why; entries whose node or component was deleted meanwhile are skipped and reported. **Pause and Step** for the whole session (beside Play/Stop in the middle of the menu bar; Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+P pauses or resumes, F10 or Ctrl/Cmd+Alt+P steps): Pause stops the play Engine's loop; Step runs one 60 Hz Engine frame (a World's fixed step when due; Worlds paused on their own sit out), and the game view strip's **Step** advances one World (`Engine._stepWorld`, editor-only accessor `stepEngineWorld`), paused or not; while paused, live edits and selections redraw without stepping (`redrawEngine`). **Structural live edits** ([0124](../decisions/0124-structural-edits-to-a-live-world.md), `liveEdits.ts`): the live Hierarchy creates empty nodes (its **+** and right-click menu: at the top level under the active scene, beside a row, or as a child), renames them in place (F2, double-click), deletes them (Del, menu) and reparents them by dragging onto a row or onto the World header (top level), keeping the world transform; the World root and loaded scenes can't move, and cycles are refused. The live Inspector adds components (the edit-mode Add Component menu, `onAdd`, colliders fitted as in edit mode), removes them (not the Transform), and sets node references from a dropdown of the same World's nodes. Everything goes through the public GameObject API, applies at once (deferred destroys run right away, `applyDestroys`, so it shows while paused), isn't undoable and isn't kept on Stop. Tests: `liveEdits.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/playdebug.spec.ts`. Gaps: outlines need a World that draws (not headless Worlds). The Worlds panel edits link `options` (see 0062). Outside the editor, only one World is visible at a time. Bootstrap links connect at `Engine.create` (see 0033), or when a Spawnable World they name is created ([0062](../decisions/0062-links-to-spawnable-worlds.md)). User guide: [Worlds](../wiki/guide/worlds.md). |
| [0033](../decisions/0033-server-authoritative-netcode.md) Netcode | Partial (through prediction, the dedicated server, network tools and the multiplayer sample) | Wave 0. `net/Transport.ts` (polled transports, channels, link conditions, a working transport registry), `net/codec.ts` (bit writer/reader and record codec interfaces), `net/messages.ts` (`defineRpc`, `defineInput`, connection fields), `net/replication.ts` (`replicate()` for data components, replication settings), `net/time.ts` (`NetworkTime`, on `TimeState.network`). `@field(type, { replicate, predicted, quantize })` and `@behavior(id, { worlds })` record their options. Tests: `src/engine/tests/net.test.ts`. **Wave 2:** transports a World creates with `createWorldTransport` are closed when it's destroyed, so connections and (page-wide) loopback addresses don't outlive a stopped game; the **loopback transport** (`net/transport/loopback.ts`, registered as `loopback`): in-process, poll-based with no timers, simulated latency/jitter/loss per sending end (`setConditions` on the transport or a connection), reliable messages never lost or reordered (a loss costs a round trip), unreliable ones dropped on loss and latest-wins, an injectable clock and random source for deterministic tests. The **codec** (`net/codec.ts`, `net/bits.ts`): little-endian bit packing, LEB128 varints to 2^53-1, zigzag, exact float32, UTF-8 strings, errors on malformed input; schema records with per-field quantization and baseline deltas (a changed bit per field, then only changed fields; i32 and quantized deltas), e.g. 15 bytes full, 1 byte unchanged, 5 bytes with one float changed. The **WebSocket transport** (T9, ADR 0035): a browser-safe client (`net/transport/websocket.ts`, global `WebSocket`, registered as `websocket`) and a Node server (`src/server/websocketServer.ts`, the `ws` package; importing it re-registers `websocket` with a transport that can `listen`, with `address.url` after binding, port 0 supported). Frames are binary with a 1-byte header (channel bit, reserved flags); unreliable messages ride the same TCP stream unless the connection has a WebRTC data channel (ADR 0333, its own row). Limits (`WebSocketLimits`, from the transport options): messages over 1 MiB are refused (the server sets `ws`'s `maxPayload`), a connection that sends more than 4 MiB between polls is closed as flooding, unreliable sends are dropped while more than 64 KiB is waiting in the socket's send buffer (`droppedUnreliable` counts them), and a connection with over 8 MiB of reliable data it can't drain is closed as too slow. Server errors after listening are logged. Sends while connecting are queued; every connection gets one `close` with a reason. Nothing under `src/engine/` imports `src/server/` or `ws`. **Wave 3.** **Sessions** (`net/session.ts`): each networked World has one, owning its transports, connections, handshake, tick sync and message routing; links in the bootstrap connect at `Engine.create`, `WorldHandle.connect` at runtime (loopback addresses per Engine and server World unless given). The handshake checks protocol version, tick rate, RPCs and message kinds and closes with a reason on a mismatch. Connections are nodes with `net.Connection` (`id, state, rtt, lastAckedTick`), made and removed by the session; destroying one kicks the client. Ticks: clients ping every 100 ms and estimate the server's tick; `Time.network` gives `tick`, `serverTick` (estimate + RTT/2 + a 2-tick command slack), `interpolationTick` (estimate − RTT/2 − `interpolationMs`, default 100), `rtt`. Built-ins: `engine.NetReceiveTick` (fixedUpdate, first), `engine.NetReceive` (update), `engine.NetSend` (lateUpdate, last). **RPCs** (`defineRpc`): reliable, codec-encoded, read as rows (values + `connection`) once per phase. An internal message API (`registerNetMessage`, `netSession`, `send`, `drain`) carries snapshots and inputs. **Replication** (`net/Replicated.ts`, `net/replication/`): `net.Replicated` (`mode`, `ownerId`, `importance`, `replicateTransform`) makes a node a ghost; its transform (quantized), `replicate()`d data components and `@field(…, { replicate })` behavior fields go in per-connection unreliable snapshots, delta-encoded against the connection's last acknowledged snapshot, with spawns and despawns repeated until acked, within 64 KiB (most important and longest-unsent first). Prefab roots spawn by prefab path; scene-placed ghosts match by scene and node id path. Clients interpolate at `interpolationTick` (lerp, slerp, snapping integers) and extrapolate up to 250 ms. About 1.25 bytes per unchanged ghost. **Per-World components**: `@behavior/@component(id, { worlds })`, or `worlds` on a scene file's component entry, keeps a component out of Worlds whose tags don't match (editor Worlds create everything). A prefab instance overrides it with `{ component, index?, worlds }` (ADR 0042), and the Inspector's component menu sets it. **Inputs** (`defineInput`): clients `write()` each fixed tick; each packet carries the last 4 ticks delta-encoded (15 bytes for a still 9-byte input); the server applies `Move.of(connection)` at its tick, repeating the newest input for a missing tick, with `stats()` counting received, repeated and late ticks; a tick's input is sent once even when two fixed steps share a `serverTick`, and a client's own `of()` (live and replayed) reads its input as it travels (f32, quantized, i32, u8), as the server does. Review fixes: a client's timeline (serverTick, interpolationTick) starts afresh on each connection; connections time out when the handshake doesn't finish in 5 s or nothing arrives for 15 s (`handshakeTimeoutMs`, `idleTimeoutMs`; a paused server World's clients time out too), and servers refuse connections past `maxConnections` (64); input-schema mismatches are reported as such; spawns carry each component's field shapes (protocol version 2), so a client decodes and ignores components it lacks instead of losing whole snapshots; a prefab ghost under a plain scene node (a container) spawns under the same node on clients; every ghost's state history is pruned; `Move.of(playerNode)` resolves the owner from `Replicated.ownerId`; over-budget snapshots choose from last tick's sizes and encode once. **Wave 4.** **Prediction** (`net/prediction.ts`): systems with `predicted: true` (fixedUpdate only) run on a client's prediction timeline for the ghosts it owns and predicts, once per `serverTick` (bounded catch-up of skipped ticks), keeping a per-ghost history of predicted values; a snapshot that differs beyond quantization restores the server's state and re-runs the ticks since with each tick's input (`isResimulating`, `predictedTick`); small corrections blend the rendered position over 100 ms (built-ins `engine.PredictionTick`, `engine.PredictionSmooth`); `predictionStats(world)` counts mispredictions, rollbacks and re-simulated ticks. **Dedicated server** (`src/server/`, `npm run server -- <project>`, bin `myco-server`): runs a project's server-tagged Worlds headless in Node over WebSocket, with the bootstrap cut down to its server Worlds (or, when it has none, its server-tagged templates, each created at start under its key, for games whose Host button creates the server World), `--port`, `--host`, `--max-connections`, `--tick-rate` and `--stats-interval` options, a drift-free step timer, connection and stats logs and clean shutdown. Each server World listens on the port of a `websocket` link to it, the first falling back to `--port` or 7777; code bootstraps run with non-server Worlds replaced by inert handles whose `connect` records the link. Server Worlds created at runtime listen through `WorldHandle.listen` (ADR 0067); the server logs one whose link names a transport other than `websocket`. Node runs the TypeScript source through module hooks (`tsLoader.mjs`). **Network tools** (`src/editor/netPlay.ts`, `ui/NetworkPanel.tsx`): Play runs 1–4 client Worlds plus 0–8 thin clients (no rendering, synthetic input) from the project's client World without editing project.json; latency, jitter and loss sliders apply live to every loopback link; the Network panel shows per World and connection the state, RTT, bytes/s per message kind, snapshot size, ghosts, input and prediction stats. **Wave 4 review fixes:** the dedicated server takes listen addresses only from `websocket` links; play mode puts simulated conditions in loopback links' options (so the handshake and tick sync see them) and Off stops reapplying them; input streams are forgotten with their scripts. **Multiplayer sample** (T16, `samples/arena/`): a top-down shooter in Unity's frontend pattern. The bootstrap starts one client World on a menu and keeps a `Server` template (headless) and a `Client 2` template; Host creates the Server World and connects over loopback, P (while hosting) adds a second client World (tiled in the editor), Join connects to a dedicated server by address (`npm run server -- samples/arena` runs the Server template on 7777). The server spawns a predicted player per `net.Connection` and removes it when the connection goes; a predicted system moves players from a `defineInput` stream; shots are server-spawned interpolated ghosts; hits score for the shooter, and scores replicate to the HUD. Tests: `src/server/server.test.ts` (join, move, leave, a hit scored and seen by two clients) and `tests/e2e/arena.spec.ts` (Host, a second local player, input, a score; Join against the dedicated server). Fixed on the way: ghost layouts were cached by component id, so after scripts loaded again (an editor reload, or a second load in one process) they held the old definitions and replicated data components stopped replicating; a cached layout is now reused only while its components resolve to the same definitions. **Multiplayer play for the Host pattern** (`netPlay.ts` `hostLayout`): a bootstrap with no links, no server World and a `server`-tagged template (Arena's) plays with the template created as a World under its own name and N client plus T thin client copies of the project's client World linked to it over loopback; the copies load the server template's scenes (join directly) or, with the Network panel's **Clients load: Their own scenes**, their own (a menu). Thin clients' synthetic input now wins over the game's own writes to the same stream (`_writeSyntheticInput` in `net/input.ts`: the World's `write`/`local` go to a scratch copy), so Arena's keyboard system writing zeros every tick doesn't cancel it. **Bandwidth per ghost type**: the server attributes each ghost's spawn and state bits in the snapshots sent to its type (prefab path, `scene#node/path` for scene ghosts, `prefab › path` for nested ones) per connection (`PeerState.typeBits`, `NetPeerInfo.ghostTypes`); the Network panel's expanded server connection shows bytes per second, share and total per type. Tests: `netPlay.test.ts` (Host bootstraps from Arena's project.json, thin input beating a zero-writing system), `replication.test.ts` (types and bytes), `tests/e2e/playdebug.spec.ts` (Arena with 2 clients and a thin client, ghost-type table). **Follow-ups (ADR 0100–0102):** node activity and reparenting replicate, and spawn strings go through a per-connection string table ([0100](../decisions/0100-replicate-node-structure-and-a-string-table.md)); `replication: { sendRate, maxSnapshotBytes }` on a World config ([0101](../decisions/0101-replication-settings-on-world-configs.md)); a `websocket` link between two Worlds of a page connects them over loopback ([0102](../decisions/0102-in-page-websocket-links-use-loopback.md)); `WorldHandle.listen` for runtime server Worlds is ADR 0067. **Interest management and ghost component changes ([0121](../decisions/0121-interest-management-and-ghost-component-changes.md)):** per-connection relevancy by distance (`Replicated.relevantDistance`, viewpoints from owned root ghosts or `Interest.setViewer`, 10% hysteresis) and by group (`Replicated.group`, `Interest.join`/`leave`), hierarchical, before the `importance` ordering; ghosts leave cleanly (prefab ghosts destroyed, scene and nested ones deactivated) and re-enter with a fresh spawn and strings; replicated components' enabled state, order among sibling ghosts, and components added or removed after first sight (relayouts) replicate; protocol 5. Arena's shots carry `relevantDistance: 20` (wider than the arena). Proposed updates, not built (O19–O23): a spatial grid, a custom relevancy filter, distance-scaled priority, sibling order among non-ghost siblings, a public API for game code to reorder children (only the editor and UI do). User guide: [Multiplayer](../wiki/guide/multiplayer.md). |
| [0034](../decisions/0034-current-world-view-and-async.md) Current World, view, async | Done | Implemented with 0032's wave 1 review; details in the 0032 row. Between frames the default World is current (facades throw only with no Engine); a parent decides the World for `GameObject.create`/`instantiate`; `this.instantiate` and `this.time` use the behavior's World; scene loads and physics events keep their World. One view per Engine: the first World that wants it (not `server`-tagged unless `renders: true`), input following it unless a config sets `input`, passed on when its World is destroyed. Game systems don't run in the Editor World unless their filter names `editor`. `createWorld` validates before changing anything. The editor tiles several Worlds' views (T5). **Not done:** the record's open question, a development warning when a facade is used after an `await` in a non-default World. |
| [0035](../decisions/0035-ws-for-the-node-websocket-server.md) `ws` for the Node WebSocket server | Done | `ws` v8 (a runtime dependency, `@types/ws` a dev one) is imported only by `src/server/websocketServer.ts`; nothing under `src/engine/` imports `src/server/` or `ws`, and the browser client uses the global `WebSocket`. Binary messages and close reasons only; `permessage-deflate` is off (the `ws` default). TLS (`wss://`) isn't supported by the dedicated server: put a reverse proxy in front. |
| [0036](../decisions/0036-connect-a-world-by-address.md) Connect a World by address | Done | `WorldHandle.connect(server, transport, options?)` with `server` a handle or a string: a string is the server's address (`ws://localhost:7777`), passed to the transport as `url` and `address` (`core/WorldHandle.ts`, `connectWorld` in `net/session.ts`). The Arena sample's Join uses it; `src/server/server.test.ts` joins the dedicated server through it. A code bootstrap run by `myco-server` ignores address connects. |
