Open Project…, Make a Copy…, saving, asset operations, scripts (with polling reload) and edit history (`slotsFor` now uses the project's own file system) all work in Chromium. **New Project** (`newProject.ts`, `ui/NewProjectDialog.tsx`): name (checked for folder-safe names), Empty 2D / Empty 3D, a parent folder; writes `project.json` with every setting, `scenes/main.scene.json` with a camera, `scripts/`, the scripting files and a `.gitignore`, then opens it; refuses a non-empty folder. **Recent projects** (`recentProjects.ts`, `ui/StartProjects.tsx`): last 10 in localStorage, desktop by path, browser by folder handle in IndexedDB; reopen (the browser asks for access again; a desktop folder outside the fs scope is picked again in a dialog starting there), remove, missing folders shown and disabled. **File menu** (`ui/MenuBar.tsx`, items as data in `menuBar.ts`; the toolbar's Project menu and its `addProjectMenuItem` hook are gone): New Project, Open Project, Open Recent (up to 5), Project Settings, Close Project. **Project panel** (`ui/ProjectSettingsPanel.tsx`, `projectSettingsModel.ts`): name, display (size, background colour), time (fixed timestep, max frame time, max sub-steps), physics (gravity, layers, collision matrix as a checkbox triangle), render (pixels per unit, sorting layers with reorder), autoloads (add, remove, reorder), build (identifier, version, icon for export, ADR 0041); problems block saving and are listed, warnings don't; undo/redo; read-only for samples. | [0045](../decisions/0045-3d-physics-rapier3d.md) 3D physics | Partial | `@dimforge/rapier3d-compat` behind the Rapier 3D backend (`plugins/physics-rapier/runtime/rapier3d.ts`, ADR 0137), driven by the shared sync core (`plugins/physics/runtime/core/PhysicsWorld3D.ts`) and loaded by the Physics plugin's 3D World service (`PHYSICS_3D_SERVICE` in `plugins/physics/runtime/systems3d.ts`, ADR 0135) only when a World needs it. Scenes with `"physics": "3d"`, or without a setting but with 3D bodies or colliders, start it. Fixed-step systems `engine.Physics3DPush/Step/WriteBack/Events`. **Editor** (`colliderGizmo.ts`, `collider3dMath.ts`): wireframes for every 3D collider (mesh colliders by triangle edges, or bounds over 2000 triangles); Edit Collider on box (face handles, Alt symmetric), sphere (radius) and capsule (radius, height) colliders, plus a centre handle; one undo step per drag. Tests: `engine/tests/physics3d.test.ts`, `editor/collider3d.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/three-d.spec.ts`. Since 2026-11-07: `CharacterController` (0091), joints (0092), shape casts and overlaps, interpolation, `Stay` events, child colliders following their node (0093), Edit Collider for mesh colliders (centre handle) and character controllers. Missing: PhysX (0005). |
| [0046](../decisions/0046-shader-graph-editor-on-xyflow-system.md) Shader graph editor | Done | **Editor** (`plugins/materials/editor/ShaderGraphPanel.tsx`, `shaderGraph/`): the dockable Shader Graph panel (`shadergraph`, a tab beside Scene; Maximize fills the window), opened by double-clicking a `*.shadergraph.json` asset, the Inspector's Open in Shader Graph, or Edit Shader Graph on a graph material. Canvas interactions from `@xyflow/system` 0.0.84 (pinned) through one adapter (`graph/xyflow.ts`): middle/right-drag pan, wheel and pinch zoom, node drag (all selected), wiring with a connection radius and validation, Bezier wires. Fixed-row node layout (`shaderGraph/nodeLayout.ts`) feeds xyflow's handle bounds. Node search (Space, right-click, Add Node, or dropping a wire in space: filtered to nodes that fit, then connected), typed port and wire colours from theme tokens, inline fields for unconnected inputs (colour pickers for colour inputs), node settings, box selection, Delete, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+A, F to frame, dragging a connected input picks its wire up. Blackboard: add, rename (nodes follow), retype, reorder, remove properties, set defaults, Place a property node. Graph settings: target, surface, double-sided. Edits are pure functions (`shaderGraph/graphEdit.ts`), saved as made; undo/redo per graph for the session (`shaderGraphAssets.ts`), separate from the scene's history. Live preview (`render/babylon/previews/MaterialPreview.ts`, through `engine/editor`: sphere, cube, capsule or plane, drag to turn; the mesh is saved with the graph as `previewMesh`, ADR 0117) and shader compile errors shown in the panel; problems listed and marked on nodes; the generated WGSL on demand. Assets panel New Shader Graph; the Inspector's graph card has New Material. Tests: `plugins/materials/editor/shaderGraph/graphEdit.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/shadergraph.spec.ts`. Node previews, groups, notes, copy and paste, the minimap and sub-graph editing are 0048's. Ctrl+F find box (`shaderGraph/graphSearch.ts`: node titles and types, property names, notes, groups; Enter and arrows cycle and frame); node preview shapes (quad, sphere, cube) per node and per graph, ADR 0117. Tests also: `plugins/materials/editor/shaderGraph/previewShape.test.ts`, `plugins/materials/editor/shaderGraph/previewMesh.test.ts`. |
| [0088](../decisions/0088-gltf-import-through-threejs-gltfloader.md) glTF import | Partial | **Engine** (`scene/gltf.ts`, `scene/models.ts`): `.gltf` (embedded or external buffers and images from the project) and `.glb` through three's `GLTFLoader`, imported on first use (three.js itself too: `gltf.ts` has type-only three imports, compatible with 0082's lazy three.js); Draco through the vendored pure-JS decoder (`scene/vendor/draco_decoder.js`, works headless), meshopt through three's decoder; images not decoded while parsing (URL, project path or bytes kept, decoded by Babylon); KTX2 handed to Babylon's KTX2 loader; WebP/AVIF passed through. Baked like OBJ/FBX with the sidecar's scale, up axis and normals (float copies, so quantized meshes keep precision), V flipped to v-up, `KHR_texture_transform` baked into UVs, GPU instances merged; a mesh's primitives merge into one mesh with a group per primitive; meshes record their node path; unsupported required extensions refused; skins, animations, morph targets and extra scenes ignored with one warning (lights and cameras are imported since 0118). **Materials**: `SurfaceInfo` gains emissive (times emissive strength) and its map, normal map and scale, metallic-roughness map, occlusion map and strength, alpha mode and cutoff, unlit, vertex colours (`MeshData.colors`), second UV set (`MeshData.uvs2`), and the physical layers' factors; `render/babylon/materials.ts` maps them onto the PBR material. **Editor**: `.gltf`, `.glb`, `.ktx2` are project assets (desktop, browser folders, samples, builds); glTF models are "Model"s with the model import settings and thumbnails (a `.gltf`'s buffers and images are dependencies when its text is known); drops build the file's node tree less the shared levels (`modelNodeEntries`). **Sample**: `samples/playground3d/models/lamp.glb` (CC0, in the main scene). Tests: `engine/tests/gltf.test.ts` (embedded, external, GLB, missing buffer, sidecar, Draco, meshopt, warnings, refused extension, the lamp), `engine/tests/materialSlots.test.ts` (surface → PBR), `editor/collider3d.test.ts` (node tree), `editor/thumbnails/thumbnails.test.ts`, `engine/tests/threeImports.test.ts`. Not verified by a test: KTX2 textures (no fixture; Babylon fetches its transcoder from its CDN), how glTF materials look on screen. Node pivots, lights and cameras, the physical layers' textures, anisotropy and variants are 0118's (built). Missing: `KHR_materials_dispersion`; skins and animations (deferred with the animation system). |
| [0089](../decisions/0089-material-slots.md) Material slots | Done | **Engine**: `materialSlots` (`scene/models.ts`: distinct materials in order of first use, one `Default` slot for meshes without materials; unnamed materials named `Material N` at import); `MeshRenderer.materials` (`@field([AssetRef])`); sidecar `materials` by material name (`scene/importSettings.ts`); `BabylonRenderer.syncMesh` resolves `materials[i]` → `material` → sidecar → the model's own, one material or a `MultiMaterial` per mesh (now disposed with it); scene loads preload sidecar materials' textures; thumbnails resolve slots through the same material library as the World (`slotMaterials`, ADR 0127). Scripts reach slots through the Material API of 0107 (`getSharedMaterial(slot)`, `setSharedMaterial(slot, m)`, `getMaterialInstance(slot)`, `sharedMaterials`). **Editor**: `materials[i]` suggests material assets; the model Asset Inspector lists slots with a material each and **Extract Materials** (`editor/materialExtract.ts`: `<model> Materials/<Name>.material.json`, embedded textures written as images, sidecar mapped; what can't be kept is reported). Tests: `engine/tests/materialSlots.test.ts`, `editor/materialExtract.test.ts`. Slot materials are warmed up through the material library (0080, 0127). Not covered: `setMaterialProperty` per slot (use a slot's Material instance, 0107), the Asset Inspector rows in an end-to-end test. |
| [0090](../decisions/0090-baked-models-in-game-builds.md) Baked models in builds | Done | `scene/bakedModel.ts` (`encodeBakedModel`/`decodeBakedModel`, `.mycomesh`), `scene/models.ts` (`bakeModelFile`; `parseModel` prefers a baked copy baked with the current import settings, else warns and parses the file); textures by project path (recorded on load, headless too) or embedded bytes. Exporters: `withBakedModels` (`export/webBuild.ts`) from `export/node.ts` and `editor/exportBuild.ts`, files under `project/`, listed in the manifest. Baked meshes have no three.js geometry, so loading them never loads three.js. Not measured: load time on large models. |
| [0047](../decisions/0047-shader-graph-assets-compile-to-glsl.md) Shader graph assets | Done (on WGSL; amended by 0075 and 0081) | **Format** (`scene/shaderGraph.ts`): `*.shadergraph.json`, `myco.shadergraph@1` (target, surface, doubleSided, properties float/vector2–4/color/texture, nodes with inline `inputs` and `settings`, edges `node.port`), parsed leniently, one node/edge/property per line. **Nodes** (`render/shaderGraph/nodes.ts`): Input (Time, UV, Position, Normal Vector, View Direction, Screen Position, Float, Vector 2/3/4, Color, Property, Sample Texture 2D, Texture 2D Asset), Math (26), Vector (Split, Combine, Swizzle, Dot, Cross, Normalize, Length, Distance, Reflection, Tiling And Offset, Rotate), Color (Saturation, Hue, Contrast, Invert Colors), Noise (Simple, Gradient, Voronoi), Lighting (Fresnel Effect, Main Light Direction/Color, Lambert), the Output node (Vertex Offset, Base Color, Alpha, Metalness, Roughness, Emission, Normal, Alpha Clip); Quality Branch (0050), Keyword (0106), Sub Graph and Custom Function (0048). Dynamic vector types and implicit conversions. **Compiler** (`render/shaderGraph/compile.ts`, no renderer imports): WGSL chunks per stage (ADR 0075; the only output since 0081), one local per output, shared values computed once, problems reported per node (loops, unknown nodes, missing properties, per-pixel nodes in Vertex Offset, textures into numbers), a program key. **Materials** (`render/babylon/graphMaterial.ts`): a Babylon PBR material with a material plugin that splices the graph's WGSL in at Babylon's injection points (lit and physical graphs set the surface, unlit ones draw their colour; anchors unit-tested against Babylon's own WGSL), shared time, resolution and main-light uniforms, property uniforms from the material's `properties` over the graph's defaults, textures through the backend's loader (white until loaded), a shadow-depth wrapper for vertex offsets. Graphs need WebGPU: on the WebGL2 fallback they draw as plain PBR with one warning (ADR 0081). Material assets take `shader: "asset:…shadergraph.json"` and `properties`; a MeshRenderer may name a graph directly; editing either rebuilds the material. **Editor**: the Material inspector lists graphs as shaders and edits a graph material's property values (Reset to the graph's default). **Sample**: `samples/playground3d/shaders/pulse.shadergraph.json` on the Pillar (`materials/pulse.material.json`). Game builds carry graphs (`tests/e2e/export.spec.ts`). Tests: `engine/tests/shaderGraph.test.ts`, `engine/tests/shaderGraphWgsl.test.ts`, `engine/tests/babylonGraphMaterial.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/shadergraph.spec.ts`. Sub-graphs, custom functions and normal-map sampling are 0048's; scripts set material properties per renderer (0049). Keywords and variants (a graph `keywords` list and the Keyword node, 0106) and changing material assets from scripts (`Material`, 0107) are in. |
| [0048](../decisions/0048-shader-sub-graphs-and-custom-functions.md) Sub-graphs, custom functions, normal maps, previews | Partial | **Format**: `*.shadersubgraph.json` (`myco.shadersubgraph@1`: properties as inputs, `outputs`), node `function` (Custom Function: name, typed inputs and outputs, WGSL code; older files' GLSL `body` is kept, shown read-only and reported, not compiled, ADR 0081), node `preview: false`, graph `groups` and `notes` (`scene/shaderGraph.ts`; port refs split at the first dot, so a sub-graph's property names may hold dots). **Compiler** (`render/shaderGraph/compile.ts`): scopes; Sub Graph nodes expand inline with per-use locals, properties bound to the node's inputs, files read through `Assets` (`resolveSubGraph`, parsed once per file version), missing files and self-containing sub-graphs reported on the node; `dependencies` listed so the render backend rebuilds materials when a sub-graph changes; Custom Function nodes become `fn name_<n>(…) -> name_<n>_Out` functions; Sample Texture 2D `type: normal` samples a linear copy of the texture and unpacks `rgb * 2 - 1` (a texture property sampled both ways is reported); a sub-graph compiles on its own as its first output, unlit; `nodePreviewGraph` for previews. **Editor**: New Shader Sub Graph; sub-graphs open in the Shader Graph panel (Inputs and Outputs in the side bar); Sub Graph nodes from the node search; the Custom Function editor in the side bar for the selected node (name, ports, code; shader errors shown); node previews from one offscreen renderer (`NodePreviewRenderer`, about 10 fps; collapsible; failed shaders outlined); copy, cut and paste (Ctrl+C/X/V, between graphs, with the properties nodes read); groups (Ctrl+G or Group; drag by the title; rename; ungroup); sticky notes (Note; drag, resize, type); a minimap (xyflow's `XYMinimap`). **Sample**: `samples/playground3d/shaders/ripple.shadersubgraph.json` (a Custom Function inside) used by `water.shadergraph.json` (`materials/water.material.json`). Tests: `engine/tests/shaderSubGraphs.test.ts`, `plugins/materials/editor/shaderGraph/graphOrganise.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/shadersubgraph.spec.ts`. Sub-graph outputs may be textures (the uniform passes through; a Texture 2D Asset node supplies one inside a graph). Custom function bodies from files (`function.file`, a `.wgsl` file: graphs compile to WGSL only, so not `.glsl`; 0106) and keywords are in; sub-graphs declare keywords of their own (Keywords in the side bar), which their uses add to the shader (0106). |
| [0049](../decisions/0049-runtime-material-properties.md) Runtime material properties | Done | `MeshRenderer.setMaterialProperty / getMaterialProperty / clearMaterialProperty / clearMaterialProperties` (`render/components.ts`; `MaterialPropertyValue` exported): numbers, `Vector2`, `Vector3`, `Color`, number lists, texture paths. The render backend gives a renderer with overrides its own material (graph materials share the compiled program and update uniforms; built-in lit/unlit materials are copied and take `color`, `emission`, `metalness`, `roughness`, `texture`), dropped when the overrides are cleared or the component goes; unknown names warn once. Runtime only: not saved, not replicated. Tests: `engine/tests/shaderSubGraphs.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/shadersubgraph.spec.ts`. |
| [0050](../decisions/0050-graphics-quality-tiers.md) Graphics quality tiers | Partial | **Engine**: `project.json` `graphics` (`core/graphics.ts`: tiers with `renderScale`, `antialias`, `shadows`, `anisotropy`, `shaderLevel`; Low/Medium/High/Ultra by default, High the default; parsed leniently, problems warned at start); `Graphics` (`render/Graphics.ts`, public): `tiers`, `quality` (setting it applies and saves the player's choice in Storage `settings`), `tier`, `defaultQuality`; the Engine activates the saved choice at start. The render backend applies the tier when it changes: pixel ratio (device ratio capped at 2 × render scale), shadows on/off and map size for every light; MSAA is the camera texture's sample count, so `antialias` changes live (0051). **Not applied:** the tier's `anisotropy` field is parsed and edited but the Babylon backend never sets texture anisotropy (`qualityState()` reports 1). **Editor**: the Project panel's Graphics section (tiers' fields, add, rename, reorder, remove, default tier; `projectSettingsModel.ts`), applied to the running editor on save (`graphicsPreview.ts`); the scene view's Quality menu previews a tier without saving a choice. Tests: `engine/tests/graphics.test.ts`, `editor/projectSettingsModel.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/graphics.spec.ts`. Post-processing tier fields and MSAA are 0051's; cascade fields are 0053's. Quality Branch shader graph node (`render/shaderGraph/nodes.ts`, `compile.ts`: only the active tier's `shaderLevel` input and its upstream nodes are compiled; the level is part of the program key only for graphs using it; materials and previews rebuild when the level changes; `engine/tests/qualityBranch.test.ts`). Automatic detection is 0055's. **Per-platform defaults and cascaded lights (0110, 0111, 2026-11-07):** `graphics.platformDefaults` (`desktop`/`web`/`mobile` tier names, parsed leniently); `detectPlatform` (Tauri → desktop, phone/tablet user agents incl. iPadOS → mobile, else web); start tier = saved choice → auto-detect (0055) → platform default → `defaultTier`; `Graphics.platform`, `Graphics.defaultQuality` is the effective default; Project panel selects for the three. Tiers gain `cascadedLights` (1–4, Low 1/Medium 1/High 2/Ultra 4; tier cards). Tests: `graphics.test.ts`, `projectSettingsModel.test.ts`. |
| [0051](../decisions/0051-post-processing.md) Post-processing | Done (on Babylon; amended by 0111, proposed) | 0051's pmndrs `postprocessing` and N8AO went with the three.js backend (0081); 0111 records what the text means now. **Engine**: `PostProcessing` component (`render/components.ts`: tone mapping none/ACES/AgX/Neutral and exposure, bloom, ambient occlusion, brightness/contrast/saturation/hue, `lut`/`lutIntensity`, vignette, depth of field, chromatic aberration); `render/postEffects.ts` combines a camera's component with the tier (`postProcessing`, `bloom`, `ambientOcclusion` off–ultra, `depthOfField`, `antialias` off/MSAA/SMAA/FXAA). Each camera's effects are Babylon frame-graph tasks (`render/babylon/frameGraph.ts`): a geometry pass (view depth, normals for AO) before the render pass, SSAO2 (low/medium at half resolution with 8/16 samples, high/ultra full with 32/48: `AO_SETTINGS` in `render/postEffects.ts`; the passes from the pass plan, 0274), depth of field, bloom, chromatic aberration, then our finish pass (`finishShader.ts`: grading, vignette, tone mapping, sRGB, then the `.cube` LUT on the display-referred image) and FXAA (SMAA asks for FXAA: the frame graph has no SMAA). MSAA is the camera texture's 4 samples, 1 with AO. **LUTs (0111):** `.cube` is the "Colour LUT" asset kind; `render/lut.ts` parses and lays it out as a strip; `render/babylon/lutTextures.ts` uploads it on first use (`Assets.bytes`, ADR 0254). **Editor**: the scene view previews the scene camera's effects (without depth of field), the grid and gizmos drawn after over the canvas; the editor's own view shows the editor background behind the tone-mapped scene, not tone mapped (0111: the camera clears to transparent and the finish pass composites `backdrop`); the Project panel's tier cards edit the tier fields; Inspector datalist for `lut`. Tests: `postEffects.test.ts`, `passPlan.test.ts` (0274), `babylonFrame.test.ts` (rebuilds, LUT sampler, AO settings), `lut.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/postprocessing.spec.ts`. **Not verified by a test:** a LUT's look on screen, the editor backdrop with every effect on (relies on effects passing alpha through). |
| [0052](../decisions/0052-physical-materials.md) Physical materials | Done | **Materials**: `shader: "physical"` with `clearcoat`, `clearcoatRoughness`, `sheen`, `sheenColor`, `sheenRoughness`, `transmission`, `thickness`, `ior`, `iridescence`, `iridescenceIOR`, `anisotropy`, `anisotropyRotation` (degrees), `specularIntensity`, `specularColor` (`scene/material.ts`, written only when not default), drawn with Babylon's PBR layers (`render/babylon/materials.ts`: clear coat, sheen, sub-surface refraction, iridescence, anisotropy, the F0 factor and reflectance colour); the Material inspector groups them; `setMaterialProperty` takes them. **Shader graphs**: target `physical`, output inputs Clearcoat, Clearcoat Roughness, Sheen Color, Sheen Roughness, Transmission, Thickness, IOR, Iridescence, Specular Intensity, each compiled in only when connected or away from its default (`fragment.physical`). The graph material switches on the PBR layers the graph uses (`enablePhysicalLayers`) and the plugin replaces those layers' uniform reads in Babylon's PBR fragment shader with the graph's values (`physicalCustomCode`: clear coat params, sheen colour and roughness, sub-surface intensity and thickness, IOR as F0 and refraction IOR, iridescence intensity, the F0 factor); anchors unit-tested against Babylon's own WGSL; checked on WebGPU in the editor's preview (each layer changes the image). Graph inputs for anisotropy, iridescence IOR and specular colour aren't in the record (an open question). Tests: `engine/tests/physicalMaterial.test.ts`, `engine/tests/physicalGraph.test.ts` (compile, injections against Babylon's PBR WGSL, layers on the NullEngine), `tests/e2e/physical.spec.ts`. |
| [0053](../decisions/0053-environments-and-cascaded-shadows.md) Environments and cascaded shadows | Done | **Environment**: `Environment` component (`render/components.ts`: `map` (.hdr, .exr, .png, .jpg equirectangular; also .env/.dds cubes and `.cubemap.json`, 0113, and `.sky.json`, 0112), `intensity`, `skybox`, `skyboxIntensity`, `skyboxBlur`, `rotation`); `render/babylon/lighting.ts` loads the map on demand (HDR/EXR/equirectangular cube textures, prefiltered by Babylon), sets the scene's environment and the skybox box fitted per camera (blurred skies use the prefiltered map); without one, an in-memory studio room at 0.4 (`studioEnvironment.ts`). `.hdr`/`.exr` are project assets ("Environment" kind). **Cascaded shadows**: tiers gain `shadowCascades` (1 = off … 4) and `shadowDistance` (Low 1/30 m, Medium 2/50, High 3/80, Ultra 4/120; Project panel tier cards); on tiers with cascades, directional lights with shadows get frame-graph cascaded shadow tasks following the main camera; rebuilt when the cascade count, map size or camera changes. **Several cascaded lights (0110, 2026-11-07):** the first `cascadedLights` (tier field) directional lights with shadows get cascades; one on devices at 16 inter-stage values (`cascadeLimit`). Tests: `engine/tests/environment.test.ts`, `babylonLighting.test.ts`, `babylonShadowCulling.test.ts`, `engine/tests/graphics.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/lighting.spec.ts`. | [0054](../decisions/0054-reflection-probes.md) Reflection probes | Done (look not verified) | `ReflectionProbe` component (`render/components.ts`: `size` box, `intensity`, `refresh` onLoad/everyFrame/baked, `capture()`); `render/probeBoxes.ts` (`pickProbe`: smallest box holding a point; `blendProbes`); `render/babylon/probes.ts` (Babylon `ReflectionProbe` cubes, half float). | [0055](../decisions/0055-automatic-quality-detection.md) Automatic quality detection | Done | `graphics.autoDetect` (default off) and `targetFrameRate` (`core/graphics.ts`, Project panel); `render/qualityDetect.ts` (`guessTier` from GPU name, cores, memory; `tierForGuess`; `FrameMonitor`: settle, rolling median, step down only, hitches ignored). The Engine (runtime Worlds with a canvas, no saved player choice) guesses a tier at start, then watches the first seconds of play and steps down while frames are slow; detected tiers are saved as `graphics.autoQuality`, and a player's choice always wins. `Graphics.detectQuality()`, `Graphics.autoDetected`. | [0057](../decisions/0057-particle-effects-and-the-particlesystem-component.md) Particle effects and ParticleSystem | Partial | **Superseded in part** by 0060 (the authored format is now `myco.particles@2` graphs, embedded effects are deprecated, the Inspector effect editor is gone) and amended by 0081 (no `three.quarks`; effects play on the engine's own simulation and Babylon.js, ADR 0075). **Model**: `scene/particles.ts` (emitters with main, emission and bursts, shape, ordered behaviours (colour, size, rotation, speed and force over lifetime, point gravity, noise, turbulence, orbit, flipbook, limit speed, colour and size by speed, trail width, plane collision), sub-emitters, renderer (billboard, stretched, horizontal, vertical, mesh, trail; material asset, or texture and blending; flipbook tiles); number and colour values as constant/random/curve/gradient variants) is the target 0060's compiler produces; `myco.particles@1` files are read and migrated. **Runtime**: `render/particles/simulation.ts` (deleted by ADR 0251; renderer-neutral CPU simulation of the whole model, no Babylon import), `render/babylon/particles.ts` (plays the World's `ParticleSystem` components on its scaled time × `timeScale`: script commands, follows the node, rebuilds when the effect, tint or a material changes, destroys finished one-shot nodes with `destroyOnFinish`), `particleDraw.ts` / `particleMeshes.ts` (`particleMeshes.ts` and `particleDraw.ts`' CPU branch deleted by ADR 0251; drawn through Babylon's CPU particle system and meshes); kernels and GPU draws are 0094's. `ParticleSystem` component (`effect`, `embeddedEffect`, `playOnAwake`, `timeScale`, `tint`, `emissionScale`, `destroyOnFinish`, `seed`; `play`, `pause`, `stop({ clear })`, `restart`, `clear`, `emit`, `isPlaying`, `isAlive`, `playTime`, `particleCount`). Without a material, emitters draw an unlit material with their texture or a built-in soft dot. Tiers gain `particles` (low/medium/high: emission and limits × 0.25/0.5/1; Project panel). **Editor**: "Particle Effect" asset kind (New Particle Effect in the Assets menu, drag into the scene), Create menu Particle System, and the scene view plays selected systems with an overlay (play/pause, restart, speed, Simulate All, time and count); the effect itself is edited in the Particle Graph (0060). **Sample**: the 3D Playground's campfire (flames, smoke, sparks). edits in `editor/particleGraph/graphEdit.ts`), its live preview (`render/babylon/previews/EffectPreview.ts`, loaded on demand; `ui/particleGraph/PreviewPane.tsx`), the shared graph-editor layer (`editor/graph/`) the Shader Graph now also uses; | [0061](../decisions/0061-asset-previews-and-the-thumbnail-cache.md) Asset previews and the thumbnail cache | Done | **Engine**: `render/babylon/previews/ThumbnailRenderer.ts` (one offscreen Babylon context, transparent 256 px stills as WebP, PNG where the browser can't encode WebP: models framed from three-quarters above, materials and shader graphs on a sphere, environments on a chrome sphere, skies and cubemaps (0112/0113), particle effects one second in with a seeded random, prefabs' meshes and sprites, sprite-only prefabs from the front; `animate` draws one hovered asset live at about 30 fps into a 2D canvas, `canAnimate` for particle effects and materials or graphs using Time; `preview` (`ThumbnailPreview`) draws live, orbitable previews on demand, several at once (the Inspector's and the Sky panel's of the same sky each draw their own scene in turn), taking priority over hover animations; an `.mtl` material library is drawn by the model renderer as a sphere per material, loaded by `scene/models.ts`), `render/thumbnailMath.ts` (framing, seeded random), reached through `createThumbnailRenderer` in `src/engine/editor`. **Editor**: `src/editor/thumbnails/` (`keys.ts`: preview kinds, dependencies followed through JSON references, .obj/.mtl texture maps and sidecars, SHA-256 content keys; `store.ts`: `.myco/thumbnails/` or memory for read-only projects; `queue.ts`: at most two renders a frame, dropped when not needed; `audio.ts`: waveform peaks, length, channels, sample rate with Web Audio, cached as `<key>.peaks.json`; `service.ts`; `index.ts`: JSON and text files (a shader asset's `.wgsl`/`.glsl`) read from their text, other files fetched by URL; `thumbnailFor`, `audioSummaryFor`, `canAnimate`, `animateThumbnail`, `previewAsset`, `installThumbnails` (pruning in idle time)). Tests: `tests/e2e/thumbnails.spec.ts` (rendered tiles and the Inspector preview, hover animation, audio waveform and playback (Chromium; Playwright's WebKit has no MP3 decoder), a writable project's `.myco/thumbnails/` read back from the cache (cached files replaced by a marker image), orbit/zoom/reset on a prefab's live preview, the particle effect Inspector's preview, an environment map's preview), `engine/tests/thumbnailMath.test.ts` (orbit maths). | [0062](../decisions/0062-links-to-spawnable-worlds.md) Links to Spawnable Worlds | Done | **Engine** (`core/Engine.ts`, `core/settings.ts`, `core/WorldHandle.ts`): a link's ends may name start-up Worlds or Spawnable Worlds (a template's `name`, else its key; `validateBootstrap`). `Engine.create` connects the links whose Worlds both exist at start; `createWorld` then applies the links the new World completes (`applyLinks`), once per client/server pair, and again for a server created anew. A script's `connect` for a pair a link connected (while that connection lasts) warns and does nothing (`_linkedServer`). `NetSession.listening()` and `liveLinks(engine)` (`src/engine/editor`) list the live client-to-server connections between an Engine's Worlds. **Editor** (`worldsConfig.ts`, `ui/WorldsPanel.tsx`): templates are shown as Spawnable Worlds (+ Spawnable World; one graph with the start-up Worlds, laid out by `layoutGraph` in `worldsLayout.ts`: columns by link depth, orthogonal arrows routed between nodes, a label pill per link; Spawnable nodes dashed with a "spawnable" tag and a legend); selecting one shows `Engine.createWorld("Name")` with a copy button and the scripts that mention the name in quotes (`mentions`); link pickers offer start-up and Spawnable World names (`linkableNames`), links naming a Spawnable World draw dashed and say when they apply; renaming or removing a Spawnable World carries its links; an empty Links section explains runtime connections; during Play the graph draws the session's live connections (`liveLinks`, polled every 250 ms). Multiplayer play's Host pattern (`netPlay.ts`) ignores links naming Spawnable Worlds when choosing the pattern and keeps those that don't name the copied client or a copy. **Arena** declares `Client → Server` and `Client 2 → Server`; Host and P only create the Worlds. Tests: `engine/tests/spawnableLinks.test.ts`, `engine/tests/bootstrap.test.ts`, `editor/worldsConfig.test.ts`, `editor/netPlay.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/worldspanel.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/arena.spec.ts`. **Follow-ups:** the Worlds panel edits link `options` (`LINK_OPTIONS`, `setLinkOption`, `linkOptionProblems` in `worldsConfig.ts`; a collapsible Options section per link with the known options of its transport, defaults as placeholders, other keys as JSON-or-text, inline validation, undo; tests `editor/worldsConfig.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/worldspanel.spec.ts`). The dedicated server honours links to Spawnable Worlds (`src/server/run.ts`): a server template created at runtime listens where a `websocket` link to it from a skipped World says, unless its code already listens (a loopback link is logged and ignored); server templates run at start are named as `Engine.createWorld` names them (their `name`, else key), so links by that name apply; links between server Worlds and server templates are kept in the server's bootstrap (tests `server/server.test.ts`). Gaps: the link options UI hasn't been checked by hand in a browser. |

| [0065](../decisions/0065-built-in-navigation-foxfire-port.md) Built-in navigation (Foxfire port) | Done | **W0 done**: `native/nav/` Cargo workspace (`nav-math`: Foxfire's exact maths, xxHash3 chaining, fingerprints, `Hash128` chunk ids, Unity.Mathematics behaviours, vector types; `nav-format`: every baked blob field-for-field (`NavMeshData`, `NavVolumeData`, landing links, chunk border, gate table, formations; C# padding kept as explicit fields), Foxfire's fingerprint rules, and the `myco.nav@1` container with per-section fingerprints; `nav-wasm`: `version`, `bakeInfo`). `npm run build:nav` builds `src/engine/nav/wasm/` (committed); `src/engine/nav/loader.ts` loads it on demand in browsers and Node. Tests: 46 in `cargo test`, `engine/tests/navLoader.test.ts`. Third-party notices in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`. **W1 done**: `nav-recast` (8.5k lines), Foxfire's DotRecast port function by function with its double-precision steps kept: shared types with index-linked spans in a `SpanPool` (`types.rs`), rasterization (span sinks, profile-mask merging), filters, flat heightfields, compact heightfield, areas (erode, median, box/convex/cylinder marking, offset poly), distance field and watershed/monotone/layer regions, contours with hole merging, poly mesh (triangulate, merge, remove border vertices, adjacency, merge meshes), detail mesh. Deviations: stable sorts where Unity's sort is unstable (deterministic, not Unity-identical tie order), guards where the C# would read out of range. Tests: 71 in `nav-recast` (incl. `tests/pipeline.rs`: floor, hole and island, separate regions, raised platform, determinism and triangle-order independence). **W2 done**: `nav-bake` (10k lines): settings and profile/flyer grouping, orientation bins, classification and deterministic bucketing, the tile pipeline with tile/task caches, fold and jump clearance, the stitcher (internal, fold and jump links, seam and open edges, tile ranges), blob output with the nearest-poly index and fingerprint lines, the flight octree (resolution regions, auto-refine, air areas) and landing links, chunk borders, and `bake::bake_chunk` (meshes + settings + scene path/chunk id → `BakeFile`, incremental with caches; `input_signature`). The file gained an agent table (agent type → walk/fly groups) and gates' starting closed flags. Tests: 176 in `nav-bake` (Foxfire's NavBakeTests, BakeCacheTests, ChunkBounds/ChunkBorder tests, determinism incl. a golden fingerprint, flight and landing bakes, file round trip). A 50×50 m scene bakes in about 41 ms (11 ms warm). Not ported yet (need queries): hybrid/chunk routing tests and path-querying flight tests. **W3 done**: `nav-query` (3.2k lines): the shared search store (stamped key table reused across sliced searches, binary-heap open set), the query filter and area-cost resolution, nearest-poly index lookups, surface queries, sliced walking A* (folds, jumps, areas, gates, congestion, partial paths) and the straight-path funnel with fold unfolding and waypoint flags, flight-volume queries (leaf lookup, snapping, segment clearance through resolution blocks) and sliced flight A* with smoothing, and a new navmesh raycast that walks the surface across internal links and allowed folds (Detour's conventions: `t`, outward hit normal; jump links never followed). Tests: 66 on baked scenes (Foxfire's NavBakeTests path cases on real queries, flight-volume path cases, sliced = one-shot, determinism). To merge later: raycast's private frame/unfold helpers duplicate straight_path/surface. **W4 done** (`nav-query` now 9.8k lines; `nav-bake::overlay`): the loaded-chunk snapshot (`chunks.rs`: one entry per chunk and agent type, gates and overlay switch-offs, sorted so load order doesn't matter), seams across shared faces (poly and leaf links, `SeamSet` rebuilding on signature change), crossing helpers, overlay planning and the overlay rebake across chunk edges, route graphs (cross-seam links, frontiers, air links, overlay landings; gate states refresh without a rebuild), walking and fly-only chunk routing, the shared sliced route search, hybrid walk/fly routes within and across chunks, and flow fields (budgeted builds, LRU memory cap, the read side). The file gained a chunk-info section (box, bounded) so fly-only bounded chunks keep their seams; entries carry the bake signature. Tests: ~100 more (Foxfire's NavSeam, NavCrossing, NavOverlay, NavFlowField, HybridRoute and NavRouteAffected tests; the pure parts of NavChunkRoute and NavHybridChunkRoute tests; load-order independence everywhere). Pending the runtime wave: the agent-driven routing tests (moving agents, steering, groups). **W5 done**: `nav-crowd` (the runtime): a `Crowd` with agents in a generational slot table (hot per-frame fields as arrays, buffers per agent), WASM-ready boundary buffers (agents in stride 10, out stride 17, state bytes, obstacles; an epoch for view rebuilds) and a command queue, chunk load/unload driving the snapshot, seams, overlays (built synchronously for now) and route graphs, and Foxfire's per-frame order: gates, agent chunks, surround, routes (hybrid legs, chunk and flight chunk routes), flow fields and flow agents, walkers (repath, pooled sliced path requests with congestion, follow, move along the surface, folds and jumps, easing), flyers (repath, pooled flight searches, steering, move), neighbour grids, ORCA avoidance with layers, priorities and make-way, flight separation, groups (formations, painted masks, pace, swap, squeeze, walk-fly members, merge) and the member move, warnings. New: `stop`, `warp`. Tests: 164 in `nav-crowd` (Foxfire's formation (31), surround-slot (17), easing, congestion and neighbour-grid tests; the agent-moving chunk-route and hybrid-chunk-route cases; a full-tick suite incl. determinism of the boundary bytes). Benchmark (`examples/crowd_bench.rs`, native release): 1,000 walkers with avoidance 2.8 ms/tick, 5,000 20.7 ms, 500 flyers 4.6 ms. Known: an avoiding walker can cycle at an eroded wall corner (follows Foxfire's code; check in Unity); flow following runs as its own pass before repath. **W6 done**: the WASM boundary (`nav-wasm`: `NavRuntime` with zero-copy agent buffers, commands, tick, queries and debug geometry; `NavBaker`, incremental per chunk; `bakeAgentGroups` and debug geometry straight from bake bytes) and its TS wrappers (`src/plugins/navigation/runtime/runtime.ts`, `src/engine/nav/bake.ts`, `settings.ts`; `CONTRACT.md` records the component contract). Engine: `ProjectSettings.navigation`; the components (`NavChunk`, `NavSurface`, `NavObstacle`, `NavModifierVolume`, `NavResolutionVolume`, `NavGate`, `NavAgent`, `NavGroup`, `NavSurround`, `NavFlowGoal`, `NavAvoidanceObstacle`); the Navigation plugin (ADR 0135; components, `Navigation`, `NavigationWorld` and the systems in `src/plugins/navigation/runtime/`): `NavigationWorld` (`navigationOf(world)`), the lazy `myco.navigation` World service, whose `prepareScene` preloads the scene's bake so its chunks load at the first step; the built-in fixedUpdate systems `engine.NavSync` → `engine.NavStep` → `engine.NavWriteBack`, before the physics pushes (kinematic bodies follow through physics), in authoritative Worlds only (`default`, `local`, `server`; ghosts skipped); the static `Navigation` facade (`findPath`, `nearestPoint`, `isReachable`, `raycast`, `setAreaCost`, `debug`). One-shot queries respect gate states. Editor: a Navigation section in the Project panel (agent types with a voxel preview, areas, advanced settings, Bake All); the NavChunk Inspector's Bake/Rebake in a worker (`navBake.worker.ts`), last-bake summary and a stale notice (records in `.myco/nav/`, per machine); geometry collection (`src/engine/nav/geometry.ts`, shared with the CLI); `EditorProject.writeBinary` and `.bin` assets; `npm run nav:bake -- <projectDir> [scene…]`. Debug drawing: composable editor overlays (`addEditorOverlay`/`removeEditorOverlay`, bulk `segments`), the scene view's Nav popover (navmesh, links, flight leaves from the bake; landing links, seams and agent paths while playing), a navigation counters panel under the play-mode Worlds tab; runtime Worlds draw overlays too. The engine library ships `myco_nav_bg.wasm` beside its chunks (library mode would inline it). Tests: 13 engine (`navSystems.test.ts`), 12 boundary (`navRuntime.test.ts`), 13 debug-drawing, 19 bake/settings/CLI; e2e `navbake.spec.ts`, `navdebug.spec.ts`. Known: the counters panel shows what `tickStats` reports (no search/slice/timing counters yet); WASM bake stage timings are 0 (no clock); a scene with several NavChunks writes `<scene>.<id>.nav.bin` for the extra chunks; chunks inside prefab instances bake with the outer scene's path. **W7 sample**: `samples/navigation`, Foxfire's Streaming Demo ported by a generator (`tools/generate.mjs`: the five agent types and three areas with fixed ids, nine 128 m chunk scenes with their bakes, the main scene, prefabs, an OBJ terrain for the south-west chunk) and scripts that stream the chunk scenes additively around the agents, send about 120 agents (crowds, four groups incl. a painted arrow, jump and climb courses) to seeded random spots round a touring goal, time the gate, orbit the camera and show a HUD. Tests: `engine/tests/navSample.test.ts` (75 s headless: every kind moves, crosses seams and arrives; chunks stream in and out; only rate-limited path warnings); e2e `navsample.spec.ts`. **W7 done**: benchmarks (`bench/nav.bench.ts`: bake 73 ms cold / 58 ms incremental for a 64 m chunk; `tick` 1.5 ms for 500 walkers, 6.1 ms for 2,000, 1.4 ms for 300 flyers; the TS sync adds ~0.2 ms; `findPath` ~0.01 ms) and the guide (`docs/wiki/guide/navigation.md`). Review fixes: a chunk shared by two scene instances loads once; the bake cache keeps only the latest bytes per asset; the seam report names only near misses (within 1 m) and neighbours with no agent type in common, with full chunk ids (it logged every non-touching pair, hundreds of lines per load). Integration review fixes: bulk overlay segments stay on the GPU until they change; incremental bakers are capped (8 most recent) and released on project switch; the Engine reports navigation settings problems; parented agents are written back only when their pose changed; Worlds that don't run the navigation systems (clients, editor) load no runtime or bakes; one-shot queries see gate states live (pending NavGate changes and SetGate commands included); a failed bake worker hands its pending bakes to the main thread. **Foxfire's custom editors**: formation assets (`*.formation.json`, `myco.formation@1`, `src/engine/nav/formation.ts`: NavFormation's fields, Resize keeping cells, max 128) with `NavGroup.formationAsset` (overrides `formation`/`layers`/`layerSpacing`; `NavigationWorld` sends its mask), New Navigation Formation in the Assets menu and a formation editor in the Inspector (paint/erase, size, Clear/Fill/Invert/Mirror, import from an image, fill-order numbers, its own undo); the NavGroup Inspector previews the asset (Edit / Create formation asset). NavAgent warns when no chunk bakes its type; NavChunk shows what it bakes per agent type (navmeshes and flight volumes and who shares them, from the bake's own grouping through `nav-wasm`'s `bakePlan`), Walkable For warnings and a Surface bins toggle (bin layout summary; the scene view outlines walkable triangles by bin, from `classifySurfaceBins`, with each bin's normal and cone); NavResolutionVolume lists each flyer's region cell size; edit-mode previews of the selected resolution volume's refined flight leaves and an air modifier volume's area leaves (`debugBakeFlightLeaves`, `src/plugins/navigation/editor/navPreviews.ts`). Tests: `engine/tests/navFormation.test.ts`, `nav-wasm` `plan.rs` tests; e2e `navformation.spec.ts` (not yet run). The Surface bins globe is drawn as lines (no shaded sphere). Follow-ups ([to-do](../plans/2026-11-navigation-followups.md)): flight volumes are large on disk (1.1–2.2 MB per 128 m chunk, ~10× smaller gzipped; a sparser layout or compression would help); group members don't get cross-chunk flight routes (as in Foxfire); a merged group's body stays "following" without moving; walk-and-fly agents and flyers log rate-limited path warnings at chunk edges (as the Unity demo does); removing a NavGate leaves its gate as last set; agent type and area dropdowns show ids (choices can't carry labels yet). Unverified: Unity's xxHash3 against more than the empty-input vector. |

The surface-bins preview waits for models to load, as the bake does. |
| [0067](../decisions/0067-runtime-listen-and-path-routing.md) Runtime listen, one port by path | Done | **`WorldHandle.listen(transport, options?)`** (`core/WorldHandle.ts`, `core/worlds.ts`): makes a World a server at runtime and resolves with the address clients connect to (rejects with the reason; throws for a client World or a second listen at the same address); **`addresses()`** lists where it listens. Options are a link's (`port`, `host`, `path`, `address`, `maxConnections`, timeouts; `listenAddress` in `net/session.ts`). **One port, many paths** (`src/server/websocketServer.ts`): a process-wide table of `node:http` servers by `host:port`; each `NodeWebSocketTransport` joins its port at its path (`ws` in `noServer` mode), upgrades are routed by URL path (query ignored), an unknown path gets HTTP 404, a lone listener at `/` still takes any path (the old behavior), the same path twice is refused, port 0 opens a new server that later listeners share by its real port, and the HTTP server closes with its last listener. `parseListenAddress` takes `7777/match/3`. **Loopback**: a `path` makes the address unique (`engine-1/match/3`). **Dedicated server** (`src/server/run.ts`): `setDefaultListenOptions("websocket", …)` makes the first server World's real port (and `--host`) the default for every listen without a port; Worlds created at runtime are logged when they start listening and when they're destroyed (the "clients can't connect to it" line is gone); a path clash maps to the "port in use" error. Browsers: `listen("websocket")` rejects (the client-only transport). Tests: `engine/tests/runtimeListen.test.ts` (real sockets, raw HTTP 404/101, port release), `server/lobbyServer.test.ts`. Resolves O15. |
| [0068](../decisions/0068-lobbies-and-matchmaking.md) Lobbies and matchmaking | Done | **Lobby server** (`net/lobby/server.ts`): `"lobby"` on a World config or template (validated by `lobbyOptionProblems` from `core/settings.ts`; `WORLD_KEYS` has `lobby`) is hosted on the World's first frame, or `Lobby.host(handle, options)`. Rooms (id, 5-letter code, name, public, quick, host passed on, slots, ready flags, settings from allowed choices), ready-up countdown (cancelled by unready/leave/join), host-only `setSetting`/`kick`/`start` (once `minPlayers`), quick match (fullest waiting public room with matching settings, else a new one that starts after `quickMatchWait`), refusals with reasons. Starting creates `Match <id>` from `matchTemplate`, makes it a match World and has it `listen` over the lobby's own transport at `/match/<id>` on the lobby's real port and host; members get the address, path, a one-time ticket and `clientTemplate`; when the match ends the lobby destroys it, sends results and readies the room. **Match** (`net/lobby/match.ts`): `Match.current` (`id`, `roomId`, `settings`, `players` with `slot`/`name`/`bot`/`connectionId`, `end(results)`); the session's handshake carries a ticket (`hello`, protocol 3) and `NetSession.admit` refuses connections without an unused ticket for that match; ends on its own after `joinTimeout` (default 30 s, an added option) with nobody joined, when every joined human has left, or when its lobby World is gone. **Client** (`net/lobby/client.ts`): static page-wide `Lobby` over one private `LobbyClient`: a hidden World tagged `lobby-client` (no rendering, input or game systems); `"local"` makes (or finds) the project's lobby World and listens over loopback; `state`/`error`/`rooms`/`room`/`me`/`matchWorld` are polled data; actions as in the ADR; a match makes `Match <id> Client` from `clientTemplate` and connects with the ticket (over a URL, the lobby's URL with the match's path, so `wss://` and proxies carry over), pauses the page's other rendering Worlds and resumes them when the match ends, the player leaves or the connection drops. One built-in, `engine.Lobby` (update, after `engine.NetReceive`), drives all three. **Strings**: `str` RPC fields (`net/rpc.ts`, `net/messages.ts`; UTF-8, 255 bytes; `defineComponent` and `defineInput` throw for it); `@field(String, { replicate: true })` behavior fields replicate whole when they change, on a reliable `net.ghostStrings` message (`net/replication/strings.ts`). Tests: `engine/tests/lobby.test.ts` (one player over loopback: bots, tickets, timeouts; several players, each a page of its own that imports the engine afresh, over WebSocket: quick match, rooms, refusals, view hand-over), `engine/tests/runtimeListen.test.ts` (strings), `server/lobbyServer.test.ts`. The match World takes the screen through `Engine._handView(name)` (internal, `core/Engine.ts`): the Engine's main view and local input pass to it, its surface goes in the Engine's container where the main canvas was (so it's laid out the same, in the editor's game view and the player, which the exported build uses), the World that had the view keeps its surface hidden and isn't drawn, and the returned function hands the view back (also when the match World is destroyed; hand-overs nest). `Engine._viewStates` reports `viewHanded` for the World that gave the view up. It replaced an overlay drawn with `Engine._attachView`, which wasn't centred like the main canvas (the match sat at the left of the game view with the paused menu showing beside it), and lost input to the paused menu when the editor's game view refocused the main view on a click. Checked in a browser with `samples/pirates` in the editor at 1400×850 and 2000×780 (Play, Practice, the match full size with keys and mouse steering working after a click on the game view, leaving it, back to the room). The editor's game view doesn't tile a World that already draws to a surface of its own or that handed its view away (`src/editor/gameView.ts`), and multiplayer play leaves a lobby's layout as it is (`hostLayout` in `src/editor/netPlay.ts`). The dedicated server runs only the lobby template when server templates include one, not its match templates (`serverBootstrap` in `src/server/run.ts`). Tests for the hand-over: `engine/tests/handView.test.ts` (fake surfaces: view, input, hidden surface, hand back, destroyed Worlds, editor tiles, nesting, headless), the view states in `engine/tests/lobby.test.ts`, and `tests/e2e/lobbyview.spec.ts` (King of Pirates in the editor, Chromium only). **Gaps:** the player page's hand-over is checked only through the shared Engine code, not in a browser; one lobby process (as the ADR says). A quick room starts on its own only before its first match; when that ends it's an ordinary room (it used to start another match at once, since its wait had long run out, so players never got back to the room). The Worlds panel's graph draws the connections a lobby makes at runtime as dotted arrows (`lobbyLinks` in `editor/worldsConfig.ts`): start-up Worlds that render join the lobby, the lobby creates `matchTemplate` Worlds, `clientTemplate` Worlds join them; pairs a declared link covers are skipped. |
| [0069](../decisions/0069-ui-components-on-uikit.md) UI components on uikit | Partial (layout, drawing and routing moved to 0076; authoring by hand-placed nodes superseded by [0421](../decisions/0421-game-ui-is-built-only-from-ui-documents.md)) | **0421:** the components stay as what UI documents' elements are made of, but only a `UIDocument` creates them; `UIText` gained `textOutlineColor`/`textOutlineWidth`. **Dependency**: none of its own now: `@pmndrs/uikit` and `ui/UIRuntime.ts` went with the three.js backend (0081); layout, drawing and routing are 0076's (`ui/UICore.ts`, `render/babylon/ui.ts`). **Components** (`ui/components.ts`, plain data in any World): `UICanvas` (`mode` screen/world, `referenceResolution`, `matchHeight` (default 1) blended in log space like Unity's Canvas Scaler, `sortOrder`, `pixelSize`), `UIPanel` (Yoga layout fields: `flexDirection`, `justifyContent`, `alignItems`, `gap`, `padding`, `margin`, `width`/`height` (pixels, `"50%"`, `"auto"`), `positionType` with `top/right/bottom/left`, `flexGrow`, `display` (`flex`/`none`: none takes the element and its subtree out of layout, drawing and routing while its node stays active); style: `backgroundColor`, `borderRadius`, `borderWidth`, `borderColor`, `opacity`, `visible` (hides the subtree too)), `UIText`, `UIImage` (`image`, `fit`), `UIButton` (`hoverColor`, `pressedColor`, `interactable`; `hovered`, `pressed`, `clicked`), `UIInputField` (`value`, `placeholder`, `maxLength`, `fontSize`, `color`, `placeholderColor`; `focused` (settable), `submitted`). The node tree is the UI tree; nodes without a UI component are skipped, children of a text, image or field lay out beside it, elements outside a canvas aren't drawn (warned). **Rendering** (0076): screen canvases draw after the game's camera and post-processing, on one fullscreen Babylon GUI texture in `sortOrder`, never tone mapped; world canvases are planes at the node's transform. **Routing** (`input/Input.ts`): `Input.beginFrame` runs the UI core's routing step (the device snapshot's `addRouter`, 0310) before any World steps; it hit-tests the pointer against last frame's layout (screen canvases from the highest `sortOrder`, topmost sibling first, then the nearest world canvas through the view camera) and claims it over a `UIButton` (interactable or not) or `UIInputField`. Then `Input.pointerOverUI` is true, that frame's presses go to the UI (`getMouseButtonDown`/`getKeyDown("mouse0")` false), and a press that began on UI stays the UI's (`getMouseButton`, `getMouseButtonUp` false) until released. A click is a press and release on the same button; scripts see `clicked` in the next World frame. A click on a field focuses it (a click elsewhere unfocuses); the core's hidden DOM `<input>` takes typing, Enter sets `submitted`, Escape unfocuses, and while a field is focused `Input.keyboardOverUI` is true and `getKey*`/`anyKeyDown` report false for keys. Only a World that takes input routes; the editor's own World draws UI but doesn't route. **Editor**: the components in the Inspector (generic fields) and the Add Component menu's UI category; Create menu UI Canvas, UI Panel, UI Text, UI Image, UI Button, UI Input Field (flat entries). Guide: [UI](../wiki/guide/ui.md). Tests: `engine/tests/ui.test.ts` (components and `Input` without UI; layout, routing, focus and world canvases are `uiCore.test.ts`'s, 0076), `editor/uiCreate.test.ts`. Missing: a panel that blocks the pointer without being a button, keyboard and gamepad navigation, scrolling, a Playwright test that draws it in a browser. Built since: `fit: contain` in a box with both sides fixed (Babylon's uniform stretch, `render/babylon/ui.ts`; the `UIImage.fit` doc comment still describes uikit's limit), custom fonts (`UIText.font`, 0076), picking UI in the editor's scene view (0077). |
| [0070](../decisions/0070-runtime-navigation-cost-volumes.md) Runtime navigation cost volumes | Done | **Rust** (`native/nav/`, WASM rebuilt): `nav-query::costs` (`CostVolume`: an affine world-to-local box, multiplier, `excluded`, agent types, ground/air; per-polygon and per-leaf tables by centre, volumes multiply, `EXCLUDED` = infinity wins); `NavMeshQueryFilter` gained `poly_costs` / `min_poly_cost` (in `travel_multiplier`, so walking A*, routing, hybrid and flow fields price it; `poly_usable` skips excluded polygons; `cheapest_travel` and A*'s heuristic scale by the cheapest multiplier below 1); flight searches take `LeafCosts` (`volume::begin_with`/`step_with`/`finish_with`/`find_path_with`, `hybrid::find_route_with`, the route search's air expansions through `RouteGraphs::leaf_costs_for`; smoothing never cuts through costlier or excluded leaves). `NavChunks::apply_cost_volumes` fills the snapshot's tables (per entry, compared with the previous snapshot: `last_cost_change`, the changed polygons); the crowd's `ChunkWorld` recomputes them when the host's volumes or the snapshot change (`ChunkUpdate::costs_changed`, `cost_version`), refreshes route graphs (`refresh_costs`), within-chunk graphs and flow-field signatures. Re-plans: walkers whose corridor ahead crosses a changed polygon (urgent that tick); chunk routes, walk-fly routes and fly-only routes whose remaining legs cross a changed chunk; flyers whose volume's leaves changed. `Crowd::set_cost_volumes` (host state, not a command, so one-shot queries use the volumes as they are: `nav-wasm` queries compute live tables). WASM: `setCostVolumes(json)`, `debugCosts(chunk, type)`. **Engine**: `NavCostVolume` (`center`, `size`, `cost` 4, `excluded`, `agents`, `domain`), synced each NavSync and before facade queries when the set changes (JSON compare; node's inverse world matrix); `Navigation.debug.costs`, `costSegments` and the runtime overlay (costlier / cheaper / excluded colours). **Editor**: Inspector note (`describeNavCostVolume`: what it does, warnings for unknown types and types the domain can't reach); the Nav popover's Cost volumes toggle while playing. No scene-view box gizmo (the other nav volumes have none either). Tests: `nav-query` `costs.rs` (3) and `tests/cost_volumes.rs` (4: walking round costly/excluded polygons, cheap multipliers and the heuristic, flight round costly/excluded leaves, snapshot and route-graph tables), `nav-crowd` `tests/cost_volumes.rs` (5: re-plan, exclusion, other types and off-corridor volumes re-plan nobody, idempotent sets, chunks loaded later), `nav-wasm` DTO test; `engine/tests/navCostVolume.test.ts` (5). Known: accuracy is per polygon/leaf centre (as the record says); a search already running when costs change continues with the new table; a walker mid-fold or mid-jump when costs change doesn't re-plan for it. |
| [0072](../decisions/0072-water-surfaces-and-buoyancy.md) Water surfaces and buoyancy | Done | **Maths** (`render/water/gerstner.ts`; the deep-water speed in `dispersion.ts`): `packWaves` folds `waveScale` and the water clock into per-wave constants (direction, wave number, amplitude, phase wrapped in doubles); `GERSTNER_SHADER` is the wave sum written once in KSL (ADR 0250), compiled into the water plugin's WGSL and GLSL (`render/babylon/water.ts`) and, in tests, run as JavaScript checked equal to the CPU twin `gerstner` at random points, wave sets and times; `sampleWaves` undoes the waves' sideways motion (Newton steps on the horizontal map with the analytic Jacobian, the plain fixed-point step where crests fold). **`WaterSurface`** (`render/water/WaterSurface.ts`, engine-native): `size`, `resolution` (halved on tiers whose shader level is "low"), up to 8 `waves` (an inspector list of objects; default the four-wave preset), `waveScale`, `deepColor`/`shallowColor`/`foamColor`, `foamAmount`, `normalDetail`, `roughness`, `opacity`, `reflectivity`; `sample(x, z)` → `{ height, normal }` in world space through the node's transform; `Water.sample`/`Water.surface`/`Water.time` use the World's first enabled surface. **Clock** (`waterTime`): the World's scaled time, or `(serverTick + fixedAlpha) / tickRate` in a networked World. **Drawing** (`render/babylon/water.ts`, `BabylonWater`; `MycoWaterPlugin` splices into a `PBRMaterial`, WGSL on WebGPU and GLSL on the WebGL2 fallback): a grid per surface with displaced vertices and analytic normals, deep-to-shallow colour by view angle and height, Jacobian crest foam (rougher where foamy), two scrolling value-noise ripple layers; lights, received shadows, Fresnel reflections of the environment and of the reflection probe whose box holds the surface's centre (0053/0054), and fog are Babylon's PBR shading, so fog (0071) applies. Opaque unless `opacity` < 1. **`Buoyancy`** (`physics/Buoyancy.ts`): `mode` physics/kinematic, `points` (default the corners of `size`), `strength`, `drag`, `angularDrag`, `follow`, read-only `submerged`; built-in system `engine.Buoyancy` (fixed step, after scripts, before `engine.Physics3DPush`). Physics: per submerged point an upward force depth × strength × mass × g / points, at the point (torque about the body origin, its centre of mass), velocities damped while submerged. Kinematic: height to the mean water height at the points, pitch and roll to the least-squares plane in the heading frame, eased by `follow`. Headless, so servers float objects the same. **Editor**: Create > Water (the four-wave preset written into the scene); Add Component lists WaterSurface under Rendering and Buoyancy under Physics 3D. Tests: `engine/tests/water.test.ts`, `engine/tests/babylonWater.test.ts` (grid, material, WGSL and GLSL against the CPU twin), `editor/waterCreate.test.ts`. User guide: [Water](../wiki/guide/water.md). Missing: no browser (Playwright) test of the shader (the GPU check page is `spikes/babylon/stage6.html`); sampling assumes a level water node; water doesn't cast shadows or take part in editor picking; the Create menu has no 3D submenu, so Water sits in the flat list; `waveScale` isn't replicated by itself (a game's replicated field drives it). |
| [0040](../decisions/0040-asset-import-sidecars.md) Import settings sidecars | Partial | **Format** (`scene/importSettings.ts`): `<asset>.meta.json`, `"format": "myco.import@1"`; images: `pixelsPerUnit`, `filter` (linear/point), `wrap` (clamp/repeat/mirror), `mipmaps`, `pivot`, `sprites` (named `rect` [x, y, w, h] from the top-left, `pivot` 0..1 from the bottom-left, optional 9-slice `border`); audio: `volume`, `stream`; models: `scale`, `upAxis`, `normals` (0044), `materials` by material name (0089). Parsed leniently (bad values warn and default; another format is refused) and written with only non-default values, one slice per line. **Engine**: `Assets.importSettings(path)` reads the sidecar through the asset sources (defaults without one; parsed again when the editor rewrites it). The render backend sets texture filtering, wrapping and mipmaps from it (again when the sidecar changes) and sizes sprites by the SpriteRenderer's, then the sidecar's, then the project's pixels per unit (`render/spriteFrame.ts`). Slice references `asset:<image>#<slice>` draw the slice's UVs on the sprite's own quad, sized by pixels per unit, with the node origin on the pivot (whole images too); a missing slice draws the whole image with one warning; scene preloading strips `#slice`. AudioSource and `Audio.playOneShot` multiply by the clip's `volume`, and `stream` plays through HTML5 audio. The server's `readProject` and the samples manifest carry `*.meta.json` as JSON (tested). **Atlases**: no packed atlas asset; a sheet with slices is one texture, sliced by grid or automatically (the ADR says why). **Editor**: the Asset Inspector (`ui/AssetInspector.tsx`) shows for one selected image or audio file (or slice) when no node is selected (a plain click on one clears the node selection); Apply writes the sidecar, Revert drops the draft. The Sprite Editor (`ui/SpriteEditor.tsx`, `spriteSlicing.ts`; from the Asset Inspector, a double-click or the right-click menu): draw, move and resize slices; grid by cell count or size (offset, padding, skip empty cells); automatic slicing (islands of opaque pixels, overlaps merged, minimum size); name, rectangle, pivot presets and border per slice; Apply and Revert; asks before discarding. Images with slices get a toggle in the Assets panel listing `<image>#<name>` sprite entries (SVG-cropped thumbnails) that drag or + into the scene as sprite nodes. Sidecars are hidden in the Assets panel; rename, move, duplicate, copy/paste and delete carry them (`EditorProject.move`/`copyFile`/`deleteFile`; a stray sidecar at the destination is replaced). Slice references follow renames and moves, slice renames in the Sprite Editor update references, and deleting an image lists and removes slice references. **ADR 0115**: a slice with a `border` draws 9-sliced (corners fixed in pixels, shrunk together if too wide; 16-vertex sprite grid, `spriteMeshData`); the Sprite Editor has draggable border guides, undo/redo (`spriteHistory.ts`, Apply resets it) and the Assets search lists matching slices; sidecars take `maxSize`, `compression` (none/low/normal/high) and `platforms: { web, desktop }` overrides of filter/wrap/mipmaps/maxSize/compression, resolved for `Assets.platform` at load and shown in the Asset Inspector. `maxSize` and `compression` are applied by game exports (0119): `src/export/images.ts` (pure planner, tested with a fake codec), `imagesNode.ts` (sharp) and `imagesBrowser.ts` (canvas, editor Export…) resize and re-encode (WebP, lossless for point-filtered) per the build platform and add `sourceSize` to the build's sidecar so sprites and tiles keep their authored size (`authoredSize`, used by the Babylon sprite and tilemap paths). Tests: `export/images.test.ts`, `importPlatforms.test.ts`. Verified by hand: exported Flappy with a 128 px, `normal`-compressed 500 px bird (76 KB → 2 KB) renders at the same size in the browser. Not verified: the editor's canvas encoder in a real export; WebP encode in Tauri's WebView2. Per-slice tiled fill (`fill: tile`, cap 1024 quads; Sprite Editor Fill dropdown). `maxSize`/`compression` are now applied at export as WebP (0119); KTX2 stays deferred (the 0115 investigation). Missing: sprite animation over slices, GPU-memory savings (KTX2), more platforms. |
| [0023](../decisions/0023-testing-vitest-and-browser-smoke-tests.md) Testing | Partial | Vitest on the headless core (lifecycle, transforms, scenes/prefabs, systems, Rapier physics). Playwright smoke tests (`tests/e2e/`, `npm run test:e2e`; `npm run test:e2e:install` fetches the browsers) run the production build on Chromium and WebKit. Locally on Windows, Chromium renders on the GPU (WebGPU through Dawn on Direct3D 12, compiling with FXC via `--disable-dawn-features=use_dxc` because Playwright's headless shell lacks `dxcompiler.dll`; ANGLE Direct3D 11 for WebGL) and runs on 8 workers: about 3.8 minutes for 216 tests, against 16.9 with software GL on 4 workers (measured 2026-11-08). `npm run test:e2e` is the everyday run: Chromium, without the specs tagged `@slow` (exports, thumbnails, long play sessions: `npm run test:e2e:slow`); WebKit runs on demand (`npm run test:e2e:webkit`), `npm run test:e2e:all` runs everything ([tests/e2e/README.md](../../tests/e2e/README.md)). With software GL (CI, `E2E_SOFTWARE_GL=1`, machines without a GPU) the suite is CPU-bound, so specs import `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts`, which caps render loops (10 fps in Play and outside the editor, 5 while editing), Chromium renders at device scale 0.5, the Code panel's background warm-up is off (`codePreload` stores the Settings > General > Code setting as off), traces are recorded only on CI's retry, and the server serves the build without compression: in a 4-core cloud container the default run went from 29.5 minutes (91 of 216 failing, mostly timeouts) to 9.5 (2026-11-09), `@slow` takes 2.0. The web server (`scripts/e2e-server.mjs`) rebuilds the editor only when its sources changed since the last e2e build (`E2E_REBUILD=1` forces it), then serves `dist/editor/` itself. They run the editor build, which opens the Flappy sample (`editor.html?sample=flappy`), and `sample.spec.ts` plays it through Play mode: it renders a non-blank frame and goes ready → playing → dead, read from the sample's GameManager through the engine module scripts share. **Not yet:** no GitHub Actions workflow runs the tests (the only workflow is CodeQL, `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml`), which the ADR asks for; golden-image screenshot comparison (its open question). |
| [0024](../decisions/0024-storage-via-platform-adapter.md) Storage | Done | Since 0317 the backends are record adapters behind `storageScope.ts` (see its row); before: `Storage` API with three backends, picked by `Engine.create`: `TauriStorage` under Tauri (one file per key at `<appDataDir>/<namespace>/<key>.json`, or `.bin` for binary values, written to a temp file and renamed), IndexedDB in a browser, and memory when headless. Cloud saves remain an open question. |
| [0037](../decisions/0037-rename-to-myco-engine.md) Rename to Myco Engine | Done | Package `myco-engine`, crate `myco` (`myco_lib`), `myco-server` bin, Tauri identifier `com.foxfirestudios.myco.editor`, art in `branding/`; scripts import `"myco"`, with `"tranquility"` still accepted (`tsconfig.json` paths, `LEGACY_ENGINE_MODULE` in `editor/scriptLoader.ts`, reused by `export/webBuild.ts`). Files are written as `myco.scene@N`, `myco.history@1`, `myco.history-slot@1` and kept in `.myco/`; `tranquility.scene@N` (`scene/SceneLoader.ts`), the old history slot format (`historyStore.ts`) and the old `tranquility.` preference and layout keys (`preferences.ts`, `layout/store.ts`) are still read. Not checked: the dedicated server's `"tranquility"` import alias. |
| [0039](../decisions/0039-polygon-edge-colliders.md) Polygon and edge colliders | Done | `PolygonCollider2D` (closed outline) and `EdgeCollider2D` (open polyline), `points` as `[x, y]` pairs (`physics/Collider2D.ts`, `@field(PlainObject)`). `physics/polygon.ts` (no Rapier): a convex outline is one hull, a concave one is split into convex parts (ear clipping, then merging) built as one compound shape; a self-crossing outline collides along its closed polyline. Changing `points` rebuilds the shape. **Editor**: Add Component (Physics 2D); Edit Collider shows vertex handles (drag, snapping with Ctrl or "always snap"), an edge-middle handle that adds a vertex, Ctrl+click removes one (down to 3 for a polygon, 2 for an edge), one undo step each (`colliderGizmo.ts`). Tests: `engine/tests/polygonCollider.test.ts`, `plugins/physics/editor/colliderGizmo.test.ts`. The Inspector doesn't edit `points` (not checked: whether it shows them read-only). Also described in the 0005 row. |
| [0041](../decisions/0041-game-builds.md) Game builds | Done | **Player** (`player.html`, `src/player/main.ts`, `vite.player.config.ts` → `dist/player/`, run by `npm run build:player`, which `build:editor` runs first): a project-independent page with the engine as a fixed-name entry, `myco.js` (`preserveEntrySignatures: "strict"`), shared with the compiled scripts; `files.json` lists the output. It reads `game.json`, fetches JSON assets, gives binaries their URLs, imports the compiled scripts, builds the Engine from the project's bootstrap full-window (letterboxed at `display`, page in `backgroundColor`), and loads the main scene into start-up Worlds without scenes of their own; a code bootstrap loads from its compiled module. Storage is the default (IndexedDB; the app data folder under Tauri). A `file://` page explains how to serve the build. **Planner** (`src/export/webBuild.ts`, layout in `format.ts`): file selection (`isExportedFile`: no hidden/build folders, TypeScript, root `tsconfig.json`/`sample.json`; sidecars go in), scripts compiled with the editor's `compileScript` (`sourceMap: false`) and `loadOrder`, imports rewritten to relative files (`myco.js`, compiled scripts, `code/<json>.js` modules), every script problem listed; manifest `{ format, name, mainScene, files, scripts }`; `serve.mjs` and README.txt; models are baked to `.mycomesh` beside each model (`withBakedModels`, 0090). **CLI** `npm run export -- <projectDir> [--out] [--target web\|desktop] [--scene] [--build\|--no-build]` (`src/export/cli.mjs`, `main.ts`, `node.ts`, through the server's `tsLoader.mjs` and `readProject`, which now lists `files`): output new, empty or an earlier build; refuses folders inside the project outside `dist/`; builds the player if missing. **Desktop** (`desktop.ts`): `<out>/web/` plus a generated Tauri project in `<out>/src-tauri/` (product name, window size, identifier and version from `project.json`/`build`, fs plugin with app-data-only capability, icons by `tauri icon` from `build.icon` or a neutral SVG); `tauri build` runs when cargo is installed, `src-tauri/target/` survives re-exports. **Editor**: File > Build and Export… opens Build / Export (`ui/ExportDialog.tsx`, `exportBuild.ts`): writes the web build through the platform FileSystem into a picked folder (needs `canWrite`; a read-only browser editor explains), with the player fetched from `player/` beside the editor (`playerFiles()` plugin; the dev server serves `dist/player/` once built); the desktop option builds the app with the host's toolchain in the desktop editor (ADR 0381) and shows the CLI command in a browser. | [0071](../decisions/0071-fog.md) Fog | Done | `Fog` component (`render/components.ts`: `mode` linear/exponential, `color`, `near`, `far`, `density`, `affectsSkybox`, `skyboxFog`); the first enabled one in a World applies. `BabylonRenderer.syncFog` mirrors it onto the Babylon scene's fog (linear or exponential squared, the colour converted to linear space), in place each frame; with `affectsSkybox`, a flat background lerps to the fog colour by `skyboxFog`, and a skybox dims by that fraction and becomes the plain fog colour at 1 (the fog's `skyFog` contribution to the lighting rig, ADR 0252; `render/babylon/sky/BabylonSky.ts`). A material asset opts out with `"fog": false` (`scene/material.ts`, the draw key's `applyFog`); editor overlays set `fogEnabled = false`. The scene view shows fog as the game does, with or without a composer (`tests/e2e/postprocessing.spec.ts`, King of Pirates on Medium). Create menu: Fog. The Material inspector has a Fog toggle (2026-11-07). **Height fog (0110, 2026-11-07):** `heightFog`, `heightFogDensity`, `heightFogBase`, `heightFogFalloff`, and `mode: "none"` for height fog alone; exponential height fog integrated along the view ray, in a plugin registered for every PBR material (`render/babylon/heightFog.ts`; compiled in only while a World has it, for meshes that take fog). Tests: `heightFog.test.ts`; checked in the browser (`spikes/babylon/rendering.html?case=probes&fog`). `fog.test.ts` went with the three.js backend (0081): nothing tests distance fog on Babylon.
