| [0157](../decisions/0157-audio-runs-on-an-engine-owned-rust-core-in-an-audioworklet.md) Engine-owned audio core | Partial (M1 done) | Accepted 2026-11-09; M1 declared done by the owner 2026-11-10. Design: `docs/design/0157-audio-core/`; plan [2026-11-audio-core-m1.md](../plans/2026-11-audio-core-m1.md); log `docs/design/0157-audio-core/build-log.md`; owner decisions `owner-review.md`; contributor note `native/audio/README.md`. **M1 (MS0–MS8):** audio is the `myco.audio` plugin (`src/plugins/audio/`) on the Rust core (`native/audio/`: `audio-core`, `audio-dsp`, `audio-wire`, `audio-decode`, `audio-bench`; ABI 1.3) in an AudioWorklet, the default `audio.backend` (`DEFAULT_BACKEND` in `engine/audio/settings.ts`), with Babylon's AudioEngineV2 as the fallback (macOS desktop until its gate; engines without AudioWorklet or `simd128`). Voices (4,096 logical, a mixed cap that drops only on underruns or late commands), selection and stealing, priority, `maxVoices`, the sample-accurate clock and `at`, `AudioHandle`, `Audio.status`/`time`/`debug()`; the bus tree with Blink biquad, compressor and convolver-reverb ports, ducking, snapshots, meters and a look-ahead master limiter; a 4-point resampler; Chromium HRTF for the nearest spatial voices (IRCAM HRIR); symphonia decoding and streaming on a worker, seek, gapless loops, AAC opt-in; trap, device and context rebuilds; background modes. Editor: Project Settings > Audio, the Audio Debug panel. Samples: `sound-lab`, `stress` (soak mode). Tests: `cargo test` in `native/audio/`, wasm goldens (`audioGolden.test.ts`), the wire round trip, Web Audio and HRTF parity (`audio-parity.spec.ts`, `webAudioParity.test.ts`), the quality harness, `@slow` specs (`audio-core`, `audio-babylon`, `audio-stress`, sound-lab), and the metered +10% instruction gate (`npm run check:audio-metered`). **Not verified at M1 close:** the 30-minute soak on Windows Chromium, Linux WebKitGTK and the low-end Android phone (only Windows WebView2 passed); the 1.3 ms p99.9 budget on the reference machine and the "low" preset; the signed listening pass; WebKit/Firefox parity and macOS. **Missing:** M2 (events and parameters, record 0158), M3 (ambisonic bed, occlusion, reverb zones, Doppler), M4 (interactive music), M5 (profiler, live mix; ADR 0171 accepted, design next); band-limiting on pitch-up (#110); true-peak detection; a public seek API. 0006/0074/0097/0098 describe the running code only where 0157 hasn't replaced it. |
| [0158](../decisions/0158-audio-events-and-parameters.md) Audio events and parameters (0157's M2) | Done (owner declared M2 complete 2026-11-10; the lab-VM run and listening-pass sign-off carried as owner items) | Accepted 2026-11-10. Handoff: [2026-11-audio-events-m2-handoff.md](../plans/2026-11-audio-events-m2-handoff.md). Build plan: [waves](../plans/2026-11-audio-events-m2-waves.md) and [task tree](../plans/2026-11-audio-events-m2-tree.md) (E3.1–E7.2). Plan [2026-11-audio-events-m2.md](../plans/2026-11-audio-events-m2.md) (E1–E7); log under "M2" in `docs/design/0157-audio-core/build-log.md`. **Landed (E1):** the engine curve type `core/curve.ts` (`[x, y]` / `[x, y, interp]` keys; linear, step, smooth, easeIn, easeOut; `evalCurve`, `parseCurve`, `curveRange`; particle curves evaluate the same); the `myco.audioevent@1` parser `engine/audio/eventFormat.ts` (all seven node types, local parameters, automation, limits, cooldown, spatial; lenient with problems; `eventClips`, `formatAudioEvent`); `audio.parameters`, `audio.automation` (bus volume and lowpass from global parameters) and `audio.seed` in `engine/audio/settings.ts`; the mixer size gate is a 256 KB tripwire (`native/audio/build.mjs`). Tests: `engine/tests/curve.test.ts`, `audioEventFormat.test.ts`, `audioSettings.test.ts`. **E2:** the core's automation (`audio-core/src/automation.rs`: 64 global and 1024×16 instance parameter slots with seek, a 1024-curve table, ≤ 8 bindings per voice and per bus, evaluated once per quantum and ramped; per-voice low-pass only for voices with a low-pass binding) and clip loop regions with release (SET_LOOP); opcodes SET_LOOP, AUTOMATE, CURVES, PARAM, BUS_AUTOMATE; ABI 1.4; command-writer methods; goldens `automation-voice`, `automation-bus`, `sustain-release`; five metered scenes. Tests: `audio-core/tests/automation.rs`. **E3 so far:** `plugins/audio/runtime/events/` (`automation.ts`: the `AutomationSink` interface and `TsAutomation`, the fallback evaluator; `eventRuntime.ts`: instances, all seven node types, parameters, limits, cooldown, seeded picks, continuous chains, sustain and release); `Audio.play`/`setParameter`/`getParameter`, `AudioEventHandle`, `AudioSource` with event assets, `Audio.debug().events`; `mixer._setAutomation` for fallback bus automation. Tests: `events/events.test.ts` (smoke, fake backend). **Missing:** the core backend's `automation` sink, `clipDuration`, `horizon` and rebuild replay, the Babylon fallback's `setLoop`, the parity test and fuller E3 tests, loop points from files (E4), the sample, guide and bench (E5), the editor (E6). Particles still use their own `evalCurve`. Wave 1 (2026-11-10): the core runs event automation (`core/coreAutomation.ts`: CURVES/PARAM/AUTOMATE/BUS_AUTOMATE/SET_LOOP, mirrored and replayed in a hard rebuild, `group` in PLAY, selection reads automated gain); Babylon region loops and release; per-World seeding and sequence state; the debug panel groups voices by event; the decoder reads WAV `smpl` and Ogg Vorbis loop points (not yet used: E4.2/E4.5); the `audioEvent` asset kind, New Event and Create event from clips (the Event editor is a placeholder until E6.3); the curve widget is shared in `src/editor/ui/` (exported through `editor/sdk`). Wave 2 (on master 2026-11-10): Rust–TypeScript parity test (26 scenes); the event test suite and four wasm event goldens; the hand-over from the loading backend; instances retire without a per-frame scan; equal-power blends; sidecar `loopStart`/`loopEnd` and file loop points resolved in `loopPoints.ts` (used by events; plain looping clips from E4.5); the Sound Lab events page and the M2 listening pass; the Sound events guide; the M2 bench (`npm run bench:audio-events`); Project Settings > Audio Parameters and Automation. Wave 3 (on master 2026-11-10): streamed region loops with a release spliced into the core's ring (E4.4); plain looping clips use their file or sidecar loop points on both backends (E4.5); selection keeps an automated voice mixed until its fade ends (fixes the owner's listening-pass gap); the Event editor with audition and asset drop targets (E6.3, E6.4, E6.3b, ADR 0160); `@field` descriptions (ADR 0159); `releaseFade`; event clips default to their own loop points; `Audio.play` allocations cut ≈ 40%; the `@slow` events spec on core and Babylon. Wave 4: `Audio.play` of a typical event ≈ 4 µs on the core (layered events ≈ 9 µs, accepted); `Audio.debug()` on the core shows automation; the editor and settings Playwright specs; the curve widget in narrow docks; groups held until their last voice ends. Remaining: the lab-VM budgets (E5.5) and the owner's listening-pass re-check and sign-off (E7.1). Decisions: [m2-decisions.md](../design/0157-audio-core/m2-decisions.md). |

| [0075](../decisions/0075-babylonjs-render-backend.md) Babylon.js render backend | Done (stages 1–10: 0078 dropped stage 10, 0081 restored it and removed the three.js render backend) | **Switch:** `render/RenderSurface.ts` (the interface `Engine` and `World` use), `render/createRenderSurface.ts` (always a `BabylonSurface`: the `rendererChoice()` toggle between three.js and Babylon went with 0081 step 1). **Stage 1:** `render/babylon/BabylonSurface.ts` (canvas, layout, render scale; loads the renderer on demand) and `render/babylon/BabylonRenderer.ts`:<br>• the engine: WebGPU, with the WebGL2 engine as a lazy fallback<br>• perspective and orthographic cameras with viewports<br>• built-in and imported meshes, converted from the model loader's geometry<br>• lit, unlit and physical material assets as `PBRMaterial` (colour, texture, tiling, normal map)<br>• models' own colour, texture and metal/rough<br>• sprites with slices, pivots, flips, colour and layer order<br>**Stage 2** (`render/babylon/lighting.ts`):<br>• Light components (directional, point, spot, ambient), with the default ambient + sun only when a World has no Lights<br>• shadow maps at the tier's size over each directional light's `shadowArea`, and cascaded shadows for the first shadow-casting directional light on tiers with cascades<br>• mesh `castShadows` / `receiveShadows`<br>• the Environment component (`.hdr`, `.exr`, `.png`/`.jpg` maps; intensity, rotation; skybox with intensity and blur)<br>• the default studio room, generated as an in-memory `.hdr` (`studioEnvironment.ts`)<br>**Stage 3** (`render/babylon/frameGraph.ts`, `finishShader.ts`):<br>• a frame graph draws every frame: per-camera HDR textures sized to each viewport, overlay cameras blended over, shadow maps as shared tasks<br>• PostProcessing per camera, filtered by the tier: SSAO2 ambient occlusion, depth of field, bloom, chromatic aberration, FXAA (standing in for SMAA)<br>• our finish pass (WGSL and GLSL): colour grading, the vignette, AgX / ACES / Neutral tone mapping with exposure, and the sRGB encode<br>• one Babylon patch (`patches/`, via `patch-package`): the geometry pass's view depth clears to the camera's far plane<br>**Stage 4**:<br>• the shader graph compiler's WGSL mode (`shaderGraph/compile.ts`, `nodes.ts`, `wgsl.ts`); graphs have no GLSL output now (0081; hand-written shaders may ship GLSL, 0106)<br>• Custom Function nodes' optional WGSL body (`function.wgsl`; the Playground's ripple sub-graph has one)<br>• `render/babylon/graphMaterial.ts`: a material plugin splicing graphs into PBR (vertex offset, colour, alpha and clip, metalness, roughness, emission, normal, properties, textures, time, resolution); WebGPU only (plain PBR on the WebGL2 fallback)<br>• per-renderer material properties (ADR 0049) for graph and built-in materials<br>**Stage 5**:<br>• `render/particles/simulation.ts` (deleted by ADR 0251): a renderer-neutral CPU simulation of the whole effect format (emission, bursts, shapes, space, every behavior, sub-emitters, flipbooks, trail history)<br>• `render/babylon/particles.ts`: plays ParticleSystem components (commands, editor preview, destroy-on-finish, tint, emission scale × tier, graph bindings) and draws the simulation through Babylon's CPU particle system (the GPU kernel backends are 0094's, `gpuParticles.ts`)<br>**Stage 6**: reflection probes (`babylon/probes.ts`; box picking in the renderer-neutral `render/probeBoxes.ts`), fog (scene fog, per-material opt-out, sky fog), water surfaces (`babylon/water.ts`, a PBR plugin in WGSL and, for the WebGL2 fallback, GLSL whose wave sum is `GERSTNER_BODY` itself: the plugin was WGSL-only, so on WebGL2 the sea was a flat plane at rest height while ships ride the sampled waves, and troughs hid their hulls in the pirates sample; seen in headless Chromium on WebGL2 before and after, hulls now sit on the waves as on three.js); the GPU check page `spikes/babylon/stage6.html`; `spikes/babylon/depth.html` (a red cube before a blue one, or `?scene=playground`, at `?tier=` with `?set=` tier overrides, read back each frame)<br>**Stage 7**: renderer-neutral model data (`ModelMesh.data` / `surfaces`), read by physics and the Babylon backend; multi-material models on Babylon; models' own textures (a model waits for the textures its loader started before its surfaces are described: they were taken before the images decoded, so every OBJ/MTL texture drew white on Babylon; `modelTextures.test.ts`); Babylon thumbnails, Inspector previews, material and node previews and the particle effect preview (`render/babylon/previews/`), routed by `render/previewRouting.ts`<br>**Stage 8** (`render/babylon/editorTools.ts` over `editorContext.ts`, loaded on demand): picking and canvas queries (`editorQueries.ts`); the grid and axes, selection outlines, editor overlays, camera and light icons, frusta and light wires (`editorOverlays.ts`); the transform gizmo (`editorGizmo.ts`); the overlay pass in the frame graph; the camera preview inset<br>`render/babylon/register.ts` holds the side-effect imports. `@babylonjs/core` 9.32.0 is a dependency, external in the engine library build. In the player build, Babylon is only in the lazy `BabylonRenderer` chunk (320 KB gzip with stage 2); the player's startup files (285 KB gzip) have none. **Spike:** `spikes/babylon/` (430 KB gzip at start with module-by-module imports). **Tests:** `babylonImports.test.ts` (no barrel import; Babylon only under `render/babylon/` and tests), `babylonLighting.test.ts` (on Babylon's `NullEngine`: light types, colours, poses, defaults, shadow requests and extents; the studio `.hdr` parsed by Babylon's reader), `babylonFrame.test.ts` (the finish shader in both languages, its read-only uniform list, each camera's pass layout for its effects and anti-aliasing, the geometry pass's own cleared depth buffer), `shaderGraphWgsl.test.ts` (every node and both sample graphs in WGSL, no GLSL tokens, uniforms, textures, varyings, Custom Function bodies), `babylonGraphMaterial.test.ts` (where the plugin puts each part of a graph, uniforms, property overrides, the WebGL2 fallback), `particleSimulation.test.ts` (the simulation, seeded; deleted by ADR 0251), `babylonParticles.test.ts` (lifecycle, commands, preview, textures, render modes on NullEngine), `babylonProbes.test.ts` (capture when ready, smallest box, intensity, tier off, removal), `babylonWater.test.ts` (grid, rebuilds, node, material, probe, WGSL and GLSL; the plugin is on GLSL materials; both languages' wave functions, translated to JavaScript and run on the uniforms the plugin binds, give `gerstner()`'s displacement, three.js's height and `WaterSurface.sample`'s height where the drawn vertex lands), `modelNeutralData.test.ts`, `babylonPreviews.test.ts` and `babylonPreviewRouting.test.ts` (scene contents per thumbnail kind, lifecycle, routing), `babylonEditorQueries.test.ts` (pick order, moved meshes, icons and text, rays, planes, screen rectangles, bounds), `babylonEditorOverlays.test.ts` (grid, outlines, overlay counts and handle picking, camera and light gizmos, icons), `babylonEditorGizmo.test.ts` (proxy placement, modes, planar, snap, multi-node drags to local poses, hit testing). **Checked by hand** in the editor on WebGPU: the 3D Playground and Flappy Bird render, and Flappy plays. The Playground in play mode matches three.js's lighting, reflections, soft shadows, AgX tone mapping, vignette, AO and bloom; Flappy's colours are unchanged through the finish pass. **Particles** run in the Playground's play mode (three campfire emitters with live particles, no GPU errors), but how they look wasn't seen: the app window was minimized. **Stage 6 on the GPU** (the check page, 240 frames): water, fog, a probe and shadows compile and draw with no WebGPU errors; the crate reflects the probe; seen only as data, not on screen. **Previews** were checked in the browser pane on WebGPU by the subagent that wrote them (model, material, graph, prefab, environment and particle thumbnails; the Shader Graph and particle previews). **Stage 8 in the editor** (WebGPU, Playground): the grid, the camera icon and frustum and the light icons seen on screen; clicking a crate selects it; with it selected, the outline and transform gizmo are drawn (checked as scene data, since the browser pane was hidden); selecting the Camera draws the preview inset after the overlay pass; no errors. A gizmo drag on screen was not tried. **Stage 9** (game UI): see 0076's row; done. **Performance on a real GPU** (WebGPU, pirates sample; ADR notes "Performance on a real GPU"): every mesh rebuilt its shader defines every frame (lights without shadow maps left at `shadowEnabled = true`, which Babylon's object renderer flips twice a frame, and a shadow casters' list emptied and refilled every frame), and nothing was frustum culled. Fixed in `lighting.ts` and `frameGraph.ts` (`refillIfChanged` in `render/changeDetection.ts`, a `FrameGraphCullObjectsTask` per camera), with matrix-change checks in `syncMesh`, no probe lookups without probes and cached Yoga properties in `UICore`. A Practice match went from about 111 ms a frame (render CPU 45 ms) to about 14 ms (render CPU 2.4 ms), against 6 ms on three.js; the editor from 19 to 9 ms. Tests: `babylonLighting.test.ts` (shadowEnabled only with a shadow map; a frame marks no mesh dirty), `babylonFrame.test.ts` (casters replaced in one splice only on change; matrices re-frozen only when changed), `uiCore.test.ts` (unchanged properties don't reach Yoga). Then the screen UI's upload: Babylon GUI copied its whole canvas to the GPU on every redraw (about 5–15 ms of GPU queue time per copy); on WebGPU `ui.ts` now writes only the redrawn rectangle (`redrawnPixels`, `flipRows` in `render/pixelRegions.ts`; pixel-identical to a full upload), taking a 1902×1070 match from about 11 to 7.7 ms a frame (`babylonUI.test.ts`: the rectangle, the row flip, Babylon's update off WebGPU). **The periodic hitch** (a pause about every 5 s in a pirates match, a cannon volley's splashes): each splash fades with `setMaterialProperty("color")` every frame, and the Babylon backend answered each change with a new clone of the material, so the splash's mesh was rebuilt every frame and, once Babylon released the old clone's effect, its shaders compiled again. `rendererMaterial` now keeps one copy per renderer and sets it in place (`applyBuiltinOverrides` in `materials.ts`, as three.js's `applyBuiltinProperties`), and `register.ts` sets `Effect.PersistentMode` so an effect outlives its last material. Over 30 s of a match: frames over 22 ms 48 → 3, longest step 728 → 37 ms, materials created 1,650 → 101, shader modules compiled 56 → 6, `sync` 4.7 → 2.4 ms. **Light count steady** (`lighting.ts`): a material's shader is compiled per light count and types, so every ship lantern spawning or sinking recompiled every lit material (100–1,400 ms frames); a gone or disabled Light's Babylon light now stays enabled at zero intensity for the next Light of its type, and a World with a point light reserves dark point lights up to the 8 a material uses. 65 s of a Practice match against the master commit before it: frames over 100 ms 20 → 6 (3 of them the match loading), over 50 ms 38 → 12, longest 1,421 → 720 ms, 99th percentile frame 60 → 21 ms, pipelines compiled 60 → 24. Clustered lights replaced the reserve on the Babylon backend (ADR 0079), and scene loads warm up the materials of things spawned mid-match (ADR 0080). Also: per-cascade shadow caster culling (`shadowCulling.ts`; shadow draws 102 → 28 a frame, pixel-identical; no measurable GPU change, since the shadow pass costs about 0.3 ms here); lights, fog and sprites updated in place, sprites re-frozen only when moved, each mesh's frozen matrix reused, the material asset's shader graph looked up once per JSON object (`materialGraphOfJson`, both backends); overlay lines uploaded only when they change and only the used part, on both backends, with 3D collider wireframes cached per collider (`colliderGizmo.ts`; the island bay's editor overlay 0.88 → 0.44 ms a frame). Tests: `babylonMaterialOverrides.test.ts`, `babylonShadowCulling.test.ts`, `babylonEditorOverlays.test.ts` (uploads only on change). Left: about 40 MB/s of short-lived garbage during a match on both backends, mostly from the client World's scripts and spawning, not the renderer's per-frame work (a paused `sync` allocates about 10 KB); first-use shader variants still compile mid-match (one 100–300 ms frame per new variant); Babylon's GPU time couldn't be split further in the hidden pane (a ~3.5 ms wait that doesn't move with the scene pass, shadows, bloom or UI off). **The WebGL2 fallback** threw in the frame graph (`buildTextureLayout is not a function`): `register.ts` imported only the WebGPU engine extensions; it now imports their WebGL twins one by one too (the set in `Engines/Extensions/index.js` less debugging, compute shaders, multiview and transform feedback). Checked in headless Chromium with no WebGPU adapter: the editor draws the pirates sample's menu scene, its camera preview inset and play mode on WebGL2 (`FrameGraph: Timeout … shadow0` is logged once on SwiftShader, with the index import as well; the graph builds on a later frame). `spikes/babylon/ui.html` reads back black on WebGL2 (it copies the canvas without `preserveDrawingBuffer`), so use the editor to check WebGL2. Not run: WebGPU device loss. (`?renderer=babylon` no longer exists: Babylon is the only renderer, 0081.) **Not checked by hand:** an Environment map (no sample has one), spot lights, point-light shadows, depth of field, chromatic aberration, colour grading, FXAA, and several cameras or split viewports (no sample has them). **Not automated:** nothing checks Babylon's pixels (the unit tests run on `NullEngine`; the Playwright specs in `tests/e2e/` open the editor, which draws on Babylon). **Finished 2026-11-06** (ADR notes "Finishing the migration"): mesh and trail particle modes, particle material assets, `blendTiles` and `renderOrder` (`particleMeshes.ts`, deleted by ADR 0251, and `particleDraw.ts`); shadows that follow a graph's vertex offset (`graphMaterial.ts`, a `ShadowDepthWrapper`); Main Light nodes read the main light by three.js's rule (`BabylonLighting.mainLight`); the camera preview inset's sky (`fitSky` per camera); the Custom Function WGSL body field; the gizmo's size and absolute-grid snapping. Tests: `babylonParticles.test.ts`, `babylonGraphMaterial.test.ts`, `babylonLighting.test.ts`, `babylonEditorGizmo.test.ts`, `functionWgsl.test.ts`. **Seen on screen 2026-11-06** (WebGPU, the Playground with temporary test emitters, a vertex-offset graph and an Environment map; ADR notes "Seen on screen"): trails, mesh particles, material-asset quads, blended tiles, shadows following a vertex offset, the inset's skybox, and the gizmo's size (as three.js's) and absolute-grid snapping. Two faults found and fixed: material-asset quads and trails had no normals, so a graph reading the normal didn't compile and the invalid WebGPU pipeline turned the whole frame black (`particleMeshes.ts`, since deleted by ADR 0251, gave them camera-facing normals); flipbooks on Babylon's particle system showed the mirrored row's tile upside down (Babylon reads sprite sheets with V 0 at the top; `usesSheet` loads those textures unflipped). Tests: `babylonParticles.test.ts` (normals on material quads and trails, none on plain trails; which textures load unflipped). The inset clears with the editor's background when the camera has none, on both backends, where the game shows the project's background colour. **Light falloff** (2026-11-06, found by 0079's pixel compare): Babylon's PBR materials default to physical falloff, which ignores a spot's inner angle and every light's range, so all spots drew as the same soft blob whatever their `spotSoftness`, and point and spot lights reached past their `range`. Lit materials (`pbr()`, graph materials, water) now use glTF falloff (`useEngineLightFalloff` in `materials.ts`): inverse square to nothing at `range`, and the cone edge from `spotSoftness` (Babylon's `innerAngle`), as the Light component documents and three.js drew. `range` 0 now means no limit (it was 0.001 m, hidden by the physical falloff). Clustered lights follow the material's falloff, so both paths still match. Checked on the compare page (softness 0 is a hard edge, 1 fully soft) and in the Playground and pirates samples. **UVs for graphs without textures** (2026-11-06): a graph material that read UVs but sampled no texture got zero UVs, since Babylon binds the UV attribute only for the material's own textures; the graph plugin now asks for them (`graphUsesUv`, `prepareDefinesBeforeAttributes`; `babylonGraphMaterial.test.ts`). It made the ripple sub-graph's previews flat and the Playground's water lose its ripples. **Left:** Babylon's GPU timer reads 0 under a frame graph; with snapping on, the gizmo's handle follows the pointer rather than the snapped node; trails take one colour and size per particle; nothing checks Babylon's pixels automatically. |
| [0076](../decisions/0076-ui-yoga-layout-babylon-gui-drawing.md) Game UI on Yoga and Babylon GUI | Done (IME and paste not checked); layout moved to Taffy by [0434](../decisions/0434-ui-layout-moves-from-yoga-to-taffy-compiled-to-webassembly.md), so the Yoga details below are history | **Core** (`ui/UICore.ts`, `ui/layout.ts`, contract `ui/uiCoreTypes.ts`; renderer-neutral): the 0069 components laid out with `yoga-layout` 3.2.1 (loaded on demand, `ready` promise; `display: none` is Yoga's display none, and the element and its subtree are reported not visible, so they're neither drawn nor hit; a core made after Yoga loaded lays out from its first sync), text measured through the host (2D canvas) or estimated in Node; screen canvases scaled like Unity's Canvas Scaler; world canvases on the node's plane; 0069's routing rules from the core's own boxes, including world canvases through the view Camera's `screenPointToRay`; input fields typed through one shared hidden `<input>` (caret position exposed for drawing); a `passive` core lays out without routing or typing (the camera preview's). **Fonts** (`ui/fonts.ts`): Inter is bundled as `ui/fonts/Inter-latin-wght.woff2` (SIL OFL, `ui/fonts/Inter-OFL.txt`; THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md): the variable font, weights 100–900, subset to Latin, Latin-1 and common punctuation as Google Fonts publishes it (`@fontsource-variable/inter` 5.3.0's latin file, 230 characters, 48 KB); other characters fall back per glyph to `system-ui, sans-serif`. It loads through `FontFace` before the Babylon UI starts; `UIText.font` and `UIInputField.font` name a project font file asset (`.ttf`, `.otf`, `.woff`, `.woff2`; empty is Inter), loaded on first use under a generated family name with Inter after it. Each font that arrives bumps `fontEpoch()`: the core measures every text again, and the drawing layer drops the GUI's cached font heights and redraws. `UIButton` has no font: its label is a `UIText`. Shipping: Vite bundles the woff2 as an asset of the player, the editor and so exported games (`assets/Inter-latin-wght-*.woff2`, listed in `files.json`); the engine library build puts it beside its bundle as `dist/engine/Inter-latin-wght.woff2` (`vite.engine.config.ts`, as the navigation WASM); `serve.mjs` and the dev server's sample and player routes serve fonts with font MIME types. **Drawing** (`render/babylon/ui.ts`, `@babylonjs/gui` 9.32.0 imported module by module): panels and buttons as rectangles (hover and pressed colours), text blocks, images (`fit`: fill, contain, and cover by cropping the source), input fields with placeholder and caret; screen canvases and the interim `Text` on one fullscreen texture drawn by a frame graph task after the game's camera; world canvases as planes with mesh textures at 2× resolution. **The editor's camera preview inset** shows the game camera's view with its screen UI: a second, passive core lays the screen canvases and the interim `Text` out at the inset's size, and a second drawing layer (`screenOnly`, so world canvases aren't duplicated) draws them at the inset's place, after the preview camera (`CameraView.previewUI`); the draw task resets the viewport the preview camera left. uikit and `UIRuntime.ts` went with the three.js backend (0081). **Tests:** `uiCore.test.ts` (layout, text, fonts named and re-measured when one arrives, a passive core, world canvases, routing, fields with a DOM stub), `babylonUI.test.ts` (control tree per kind on NullEngine, colours, visibility, caret, the font field, redraw on a font's arrival, image source, world planes, interim Text placement, the preview's origin and screen-only drawing, release). **Checked in headless Chromium** (Playwright's Chromium 1243, `--enable-unsafe-webgpu --enable-unsafe-swiftshader --use-vulkan=swiftshader --no-sandbox`: WebGPU on SwiftShader), deviceScaleFactor 2: on `spikes/babylon/ui.html`, Inter loads, the button hovers and clicks, text is sharp at 2× (rasterised at the device resolution), and a 3:1 image with `cover` in a square shows only its middle third (`fill` shows all three; this found UI images never drawing on Babylon: the loaded texture was dropped by a stale-check against the wrong cache, fixed); in the editor, the pirates sample's menu scene draws in edit mode, its camera preview inset shows the menu laid out at the inset's size (this found the second core waiting for a sync that the idle editor doesn't make, and the UI squeezed into the inset's viewport; both fixed), and the menu runs in play mode (131 elements, 36 visible, the name field filled by its script, the lobby connected); the same on WebGL2 without a WebGPU adapter; an exported pirates build served by `serve.mjs` loads `assets/Inter-latin-wght-*.woff2` (`font/woff2`) and draws the menu. **Not checked:** IME and paste in a real field (not possible headless); a project font file in a real project (only with a FontFace stub in tests). Documents are 0077's row. |
| [0077](../decisions/0077-ui-documents-markup-styles-scripts.md) UI documents: markup, stylesheets, scripts, scene-view editing | Superseded by [0423](../decisions/0423-game-ui-is-authored-in-svelte-rendered-onto-the-ui-core.md): retired in its wave D (it was done: wave A the runtime, wave B the editor) | **Retired by 0423 wave D (2026-11-26):** `ui/UIDocument.ts`, `ui/documentSource.ts`, the markup parser (`ui/document/markup.ts`, `parsers.ts`), `{name}` bindings, `ui.data`/`ui.list()`, `<Use>`/`<Style>`/`<Template>`, the `*.ui.css` stylesheet kind, the `uidocument` asset kind and New UI Document, the editor's markup tooling (`uiDocuments.ts`, `uiDocumentEdits.ts`, `uiEdit/markupText.ts`, `uiEdit/cssText.ts`, the document Inspector, rows, menus, drags and handle paths) and their tests are gone; a saved `engine.UIDocument` fails to load. What follows is the record of what was built. **Living on** for Svelte UI: the stylesheet parser `ui/document/stylesheet.ts` (components' CSS, parsed in the editor and at export), the cascade (`cascade.ts`, `selectorQuery.ts`), the event core, `display`/`position`, the editor's value sources (`uiFieldSources.ts`), field rows (`ui/UIFieldSourceView.tsx`), handle maths and drags (`uiHandleMath.ts`, `uiHandleDrags.ts`), the outline, picking and `WriteFiles` file patches in the scene's history. **Parsers** (`ui/document/`, contract `types.ts`; pure, renderer-neutral, loaded on demand as their own chunk, about 41 KB gzip, by `loadUIDocumentSupport`, which `SceneManager.load` awaits for scenes that reference a `*.ui.xml`): `markup.ts` on `htmlparser2` 12.0.0 in XML mode, with element, open-tag, attribute and value spans; `<Use src>` expanded (cycles and missing files reported; attributes on `<Use>` override the used root's), `<Style src>` collected, `<Template>` kept apart, `{name}` bindings split (`{{` is a literal brace); problems carry file, line and column and never throw. `stylesheet.ts` on `css-tree` 3.2.1's parser only (no lexer data): tag, `.class`, `#id`, `*`, descendant and child selectors, `:hover`/`:pressed`/`:focus`/`:disabled`, `:root` variables with `var(--x, fallback)`; at-rules, attribute selectors, pseudo-elements, `+`/`~` and `!important` reported. The cascade: specificity then order, attributes beat rules, `color`/`font-size`/`font-weight`/`text-align` and variables inherit (through elements without the field, so a Panel's `color` reaches its Text), values converted to the field's type (px, %, `auto`, hex/`rgb()`/named colours, `none` for no fill, Vector2 pairs, `url()` and relative asset paths), unknown properties and bad values warned once. **Runtime** (`ui/UIDocument.ts`): the `UIDocument` component (`document` asset field) builds the elements as child nodes (named by `id`, else the tag), not saved in the scene; a Button's text content becomes a Text child. `query`/`queryAll` (on the document and on elements), `classList` (restyles the element and its descendants), `as(Type)`, polled `clicked`/`pressed`/`hovered`/`changed`/`submitted`/`focused`/`value`; `data` bindings checked each frame (a missing value leaves `{name}` as written, so the editor shows it); `list(selector, items, setup?, { key })` from the `<Template>` with row reuse by key, `{field}` reading the item, then `data`. `on`/`off` for `click`, `change`, `submit` and `hover`, run by `engine.UIDocumentEvents` (update phase, after the network systems, before `engine.UpdateScripts`), which also fills the `UIEvents` buffer (element, document, list item); a script setting a field's value isn't a `change`. `engine.UIDocumentSync` (after `engine.LateUpdateScripts`) applies bindings, restyles elements whose pseudo-class state or classes changed, and rebuilds when the document, a used document or a stylesheet changes in `Assets` (hot reload), keeping data, re-running lists with their setup, and giving static elements' handles and handlers to the new nodes. A Button's `:hover`/`:pressed` backgrounds become its `hoverColor`/`pressedColor`, so the drawing shows them. Disabling the component hides the tree; removing it destroys the tree. The player and the editor load `.xml` and `.css` as text; the Code panel edits them (CSS with Monaco's CSS worker). **Interpretations:** `hover` handlers and events fire when the pointer moves onto a button, not every frame it's over it; an InputField's text content is its `value`; markup `src`, `image` and other asset paths are project-relative unless they start with `./` or `../`. **Tests:** `uiDocument.test.ts` (markup spans, `<Use>`, problems, bindings, whitespace; `display` and the `position` alias from rules and attributes, `display: none` from a class and a pseudo-class; selectors, specificity, matching, the cascade and conversions; building, bindings, classes, `:hover`, handlers and the buffer, rows by key with items, hot reload keeping handlers, disable and removal). **Proving ground: the pirates menu** (built as `menu-document.scene.json` beside the hand-built original; since [0421](../decisions/0421-game-ui-is-built-only-from-ui-documents.md) it is the game's `scenes/menu.scene.json`): its 131 hand-built UI nodes become one node with a `UIDocument` (`ui/menu.ui.xml`, `ui/menu.ui.css` with the theme as `:root` variables) and `scripts/behaviors/MenuDocument.ts` (now `Menu.ts`), which does `Menu`'s job with handlers, `data` bindings and `list()` for the open rooms, the crew, the settings and the results (`piratesMenuDocument.test.ts`, now `piratesMenu.test.ts`: builds with no problems, its buttons create a room, step a setting, ready, leave and start a practice match). Drawn in the editor's play mode it matched the original's layout (on Babylon and, then, three.js). It found: the Node project reader (`server/project.ts`, used by the dedicated server, exports and tests) gave `.xml`/`.css` as `file:` URLs, not text (fixed); text content collapses runs of spaces as in HTML (kept, see below); no `display: none`, so an element couldn't start hidden without keeping its space (fixed: the `display` field, 0069's row); `position` wasn't an alias of `position-type` (fixed). With those, `MenuDocument` shows and hides screens and controls by toggling a `.hidden { display: none }` class instead of `setActive` (what starts hidden is marked in the markup, so the scene view shows the home screen alone), the veil is `position: absolute` and hidden the same way. **`display`** is a field like any other: set by attribute (`display="none"`), by rules (so `classList` changes and pseudo-classes toggle it) or by scripts; the node stays active, so scripts and polled state keep working. **`position`** is the one alias: `position: absolute` in a rule or attribute sets `positionType` (`propertyField` in `stylesheet.ts`). **Whitespace (decided: no `white-space` property):** text content keeps HTML's `white-space: normal`: runs of ASCII whitespace become one space, trimmed (now only ASCII whitespace, as in HTML, so `&#160;` keeps a space); exact spacing is the `text="…"` attribute, which is never collapsed. A `white-space` property would be the cascade's first property that isn't a component field, would make content parsing depend on stylesheets resolved after it, and would have to rebind text when classes change, for what an attribute already does. **Wave A in a sample:** the pirates menu document (above) uses them; since 0421 the game boots it. **Editor, wave B part 1** (read-only): the editor loads the parsers at startup (`session.ts`, beside `Engine.create`), so documents build with the scene. Built-in components may now run their activation hooks inside an edit-mode subtree (`Component._activatesInEditMode`, `World._refreshComponent`; ADR 0026's rule still holds for everything else); `UIDocument` opts in, so documents build in the scene being edited, and the Editor World's frame runs `engine.UIDocumentSync`, so saving a `.ui.xml` or `.ui.css` in the Code panel (`project.writeFile` updates the asset value `Assets.get` returns) rebuilds them the next frame; `engine.UIDocumentEvents` skips documents in edit mode. A missing document or stylesheet is remembered, so the document rebuilds once the file appears. Document-built nodes are never saved: scene saves, prefabs made from a node, play mode's snapshot, copy and paste all read the SceneDocument, not the World (saving and prefabs tested). **Hierarchy:** a node's documents' elements show under its children, muted and italic with a ◇, the file in the tooltip, starting closed and opening to a selected element; read-only (no rename, delete, drag or menu); hidden while searching. **Selection** (`src/editor/uiDocuments.ts`, the model part 2 builds on): an element is addressed by a ref (host node path key, which UIDocument, the element's static path `UIDocumentElement._path`, e.g. `/#menu/#play`, `/Panel[1]`, `/#play/label`), selected in `selectedUIElement` apart from the node selection (each clears the other); refs survive rebuilds. **Scene view:** a plain click picks UI first, with the UI core's layout boxes (`UICore.pickAll`: every drawn element, screen canvases then world canvases by the scene camera's ray, `pickUI` in `engine/editor`), then colliders and renderers; clicking the same spot again cycles down through the elements and on to scene nodes. The selected element's box is outlined (`uiOutline.ts`: an SVG polygon over the viewport from `UICore.cornersOf`, a world canvas's corners projected through the view). **Inspector:** an element's component fields with their value and where it comes from: its attribute (file:line), a stylesheet rule (selector, file:line), inherited (followed to the element that sets it), the text content, a `{binding}`, or the default; a button's hover and pressed colours name their `:hover` rule (`UIDocument._resolveAt`). Clicking a source opens its file in the Code panel. Internal engine API for the editor (all `@internal`): `UIDocument._onBuilt`, `_elementOf(component)`, `_elementAt(path)`, `_markupPath`, `_sheets`, `_files`, `_resolveAt`; `UIDocumentElement._path`, `_boundFields()`; `UICoreLike.pickAll`/`cornersOf`. **Tests:** `uiDocuments.test.ts` (documents building in the Editor World, hot reload through `writeFile`, nothing in saved scenes or prefabs, refs and rows, markup locations, every kind of value source, selection apart from nodes, picking keys and click cycling), `uiDocument.test.ts` (edit-mode building and hot reload, element lookup and paths across rebuilds, no events in edit mode, `_resolveAt`), `uiCore.test.ts` (`pickAll`, `cornersOf`, world canvases by a given ray). **Checked in the editor** (Chromium, WebGPU on SwiftShader): a screen and a world canvas document draw; the hierarchy lists their elements; clicking the Play button selects it, again selects its panel; clicking the world sign selects its panel; outlines follow; the Inspector shows attribute, rule, inherited and content sources; saving the stylesheet in the Code panel restyles the title at once, keeping the selection. **Not checked:** WebKit, a document inside a prefab instance in the editor. **Editor, wave B part 2** (editing, written back to the files): **Text edits** (`src/editor/uiEdit/`, pure): `markupText.ts` parses its own source tree with `htmlparser2` (every tag, `<Use>`, `<Style>` and `<Template>` included, with spans) and sets, inserts (at the end of the open tag, one per line when the tag is written that way) and removes attributes, keeping their quotes; sets text content (keeping the whitespace and comments around it, opening a self-closing element); inserts, removes (its whole line), moves and duplicates elements (fresh ids) indented like their siblings, or one level in for a first child (a self-closing parent opens up); adds a `<Style src>`. Elements are addressed by indices among element children (UI tags and `<Use>`), the parsed markup's `children` order. `cssText.ts` uses the runtime's stylesheet parser to change a declaration's value in place, add one in the rule's own style (fixing a missing `;`), remove one (its line when alone on it), and add a rule at the end. Each edit re-parses; nothing else in the file changes (tested byte for byte, `uiEdit/textEdits.test.ts`). **Undo: the scene's history** (ADR 0026/0027): a `Command` may list `files` (`WriteFiles`: path and whole new text); the `CommandStack` writes them through its `files` host (`TextFileHost`; the editor's is `textFiles.ts` on `project.writeFile`, reads see writes at once, writes are queued per file and coalesced, so a drag costs one write in flight) and keeps each file's change in the step as a small patch (`FilePatch`: offset, old and new middle; `created`/`removed` for whole files), so Ctrl+Z, redo, the History panel and history files cover UI edits in order with scene edits. A group (`beginGroup`) records one patch per file. Undo or redo leaves a file that changed elsewhere since (the patch doesn't match) as it is and says so. Steps that only wrote files don't make the scene dirty (the files are saved as they're made). History files gain an optional `files` per state (same `myco.history@1` format). **Edits** (`src/editor/uiDocumentEdits.ts`, every one a `WriteFiles` step on the open scene's stack): `setUIElementAttributes(ref, {name: value | null}, label?)` (the general API: the Inspector and part 3's handles), `setUIElementField` (the Inspector: validated with the cascade's own conversion; a Text's text, a button label or an InputField's value is the text content unless an attribute sets it), `setUIElementText`, `renameUIElement` (sets `id`, unique in the file, keeps the selection), `setUIElementClasses`, `deleteUIElement` (selects the parent), `duplicateUIElement`, `moveUIElement`/`whyNotMovable` (within the element's file and document), `createUIElement` (into a Canvas or Panel, after anything else, or into a document's root; selected), `moveUIElementToClass` (attributes but `id`, `class` and bindings into `.name { … }` in the file's first stylesheet, added or extended, kebab-case, colours as hex, lengths in px, assets as `url()`; the class added; without a stylesheet `<name>.ui.css` is made beside it and linked; it says when a more specific rule still wins), `createUIDocument` (a Canvas root with a panel and title, and its `.ui.css`; in the UI plugin's `editor/uiDocumentAssets.ts` since ADR 0149), `beginUIEdit`/`endUIEdit` (a gesture as one step). An element is edited in its own file: elements from a `<Use>` in the used document; a used root's moves, deletes and duplicates act on its `<Use>` tag, and attributes written on the `<Use>` tag (they override the root's) are edited there. Each edit checks the tag at the path still matches the built tree. Refused, with the reason in the status bar: read-only projects (samples; `project.canSave`, as other asset edits), play mode, list rows (no path), a button's label (only its text). **UI:** the Inspector edits each field (checkbox, choices, colour picker and hex, or text in attribute syntax), ✕ removes an element's own attribute, the id and classes edit in place, Move to class asks for the name (the id, else the tag, to start with); fields set by a rule, inheritance or a binding still show their source, and editing overrides them with an attribute. The hierarchy's element rows have a menu (create inside, rename, duplicate, delete), double-click or F2 renames, Delete and Ctrl+D act on a selected element, and dragging reorders or reparents within the file (`uiElementDrag.ts`, with a label saying what releasing does or why it can't). The Create menu (and the "+" menu) adds Panel, Text, Image, Button and Input Field into the selected element or the selected node's document; Create › UI Document makes a new document and a node showing it; the Assets panel has New UI Document. **Tests:** `uiEdit/textEdits.test.ts`, `document/history.test.ts` (file patches undone and redone with scene edits, groups, dirtiness, conflicts), `uiDocumentEdits.test.ts` (Inspector writes and hot reload, `<Use>` files, undo and a gesture as one step, conflicts, Move to class with and without a stylesheet, Create, rename, delete, duplicate, move, menus and shortcuts, read-only and play-mode refusal). **Checked in the editor** (Chromium, on the three.js backend since removed, a copy of the pirates menu in a test project): font size, text content, Move to class, Ctrl+Z three times back to the original files, Create Button in Document, rename from the row menu, dragging a row above another, Create › UI Document, a colour edit and its reset, the History panel and Edit › Undo naming the steps. **Not checked:** WebKit, a real folder on disk, writing through a slow file system during a long drag (Babylon was checked with part 3, below). **Interpretations:** text-file edits are saved as they're made (not on Save), like the Code panel; creating a document's files isn't an undo step (like other new assets), but the node showing it is. **Editor, wave B part 3** (moving and resizing in the scene view): the selected element gets eight resize handles (corners and edges) and its box drags (`src/editor/uiHandles.ts`, drawn in an SVG overlay above the outline's, theme tokens, no renderer code; the maths in `uiHandleMath.ts`, pure). The pointer is mapped into the element's frame through the homography of its box's four canvas corners and its size in UI pixels (`uiElementBox` in `engine/editor`; `UICorners.size`), taken when the drag starts: on a screen canvas that divides by the canvas scale, and a world canvas seen at any angle works the same way (no gap there). **Resize** sets `width`/`height` (whole UI pixels; Alt for fractions); the west and north handles also move `left`/`top` of an absolutely positioned element placed by them, otherwise the layout decides where the box sits (a left-aligned flex item grows to the right). Shift keeps the starting aspect ratio (a corner follows the side that changed more; an edge scales the other side). **Percentages (decided):** a size written as a percentage stays one, scaled by how much the box changed (`25%` of 320 px dragged to 352 px is `27.5%`), which is right whatever the percentage is of; a box 0 wide uses the parent's size. `auto` and pixels are written as pixels. **Move:** an absolutely positioned element (`position: absolute`) gets `left`/`top`, or `right`/`bottom` when those place it and `left`/`top` don't; with neither, `left`/`top` are written from where it is, measured from the parent's padding box (inside its border, as Yoga places absolute children: tested); percent offsets stay percentages of that box. An element in flex flow reorders among its siblings in flow (shown, not absolute, written in the file): an insertion marker crosses the parent's content box along its main axis (reversed directions handled), and releasing moves it with `moveUIElement` (its refusals apply). A press on the box that travels under 4 CSS pixels is a click, so click-to-select and cycling work as before (`viewportSelection.ts` doesn't start a selection box from a press on the selected element's box). A resize or move drag is one undo step (`beginUIEdit`, `setUIElementAttributes` per pointer move, the file rewritten as it goes, `endUIEdit`); **Escape** puts the files back (`cancelUIEdit`, recording nothing); a reorder is one step on release. Handles show only where the element can be edited: not in read-only projects (samples), play mode, list rows, a button's label or the document's root (the outline still shows). The transform gizmo doesn't show for an element (selecting one clears the node selection). **Renderer:** picking, the outline and the handles read the UI core's `pickAll`/`cornersOf` through `RenderSurface.uiRuntime()` (the three.js `UIRuntime` that also supplied them went with 0081). **Tests:** `uiHandleMath.test.ts` (canvas ↔ UI pixels at a scale and in perspective, each handle, `left`/`top` with the west and north edges, percentages, aspect lock, snapping, absolute moves, reorder targets in both directions), `uiHandles.test.ts` (from a real layout: a resize at canvas scale 2 as one step written as it goes and undone, a percentage width with the west edge, Escape, absolute moves by left/top and right/bottom, a flex reorder with its marker as one move, refusals), `uiCore.test.ts` (box sizes; absolute insets from the padding box). **Checked in the editor on Babylon** (Chromium, WebGL2) and then on three.js (since removed), on a copy of the pirates menu (plus an absolute badge) in a test project, with `the same script on each: the hierarchy's element rows; picking a button by clicking it in the viewport; the outline and eight handles; the Inspector's sources; dragging the south-east handle (width and height written) and one Ctrl+Z undoing the whole drag; Escape mid-drag restoring the file; dragging a button in a flex column past its sibling (the insertion marker, the markup reordered, Ctrl+Z putting it back); moving the absolute badge (left/top) and Shift-resizing it from the north-west corner (2:1 kept, left/top moved); then part 2's Inspector edit, Move to class, Create Button in Document and hierarchy drag on Babylon, and undoing all of it back to both original files; no handles in play mode. All passed on Babylon (WebGL2) and three.js. WebGPU wasn't checked in this session: SwiftShader's Vulkan device was lost on every page (the Babylon UI spike too), so the GPU process crashed before the editor drew`. **Not checked:** WebKit; a real folder on disk; handles on a world canvas in the real editor (the maths is tested in perspective); rotated cursors (handle cursors assume an upright box). Scene-view clicks (0117): UI hits split into painted elements (fill, border, text, image, filled input), which win over meshes and sprites, and bare containers, which come last in the click-cycle (`editor/uiPicking.ts`). UI canvases are click-selectable too: world canvases by rect, screen canvases by an 8px frame-edge strip (`pickCanvases`). |
| [0078](../decisions/0078-keep-both-render-backends.md) Keep both render backends | Superseded by 0081 (the toggle was removed 2026-11-06) | Built 2026-11-06 and removed the same day by 0081 step 1: `rendererChoice()` defaulted to Babylon.js with `?renderer=three` or `localStorage["myco.renderer"]` picking three.js (Settings > General > Renderer). All of it is gone (`rendererChoice.test.ts` deleted); an old `?renderer=three` link is ignored. See the 0081 row. |
| [0079](../decisions/0079-clustered-lighting-for-point-and-spot-lights.md) Clustered lighting | Done (Babylon backend) | `render/babylon/lighting.ts`: point and spot Lights without a shadow map go in one `ClusteredLightContainer` per scene, made with the first one. Gone or disabled ones park in it at zero intensity and 0.001 m range, and the next Light of their type reuses them, so materials only ever see the sun, ambient and the container. `reclusterForNextCamera` makes the container cluster again before each camera's render task (Babylon caches the first camera's). `frameGraph.ts`: render tasks built with `enableClusteredLights`. The WebGPU engines ask for the adapter's limits (`setMaximumLimits`). A device with only 16 inter-stage values, WebGL2 without float blending, or NullEngine keeps regular lights with the stop-gap (parked lights, the dark point-light reserve). Pirates match, 69 s: 6 frames over 100 ms (3 while loading), 99th percentile frame 21 ms, 25 pipelines, no WebGPU errors; level with the stop-gap, without unlit lights or the cap of 8. Tests: `babylonLighting.test.ts` ("clustered point and spot lights", on a NullEngine set up to cluster). **Pixel compare (2026-11-06):** `spikes/babylon/lights.html` draws one scene twice, clustered and with regular lights (`_setClustering`, check pages only), and diffs the frames: four spot lights at spotSoftness 0, 0.2, 0.5 and 1 over a matte floor, and a point light over a sphere. The two match exactly (largest channel difference 0 of 255) on WebGPU at Ultra. It also showed that `spotSoftness` and `range` did nothing on Babylon, clustered or not: see the 0075 row ("Light falloff"). **Not verified:** GPU time against the stop-gap, a camera that draws to part of the target (split screen). Follow-ups ([to-do](../plans/2026-11-rendering-followups.md)). |
| [0080](../decisions/0080-warm-up-shader-pipelines-on-scene-load.md) Pipeline warm-up | Partial (Babylon backend, engine side) | `scene/warmUp.ts` (`warmSet`): a scene's MeshRenderers, nested prefab instances, prefabs its component data names, its `settings.preload` list, and the files the scripts name (`scene/scriptAssetRefs.ts`; set by the editor's `loadScripts` and, from `game.json`'s `assetRefs`, by the player). `SceneManager.load` loads their textures and models with the scene's and calls `RenderSurface.warmUp` before the swap; `SceneManager.preload(paths)` does it at runtime. `render/babylon/warmUp.ts`: `PipelineWarmer` draws batches of 6 meshes a frame at 1e-5 scale in front of the view camera in the frame graph's passes (blended copies for script-driven nodes, kept in step with their base's textures; waits for loading textures). `VariantReport` logs mid-game variants with node, prefab and define changes in editor play mode (`setVariantReport(engine, on)`). Also fixed: renderer override copies follow a base texture that loads after them. Pirates match: after the load, 0 frames over 100 ms, 3 over 50 ms (worst 80 ms), 2 pipelines after 10 s; the load's frames 260–520 ms. Tests: `warmUp.test.ts` (scan, warm set, warmer on NullEngine). **Editor support (2026-11-06):** with no node selected (or after clicking the scene's name in the Hierarchy), the Inspector shows **Scene settings** with the preload list: add a prefab or material (suggested from the project's files) or remove one, each an undoable `SetScenePreload` that keeps the file's key order and drops empty keys (`editor/scenePreload.ts`, `document/commands.ts`); saving keeps `settings` as authored. The report's warning now names the file to add (the prefab, else the material asset; `variantWarning`), and the message log shows **Add to preload list** on it (then "On the preload list"). Tests: `scenePreload.test.ts` (kept on save, the command and its undo, add and remove, the warning's suggestion). Checked in the editor: the field, and the log's button adding a material from a warning. **Async compiles (2026-11-06, the budget question):** on WebGPU the warm-up's pipelines compile with `createRenderPipelineAsync` instead of stalling their frame (`render/babylon/asyncPipelines.ts`). While the warmer is busy, a mesh draw (`_draw`) whose pipeline isn't in Babylon's cache starts an async compile with the state Babylon has just set up for it (one per distinct state) and skips that draw; the pipeline goes into the cache when it's ready, and the warm-up resolves only once every compile has finished. Lookups from outside a mesh draw (the clear quad) pass through: a skip thrown there broke the frame graph for good. With no stall, batches are 24 meshes instead of 6. It relies on Babylon internals (`_cacheRenderPipeline`, `_lookupRenderPipeline`, `_buildRenderPipelineDescriptor`, `_setRenderPipeline`, `_draw`): re-check on each Babylon upgrade; without them it isn't installed and warming stalls as before. Pirates Practice load, 15 s from the click (one run each): blocking 24 pipelines, 5 frames over 100 ms, worst 600 ms; async 15 async and 7–9 blocking (Babylon's internal clear, post-process and UI pipelines), 3 frames over 100 ms, worst 240–280 ms. Every remaining long frame but one lined up with a main-thread long task (64–275 ms: the load's own JavaScript), none with a blocking compile; the variant report logged no missed variant. Tests: `asyncPipelines.test.ts` (against a stand-in cache and engine). **Batches sized by effect time (2026-11-06):** the load's long tasks were mostly Babylon creating effects (about 10 ms each, preprocessing the PBR shader in JavaScript), and 24 warm meshes at once made 220 ms tasks; batches now count Babylon meshes (a many-part model or a blend copy is several), start at 4 and grow or shrink so a batch spends about 12 ms creating effects (`effectClock`, `nextBatchSize` in `render/batchSize.ts`; `warmUp.test.ts`). Pirates load (one or two runs): frames over 100 ms 3 → 2, worst 240–280 → 200–220 ms. What remains: logging every effect the match World's engine created showed the warm-up covers the swap (no new effect after it drains; an earlier reading of a "swap lump" was wrong: those effects were made during the warm-up). A load makes about 22 PBR effects, now about one a frame, and each takes 20–60 ms of Babylon's JavaScript shader preprocessing, which sets the floor for a load frame. Profiled (16 PBR effects, 410 ms): our material plugins' code injection 2 ms, Babylon's WGSL processor about 49 ms, pipeline-context preparation 13 ms, and the rest, about 21 ms an effect, Babylon's core preprocessor expanding the PBR shader's includes and `#ifdef`s afresh for each set of defines; the match World's ~10 pass effects (post-process, blur, shadow map) are cheap. Instantiating the scene takes about 90 ms (some 1,000 vertex buffers). Each World keeps its own Babylon engine, by the owner's choice (isolation, clean teardown), so a match makes again the effects the menu World has; a cross-engine cache of preprocessed shader code is the shelved lever (the rendering plan, item 4). **More variants (2026-11-06 and 2026-11-07, the plan's item 5):** the warm set (`warmSet`) also takes the particle effects its ParticleSystems play (asset paths and embedded effects; scripts may name `.particles.json` files, as may `settings.preload` and `SceneManager.preload`) and whether it has a world-space UI canvas; `RenderSurface.warmUp` takes the set (`WarmContent`). The warmer draws custom items besides meshes (`WarmCustom`): a particle effect's CPU draws, built by `BabylonParticles.warmDraws` with one particle each at the warm point, 1e-5 m (Babylon particle systems with the effect's blending and sheets, mesh-mode thin instances, trail ribbons, material-asset quads; retried while a texture, material or model loads, and the particle player loads on demand), and a world canvas's plane with the GUI's material (`warmWorldCanvas` in `ui.ts`, shared with `createWorldCanvas`). A warm batch also draws the renderer's probe copies of each built-in material (`probeVariants`). Warm meshes are drawn instanced or thin-instanced as their material says, so the variants match the real draws (0108), and every `variants: "all"` keyword combination of a material is drawn too, capped at 16 (0106). Everything warmed so far (kept by `warmKey`, so a second load doesn't queue it twice) is warmed again when the tier changes a setting pipelines depend on (`pipelineTierKey` in `core/graphics.ts`: anti-aliasing, shadows and cascades, shader level, post-processing, bloom, ambient occlusion, depth of field, probes; not the render scale or particle budget) or new probes capture (`PipelineWarmer.rewarm`; probes capture after the load's warm-up). Async compiles skip only the warmer's own draws (`PipelineWarmer.drawing`, set by wrapping each warm mesh's `_draw` and particle system's `render` on the instance), so content on screen, a real probe's capture or a tier change's visible meshes never lose a draw. The report also reads Babylon particle systems' effects and names particle draws (the ParticleSystem's node, its prefab or effect file) and world canvases. Tests: `warmUp.test.ts`, `warmUpVariants.test.ts` (the warm set's effects and world UI; custom items retried and freed; only warm draws marked; no double queueing; particle warm draws per mode, waiting for textures, material quads; the world canvas plane; the tier key). **Slots, probe blends, first frames, load tasks (2026-11-07, ADR 0120):** a warm mesh keeps its renderer's per-slot materials (`WarmMesh.materials`, from `MeshRenderer.materials`; their textures preload with the scene), and the warmer resolves each model mesh's material as `syncMesh` does (`WarmMaterials.slotMaterials`, the material library's, ADR 0127: `materials[i]`, then `material`, then the model's import settings, then its own, sharing `syncMesh`'s multi-materials), drawing every slot material's keyword copies and, for one-material meshes, probe copies; a multi-material's parts are varied on the whole mesh (a pipeline doesn't depend on which indices draw). Probe blend copies (ADR 0110): `BabylonProbes.warmHits` adds a blended hit for each probe that can blend (a `blendDistance` and a bigger overlapping box), so `MYCO_PROBE_BLEND` copies compile at load. A new World's first frames are held on the clear colour while every frame-graph draw whose pipeline is missing (content, Babylon's post-process passes, the screen UI layer) compiles asynchronously, until a frame draws complete once its textures, models and UI have loaded, at most 3 s (`FirstFrames`, `AsyncPipelines.skippedShown`). Babylon's include expansion is memoized in our patch (`ProcessIncludes` in `shaderProcessor.js`), and the screen UI's canvas is made with `willReadFrequently` (`readableCanvas`: the first readback had Chrome move the canvas off the GPU, a 436 ms task). Pirates Practice load (15 s from the click; headless Chromium on the real GPU through D3D11 with FXC, 3 runs before, 4 after): match World pipelines compiled blocking 18 → 6–7 (async 7 → 19), worst frame 0.9–1.35 s → 150–270 ms, long tasks 1.7–2.4 s → 0.7–1.5 s in all with the worst 0.9–1.35 s → 150–250 ms, effect creation about 560 → 360 ms; the match World shows its clear colour about 0.6 s longer. A cross-engine cache of processed shaders was measured and dropped: 4 of the match World's 30 PBR-sized shader modules repeat an earlier World's code. Tests: `warmUp.test.ts` (slot materials in the warm set; a model's slots drawn, varied, multi-material submeshes, no probe copies of its parts), `babylonProbes.test.ts` (`warmHits`), `asyncPipelines.test.ts` (`skippedShown`, `FirstFrames`), `warmUpChecks.test.ts` (the variant report naming node, prefab and define change once, quiet for warm meshes, helpers and after a drain; override copies following a late base texture; `readableCanvas`; the include memo, invalidated by a light count or include change). **Kernel emitters (2026-11-07, ADR 0126):** `warmDraws` plans the effect as the World does: CPU systems warm as before, and each kernel system gets a kernel effect stepped once with nothing spawned (its simulation pipeline, the dead list's spawn pass and the trail history pass) and its output draws (`KernelDraw`, trail strips, `KernelMaterialDraw` with its material copy), updated once so a sorted output builds its sort passes, returned as meshes; disposing them frees the runs (`particleKernelMaterials.test.ts`). The kernels' WebGPU compute pipelines compile synchronously there (at load rather than on first play). **Not yet:** checking the new kinds in the editor on WebGPU; Babylon's texture tools (the engine's mipmap pipelines, the environment's cube mipmaps, prefilter and RGBD decode) still compile blocking, once per World; a scene load in the same World that changes the frame graph's layout compiles the new post-process passes blocking at the swap; seen once in 5 runs: the wreck or ship `Hull` reported "not warmed up" in a slow run (not investigated; timing, likely older). **Checked by hand only:** the held first frames (the match draws complete with its UI after the hold, in the headless runs' screenshots). Follow-ups ([to-do](../plans/2026-11-rendering-followups.md)). |
| [0081](../decisions/0081-remove-the-threejs-render-backend.md) Remove the three.js render backend | Done (steps 1–4) | **Step 1, the toggle (2026-11-06):** `render/createRenderSurface.ts` always makes a `BabylonSurface`; `rendererChoice`, `storedRendererChoice` and `setStoredRendererChoice` are gone, with the editor's Settings > General > Renderer field; `render/previewRouting.ts` loads only the Babylon previews. An old `?renderer=three` link or `localStorage["myco.renderer"]` is ignored (checked in the editor: the Playground draws on Babylon with `?renderer=three`). Tests: `rendererChoice.test.ts` removed; `babylonPreviewRouting.test.ts` (Babylon previews, even with `?renderer=three`); `handView.test.ts` fakes `BabylonSurface` instead of `RenderBackend`. **Step 2, the maths (2026-11-06):** `physics/Physics3DWorld.ts`, `render/gizmoMath.ts` (now `localPoseFromMatrix`, `matrixFrom`, `composeMatrix`, in double precision), `render/babylon/editorGizmo.ts` (drags and snapping) and `engine/editor/index.ts` (`localPoseUnder`, `worldBounds`, `localPositionFor`) use `core/math`: `Mat4` takes `Float64Array` too, and an internal `Quat` (rotate, conjugate, axis-angle) is beside it, not on the public `Quaternion`. Checked in the editor: a snapped move (x to 3.25) and a snapped 15° turn write clean values. Tests: `quatMat4.test.ts`; `gizmo.test.ts` and `babylonEditorGizmo.test.ts` pass unchanged in substance; physics tests pass. `render/picking.ts` and `ui/UIRuntime.ts` served only the three.js backend and went with it in step 3. **Step 3a, the backend (2026-11-06):** deleted `render/RenderBackend.ts`, `ThumbnailRenderer.ts`, `MaterialPreview.ts`, `PostProcessStack.ts`, `cascadedShadows.ts`, `reflectionProbes.ts`, `fog.ts`, `environmentMap.ts`, `picking.ts`, `water/WaterRenderer.ts`, `water/waterShader.ts` (and `gerstnerGLSL`), `shaderGraph/threeMaterial.ts`, the quarks player (`particles/quarksEffect.ts`, `ParticleRenderer.ts`, `EffectPreview.ts`) and `ui/UIRuntime.ts`; removed `postprocessing`, `n8ao`, `three.quarks` and `@pmndrs/uikit`. Moved first: the post-effect types and `stackKey` to `render/postEffects.ts`, the preview types to `render/previewTypes.ts`, the editor's gizmo and overlay types to `RenderSurface.ts`. Tests: deleted those of the three.js backend (`cascadedShadows`, `fog`, `picking`, `particles`, the three.js parts of `ui`, `water`, `shaderGraph`, `physicalGraph` and `babylonWater`); ported `lightGizmo`, `environment` and `reflectionProbes` to their Babylon or neutral twins; `postProcessStack.test.ts` became `postEffectsKey.test.ts`. `npm run build` passes; the full suite had only `lobby.test.ts` failing (already failing on master). Checked in the editor: Flappy, the Playground (particles) and a pirates practice match (UI, water, HUD) play with no console errors. Older rows below may still name three.js backend files: those parts are history since 0081. **Step 3b, GLSL out of the shader graph (2026-11-06):** `render/shaderGraph/compile.ts` writes only WGSL (no `language` option or result field; `glslFloat` is `floatLiteral`; the GLSL built-ins, varyings, uniform lines and Custom Function path are gone), `nodes.ts` has no GLSL helper table or `c.language` branches (`HelperName` is its own union), `wgsl.ts` loses `ShaderLanguage`, and `graphMaterial.ts` no longer checks the language. (Hand-written shader assets can ship GLSL for WebGL2, ADR 0106; graphs stay WGSL-only.) The listing (`shaderGraphListing`) describes the WGSL the plugin splices in, behind the Shader Graph panel's WGSL button (was GLSL). Custom Function: `wgsl` is the code; `body` (GLSL) is optional, kept in old files and written back only when present, shown read-only as "Old GLSL body" with a remove button; a node with only GLSL is reported as needing WGSL; new nodes start with a WGSL body. Checked: a temporary test compiled every sample graph, every node preview of them and every node type with both this compiler and the previous commit's WGSL mode, and the vertex, fragment, uniforms and problems were identical; the Shader Graph panel shows the WGSL listing and the Custom Function editor. Tests rewritten to WGSL: `shaderGraph.test.ts` (plus the kept GLSL `body` round trip), `shaderGraphWgsl.test.ts`, `shaderSubGraphs.test.ts`, `qualityBranch.test.ts`; `babylonGraphMaterial.test.ts` loses the GLSL refusal test. Seen, not from this change: the ripple sub-graph's UV node preview draws black. **Step 4, the boundary (2026-11-06):** `threeImports.test.ts` fails if any module but the model loaders imports three.js (tests excepted: `scene/models.ts`, and `scene/gltf.ts` since 0088), if `models.ts` imports more of it than the OBJ, MTL and FBX loaders, or if `postprocessing`, `n8ao`, `three.quarks` or `@pmndrs/uikit` come back in the sources or `package.json`. The architecture overview, the UI guide and `CLAUDE.md` describe Babylon.js as the renderer; 0021 (three.quarks) is superseded by 0081 and 0057 amended. Only `scene/models.ts` and `scene/gltf.ts` (and their tests) import `three` now. **Open:** the player still needs `three` for OBJ, MTL and FBX files that aren't baked: 0090 bakes models at export (`.mycomesh`, no three.js load), but the editor and an unbaked project load it on first model use. |

| [0103](../decisions/0103-tilemaps.md) Tilemaps | Partial | **Amended by [0392](../decisions/0392-tiles-have-stable-uuids-and-tilemap-layers-name-them-through-a-palette.md)**: maps name tiles by stable uuid through each layer's palette (layer version 2), so a deleted tile's id being reused no longer repaints maps. **Amended by [0400](../decisions/0400-tile-variants-are-chosen-by-a-hash-of-the-cell-and-a-per-tilemap-seed.md)**: every variant choice (auto-tiling ties, the random brush, `fillRect` with several ids) is `cellRandom(variantSeed, x, y, stream)` (`runtime/cellHash.ts`), so painting keeps neighbours' variants and the same strokes give the same map; `Tilemap.variantSeed`, `Tilemap.retileTerrain()`, and the Inspector's Re-roll variants button (`rerollVariants` in `editor/brush.ts`, one undo step); tests `runtime/variants.test.ts`, `editor/brush.test.ts`. `src/plugins/tilemaps/runtime/` (moved from `src/engine/tilemap/`, ADR 0136): `*.tileset.json` assets (tiles drawing sidecar slices, collision, custom data, terrains with neighbour patterns, weights), the `Tilemap` component (several layers with sorting layers, tint, opacity; 16 × 16 run-length chunks; per-layer versions and migrations), auto-tiling (terrain/rule patterns, 16-tile and 47-blob sets), brushes, the scripting API (`getTile`/`setTile`/`getTileData`/`paintTerrain`/`worldToCell`/`cellToWorld`/`onTileChanged`, `TilemapChanges`), chunked rendering (`tilemapRenderer.ts`, one quad batch per chunk and texture, rebuilt when changed, drawn by `render/babylon/quadBatches.ts`, ADR 0136). Editor: Create > Tilemap, scene-view painting (the Tilemaps editor half, `src/plugins/tilemaps/editor/` `paintTool.ts`/`brush.ts`/`palette.ts`, a viewport tool since ADR 0138: pencil, rectangle, fill, erase, eyedropper/stamps, random, flips; one undo step per stroke), the Tile Palette panel, tileset assets and inspector. Sample: `samples/platformer/`. Tests: `plugins/tilemaps/runtime/tilemap.test.ts`, `tilemapRenderer.test.ts`, `engine/tests/babylonQuadBatches.test.ts`, `platformerSample.test.ts`. | [0082](../decisions/0082-editor-start-up-loading-order.md) Editor start-up loading order | Done (Proposed) | **Desktop**: `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` marks the editor window `create: false`; `editor/launcher/editorWindowStart.ts` creates it from that entry after the Projects window's first paint and an idle callback (or at once when a project is picked; `launcher/main.tsx` wraps the hand-off link); capability `core:webview:allow-create-webview-window`; `launcher/editorWindow.ts` destroys the editor window if the Projects window is gone. **Browser**: `editor/main.tsx` renders the start screen from its own few modules, then loads `App`, shortcuts, the panel registry and the session as dynamic imports (start actions and `window.openTestProject` wait for the session). **Prefetch**: `editor/prefetch.ts` (`prefetchEngine`: renderer, editor tools, particles, UI parsers; `prefetchWhenIdle`: then `typescript` and both physics engines); `session.loadProject` starts `prefetchEngine` before compiling scripts. **Renderer ready**: `RenderSurface.started` (optional; `BabylonSurface` resolves it when `BabylonRenderer.create` finishes) and `session.sceneViewStarted()` (4 s cap), awaited before the desktop editor window shows. **Dev server**: `vite.config.ts` `optimizeDeps.include: ["preact/jsx-runtime"]` (no cold-cache reload) and `server.warmup` of both entries. `vite.shared.ts` `engineTypes()` caches its declaration emit in `node_modules/.cache/myco-types.json` (first request after a restart 27 s → 0.5 s). Measured (Chromium, simulated Tauri): Projects card 1.6 s → 0.4 s (server restart), 7.8 s → 4.0 s (cold); browser start card 0.9 s → 0.3 s (dev), FCP ~250 → ~95 ms (prod). Tests: `editor/launcher/editorWindowStart.test.ts` (config and options). Not verified by an automated test: the real Tauri windows (creation from JS, the hidden window's loading, the close race) — check by hand on Windows. **three.js out of start-up**: `scene/builtinMeshes.ts` (built-in meshes without three.js; `tests/builtinMeshes.test.ts` checks them equal to three.js r187's), `scene/models.ts` imports three.js types only and loads it in `parseModel`; `ModelMesh.geometry` optional; `engine/editor/index.ts` bounds and triangles from `data`; `tests/threeImports.test.ts` checks no static value import. The player no longer preloads `three.core` (197 KB). |

Replaces `lazyPanel` in `App.tsx`. Tests: `editor/lazyPanels.test.ts` (placeholder, single load, preload, failure and retry, dialogs, registry covers every panel, visible panels). |

Tests: `assetImportFiles.test.ts`, `osDropController.test.ts` (0324). Not verified: a real Tauri window. Missing: a progress indicator for large drops (files are read fully into memory). |

Editor: `addInnerNode` and `writeRemoveNode` (`editor/prefabs.ts`, `editor/overrideWriter.ts` since ADR 0287), used by the Hierarchy's delete (`deleteSelection.ts`) and create (`nodeCreate.ts`) for rows inside an instance; Apply writes the added and removed nodes into the prefab.
