| [0087](../decisions/0087-multi-node-create-prefab-and-apply.md) Multi-node Create Prefab and Apply | Done (Proposed) | **Amended by [0401](../decisions/0401-create-prefab-keeps-references-to-scene-nodes-as-instance-overrides.md)** (references out of the new prefab become instance overrides). `createPrefabFromNodes` (`editor/prefabs.ts`): several sibling nodes become one prefab whose root is a new "Group" node, one instance replaces them where the first stood, one undo step; refuses nodes with different parents, and nodes outside the selection may reference only the new root. `applyOverridesTo` writes each selected instance's overrides into its prefab. Both are buttons on the multi-selection Inspector (`ui/Inspector.tsx`), which draws them from `prefabActions` and runs them through `createPrefabFrom` / `applyPrefabOverrides` in `editor/nodeEdits.ts` (November 2026: the rules left the view; Create Prefab on non-siblings is now disabled with the reason instead of failing on click). Tests: `editor/prefabs.test.ts` ("multi-node prefabs": group creation, refusal), `editor/nodeEdits.test.ts` (which actions are offered and why not, read-only included; Create Prefab single and multi; Apply on one and on several instances; Revert All in one undo step). Missing: naming the prefab and Group node in a dialog. |
| [0106](../decisions/0106-hand-written-shaders-keywords-and-variants.md) Hand-written shaders, keywords, variants | Partial (Proposed) | **Shader assets** `*.shader.json` (`myco.shader@1`, `scene/shaderAsset.ts`): target lit/unlit, surface, doubleSided, graph-format properties, keywords, WGSL and/or GLSL code from `.wgsl`/`.glsl` files or inline. `render/shaderGraph/shaderAsset.ts` compiles one to the graph's compiled form (surface function contract in its header; WGSL plus a GLSL block), so the graph material plugin draws it with lights, shadows (displaced too), fog, overrides and warm-up; the plugin injects GLSL on WebGL2 for shader assets that ship it (graphs stay WebGPU-only). Code files are material dependencies (edits rebuild). **Keywords** (`scene/shaderKeywords.ts`): boolean and enum, `KW_` defines set by the plugin (`prepareDefines`), so each combination is a Babylon effect, cached and shared; graphs get a `keywords` list and a Keyword node (constant under `#ifdef` + `select`); sub-graphs declare their own keywords, added to the using shader once with the outer graph's same-named keyword winning, and the Material inspector lists the compiled keywords (sub-graphs' included; `shaderSubGraphs.test.ts`); material assets pick values (`keywords`); `variants: "all"` keywords are warmed in every combination (max 16). **Custom Function files** (`function.file`, `.wgsl`). `.wgsl`/`.glsl` are text assets in the editor and the player. **Editor**: Keywords in the Shader Graph side bar, keyword select for Keyword nodes, Material inspector shader assets with property and keyword editing, New Shader (JSON + WGSL + GLSL templates). **Sample**: `samples/playground3d/shaders/rim.shader.json` (+ `.wgsl`, `.glsl`) with `materials/rim.material.json`, not placed in a scene. Tests: `shaderAssets.test.ts` (format, keywords, compile, GLSL anchors against Babylon's sources, keyword variants on the GLSL NullEngine), `customFunctionFiles.test.ts`, `warmUp.test.ts`, editor `graphEdit.test.ts`, `materialAssets.test.ts`. Not checked on a GPU: the sample's WGSL/GLSL compile only in a browser. Missing: a raw (non-surface) shader target, a code editor with inline compile errors. |
| [0107](../decisions/0107-script-material-api-shared-and-instance.md) Script material API | Done (Proposed) | `Material` (`render/Material.ts`, exported): `Material.shared(path)`, `new Material(path)`, `clone()`, `setColor/Float/Vector/Texture`, getters, `clear`, `clearAll`, `setKeyword/getKeyword/isKeywordEnabled/clearKeyword`. `MeshRenderer.sharedMaterial` (get/set) and `materialInstance` (copy on first access) are slot 0; `getSharedMaterial(slot)`, `setSharedMaterial(slot, m)`, `getMaterialInstance(slot)` and `sharedMaterials` index slots, reading and writing 0089's `materials[]`. The renderer resolves each slot through its script Material (own `materials[i]` entry), else the renderer-wide `material` (slot 0's instance and 0049 overrides), the sidecar map, then the model's own; multi-slot materials are shared per slot tuple, so matching renderers still batch (0108). Renderer: shared values applied in place to the asset's Babylon material (built-ins reset to the asset's settings first; graphs and shader assets take properties and keywords), instances get their own copy (graph copies share the program) disposed once no renderer draws them, 0049 overrides follow base changes. The editor resets shared Materials when play stops. Tests: `materialApi.test.ts` (on a NullEngine renderer). Not replicated or saved, by design. Missing: `Material.fromShader(path)` (a Material from a shader asset with no material asset). |
| [0108](../decisions/0108-gpu-instancing-sprite-batching-pixel-perfect-camera.md) Instancing, sprite batching, pixel-perfect camera | Done (Proposed) | `render/babylon/meshBatches.ts`: MeshRenderer meshes are `InstancedMesh`es of a hidden source per geometry + drawn material (after probes and overrides) + shadow settings; material `"instancing": "thin"` packs a batch into one thin-instance buffer (swap-remove slots, refilled when moved; under the per-object draw policy, ADR 0129, they draw as instances instead, for picking), `"off"` keeps a mesh each. Casters, editor queries and the frame graph take `AbstractMesh`. Warm-up draws warm meshes instanced/thin as their material says. `render/babylon/layeredQuads.ts` (was `spriteBatches.ts`, ADR 0186): sprites batch per texture run (instance colour for the tint, a GLSL/WGSL plugin for slice UVs), refilled only when a sprite changed; sprites keep a mesh each under `RenderPolicy.drawPolicy: "perObject"`, which the editor's viewport handle sets the moment the editor asks for a World's viewport (scene view, editor play mode; ADR 0129), not when the editor tools happen to load. `Camera.pixelPerfect` / `pixelSnapping` (`render/pixelPerfect.ts`); zoom and snapping count whole device pixels on the surface's grid, `RenderSurface.pixelGrid()` (CSS size × `appliedPixelRatio`: screen ratio capped at 2 × render scale; ADR 0154). Tests: `instancing.test.ts`, `spriteBatching.test.ts`, `pixelPerfect.test.ts`, `warmUp.test.ts`, e2e `shaderassets.spec.ts`; benchmark `bench/instancing.bench.ts` (NullEngine, N=4000: mesh frame 31 ms off → 9.8 ms instanced, 7.5 ms thin; sprites 41 ms → 2.3 ms batched). `renderStats().drawCalls` now reads Babylon's counter. Missing: static batching. Lit sprites: ADR 0399. |
| [0094](../decisions/0094-gpu-particle-simulation-backends.md) Kernel particle backends | Partial (Proposed) | **Kernels** (`scene/particleKernel.ts`): systems → init/update/output statements over an expression IR (constants, uniform slots, attributes, stable randoms, baked curve/gradient rows, operator nodes), uniform bindings evaluated on the CPU each frame (all live), a 128-sample look-up table, 16 floats of state per particle, `chooseBackend` (auto/cpu/gpu; auto picks a kernel for per-particle chains, Set Attribute, several outputs, GPU events, depth collision or 4096+ particles when every block runs there). **Executors** (`render/particles/gpu/`): `kernelJs.ts` (closures, no eval), `webgpuRun.ts` (WGSL compute on a raw GPUDevice, ping-pong storage buffers, explicit bind group layout), `webgl2Run.ts` (GLSL ES 3.0 full-screen pass into four RGBA32F targets, ping-pong), all generated from one KSL source in `kernelShader.ts` with shared hashes (`kernelMath.ts`); `spawner.ts` (CPU spawning into a ring, alive estimate), `kernelEffect.ts` (per-instance runtime: uniforms, look-up table re-bakes on property overrides, GPU events parent-first, prewarm). **Babylon** (`render/babylon/gpuParticles.ts`): runs on Babylon's device or GL context (WebGL2 runs `wipeCaches` after), the JavaScript executor uploads to textures or storage buffers, one instanced mesh per output (`forcedInstanceCount`, WGSL or GLSL `ShaderMaterial` reading the state in the vertex shader: billboard, stretched, horizontal, vertical, mesh; flipbooks; blending; render order), `kernelExecutors` (WebGPU → WebGL2 with float targets → JavaScript). `particles.ts` plans each effect (CPU systems stay on `EffectSimulation`, kernel ones are disabled there; event parents and children kept on one side), drives commands, routes, emit, counts and destroy-on-finish, and hides CPU-only problems for kernel systems. **Checked:** `npm run bench:particles` (bench/particles): WebGPU and WebGL2 match JavaScript on live particles exactly and to 0.25% on values after 90 frames, with a GPU-event child; the WebGL2 path draws in the real engine (screenshot); frame times in `bench/particles/README.md` (SwiftShader). Tests: `particleKernel.test.ts` (compiler, choice, JS executor: ring, CPU parity under gravity, death flag, operators, Set Attribute, several outputs, look-up tables, GPU events, determinism, spaces, maths), `babylonKernelParticles.test.ts` (NullEngine: planning, draws, state, commands, problems, disposal). **Not verified:** WebGPU drawing on screen (headless WebGPU canvases don't present here; the WGSL draw compiles without errors), any real-GPU performance, depth collision and soft particles on screen. **2026-11-07 (ADR 0126):** **dead list** on WebGPU and JavaScript (`kdead`/`kcount` with i32 atomics and a `spawn` compute entry point in `wgslSimulation(kernel, { deadList })`; `JsKernel` keeps a free stack): a full system stops spawning instead of replacing its oldest particles; seeds hash the spawn number; `KernelRun.alive` is exact (JavaScript at once, WebGPU through two staging buffers read without waiting, a frame or two late while the GPU keeps up), `KernelEffect.count` uses it; WebGL2 keeps the ring and the estimate; GPU-event children keep the slot mapping. **Sorting** (`render/particles/gpu/sort.ts`, the network in `bitonic.ts`): `settings.sort` auto sorts alpha-blended outputs; a key pass and a bitonic network over the next power of two (WebGPU compute, WebGL2 RG32F passes, a JavaScript sort), instance i reads slot `kidx[i]`, once a frame per system for the active camera (`sortRunOnce`). **Trails:** trail outputs compile (`KernelSystem.trail` points, 2–64, at most 4 M in all; Width over Length baked to a look-up row), each slot's (position, seed) history is written by the update (WebGPU, JavaScript) or a second pass (WebGL2, `GLSL_TRAIL_HISTORY`), and the draw is an instanced strip whose vertex shader finds the particle's own points by a binary search on the seed tag. **Material assets** (`render/babylon/kernelMaterial.ts`): non-trail outputs draw an unshared copy of a PBR material (built-in assets and shader graphs; `BabylonParticleHost.materialCopy`, graphs rebuilt with `createGraphMaterial`) with `MycoKernelParticlePlugin`, which reads the particle from textures (`materialVertexCode`: WebGPU copies its buffer into one RGBA32F texture, `copyBufferToTexture`; uniforms and the look-up table from CPU copies) and sets position, normal, UV and vertex colour, overriding the instanced world matrix at `CUSTOM_VERTEX_UPDATE_WORLDPOS`; its define carries a hash of the code (Babylon shares effects by defines); other materials and trails draw with the texture (reported). Kernel draws also take height fog (`useHeightFog`). **Checked in the in-app browser (real GPU, 2026-11-07):** `bench/particles/parity.html` WebGPU and WebGL2 match JavaScript (ring), WebGPU dead list matches by seed with an exact count, both GPU sorts give the JavaScript order, both trail histories match; `index.html?trail=12&blend=alpha&soft=0.3` compiles the sorted, soft, fogged quad and trail draws on WebGPU, WebGL2 and both JavaScript paths, and trails draw on screen (WebGL2); `&material=1` draws lit, tinted PBR particles on WebGL2 and places them on WebGPU (that page's lighting saturates every PBR material on WebGPU, a plain ground too, so lit colour there isn't judged). Tests: `particleDeadList.test.ts`, `particleSort.test.ts`, `particleTrails.test.ts`, `particleKernelMaterials.test.ts`. **Not verified:** material-asset kernel particles lit on WebGPU in a World, trails on screen on WebGPU, real-GPU cost of sorting large systems. Missing: per-camera sort order for split screen. **2026-11-20 (ADR 0251):** material assets on trail outputs are built, and every system runs on a kernel (see the 0251 row). |

| [0096](../decisions/0096-soft-particles-collisions-seed-and-particle-materials.md) Soft particles, collisions, seed, particle colour | Partial (Proposed) | **Soft particles:** `OutputSettings.softness` (format, editor control), `SceneDepth` (the shared scene depth of ADR 0109 through `requestSceneDepth`/`sceneDepth`, bound per camera while drawing; Babylon's depth renderer with camera-space Z for scenes without a frame graph, such as the Particle Graph preview), fade in the kernel draw's fragment shader. **Collisions:** `update.collisionDepth` (kernel `k_depthCollide`), `update.collisionColliders` (CPU, `SimulationOptions.raycast` from a World service that answers rays (3D physics, `_worldRaycast`), bounce, friction, radius, kill; soft billboards draw as mesh quads (`drawsAsSystem`), and `MycoSoftParticlePlugin` (`render/babylon/softParticles.ts`, WGSL and GLSL) fades the unlit particle material or a copy of a material asset by the scene depth (`SceneDepth`). **Height fog**: `heightFog.ts` exports `heightFogOn`, `heightFogShader(language)` and `useHeightFog(ShaderMaterial, scene)` (the define follows the scene, uniforms bound each draw); Tests: `particleSoft.test.ts`, `particleKernelMaterials.test.ts`. Not verified on screen: soft CPU particles, height fog on particles. |

| [0118](../decisions/0118-gltf-pivots-lights-cameras-layer-textures-and-variants.md) Node pivots, glTF lights/cameras, material features, variants | Done (Proposed) | **Pivots**: sidecar `pivots` (`"node"` default, `"model"`; `importSettings.ts`, Asset Inspector "Pivots"); every `ModelMesh` records its node's model-space `pose` and `ModelData.nodes` the group nodes, lights and cameras (`models.ts` `bakeMeshes`); `asset:m.glb#Part` resolves to a node-space copy (`nodeSpaceMesh`, through `selectMeshes`, so rendering, physics, navigation and bounds agree), `#Part#model` pins model space (`MODEL_SPACE_SUFFIX`), whole-model references stay in model space; the drop (`editor/assetBrowser.ts` `modelNodeEntries`, via `modelTreeIfLoaded`) gives nodes their local poses, shared levels folded in. **Scene format 2** (`SceneLoader.CURRENT_FORMAT_VERSION`): `scene/pivotMigration.ts` pins version 1 FBX/glTF part references (MeshRenderer and MeshCollider data and prefab overrides) with `#model` unless the sidecar opted into node pivots; OBJ left alone; samples rewritten to `@2` (playground robot parts pinned). **Lights and cameras** (`scene/gltf.ts` `tagLightsAndCameras`, `engineLight`, `engineCamera`): `KHR_lights_punctual` lights become `engine.Light` nodes (linear colour to sRGB, intensity as is, range and spot cone/softness; range times the import scale), cameras `engine.Camera` nodes (fov, ortho size, near/far, no `zfar` → 1000) with the Camera **disabled** and one editor message per drop. **glTF layer textures and anisotropy**: `SurfaceInfo.layerMaps` and `physical` extras; `KHR_materials_anisotropy` supported. **Material assets** (`scene/material.ts` `MATERIAL_MAPS`): `normalScale`, `emissionMap`, `emissionIntensity`, `metalRoughMap`, `occlusionMap`/`occlusionStrength`, `alphaMode`/`alphaCutoff`, and per-layer maps, attenuation and iridescence thickness range on physical; `render/babylon/materials.ts` `builtinTextureSlots`/`setMaterialMap` (shared with glTF surfaces) in `BabylonRenderer.builtinMaterial` and the previews; scene preload includes them; Material Inspector rows. **Extraction** (`editor/materialExtract.ts`) keeps all of it (lost: textures on the second UV set). **Physical graph inputs** (`shaderGraph/nodes.ts` `OUTPUT_INPUTS`, `compile.ts`, `graphMaterial.ts` `physicalCustomCode`, `CLEARCOAT_NORMAL_ANCHOR`): Clearcoat Normal, Specular Color, Iridescence Thickness, Anisotropy and Anisotropy Direction. **Variants** (`gltf.ts` `loadVariants`): `ModelData.variants`, `ModelMesh.variantSurfaces`; `MeshRenderer.variant`/`variants`, slot material assets winning; Inspector dropdown, hidden without variants (since ADR 0152 a field-choices contribution of the Meshes plugin). **Baked models** (`bakedModel.ts`, version 2) carry poses, nodes, variants and layer textures. Tests: `engine/tests/gltfNodesVariants.test.ts` (poses, node space and `#model`, pivots setting, the format 1 → 2 migration, lights/cameras, layer maps, variants, baked round trip, Babylon mapping), `engine/tests/materialMaps.test.ts` (material keys parse/save, slots on Babylon, alpha/emission/attenuation, graph inputs injected into Babylon's own PBR shader), `editor/gltfDrop.test.ts` (drop with node pivots, model pivots, lights, disabled camera and message), `editor/materialExtract.test.ts` (lossless extraction); `models`, `gltf`, `bakedModel`, `rewrite` and format tests moved to `#model` / `@2`. Not verified by a test: how the new maps, graph inputs and variants look on screen; the Inspector rows and dropdowns (not opened in the editor). Missing: a per-texture UV set on material assets; a command converting pinned parts to node pivots; warming up variant materials (0120). |
| [0121](../decisions/0121-interest-management-and-ghost-component-changes.md) Interest management, ghost component changes | Done (Proposed) | **Amended by [0402](../decisions/0402-ghost-creation-goes-reliably-apart-from-per-tick-state.md)**: leaves and relayouts go in reliable lifecycle messages, once. `net/interest.ts` (`Interest`: groups, viewer, `isRelevant`; public), `Replicated.relevantDistance`/`group`; `net/replication/server.ts` (`interestFrame`, `relevantTo`, leaves and dormant scene ghosts, `updateLayouts` and the relayout section, `siblingOrder`, `disabledOf`, baselines tied to a layout version), `client.ts` (`leave`, `relayout`, `matchComponents`, `applyOrder` rebuilding each parent's ghost order once per frame, enabled flags), `wire.ts` (`Structure.after`/`disabled`, `writeSpec`/`readSpec`), `strings.ts` (strings resent on re-entry); protocol 5. Tests `engine/tests/replicationInterest.test.ts` (groups under loss, owner and per-player groups, argument checks, distance with hysteresis, `setViewer`, a scene ghost leaving, coming back and despawning while away, child ghosts following their parent, strings on re-entry, irrelevant ghosts costing nothing, enabled state under loss and for a late joiner, sibling order under loss, components added/changed/removed under loss, a late joiner's added and removed components, a ghost bouncing out and back within a round trip (late acks of its earlier stay ignored, so no baseline or spawn ack from before its return counts), a prefab ghost destroyed while its leave was lost (a despawn is sent for every ghost that left, so clients drop its node and strings)). Proposed updates, not built: O19–O23 in [open-questions.md](open-questions.md). Not checked by hand in the editor or with the Arena sample in a browser. |
| [0122](../decisions/0122-rename-and-deactivate-inside-instances-and-open-prefab-from-an-instance.md) Inner-node rename/active, Open Prefab | Done (Proposed) | **Rename and deactivate inside an instance:** the Inspector's name field and Active box work on inner nodes (single and multi-selection, through `setNodesActive` / `renameNode` / `revertNameAndActive` in `editor/nodeEdits.ts`, tested in `nodeEdits.test.ts`), writing `{ target, name }` / `{ target, active }` overrides through the override writer (`writeProperty` in `overrideWriter.ts`, ADR 0287; `SetInnerNodeProperty` and `nodePropertyCommand` until then), removed when the value matches the prefab's (prefab file plus nested instances' overrides, `innerBaseValue`); overridden ones are marked, with ↺ to revert. `WorldSync` writes the name or active flag on the World node (rebuilding the instance when the override goes). **Hierarchy:** `innerNodes` / `instanceInnerNodes` apply the instance's overrides (names, active, `addNodes`, `removeNode`) and nested instances' own; inactive inner rows are dimmed. It also fixes inner-node paths below the first level (`[instance, nodeId]`, as the loader keys them, no longer `[instance, parent, nodeId]`) and `ancestorKeys` for them. **Apply** writes non-root name/active overrides into plain prefab nodes (`active: true` removes the key) and keeps them as overrides on nested instances; the instance root's own stay. **Open Prefab** (Inspector button on instances and inner nodes, Hierarchy right-click item, double-click on the prefab badge; `prefabTabs.ts`, `prefabTarget` in `prefabs.ts`) opens the prefab in its scene tab with the matching node selected, and a bar over the scene view leads back (one level at a time). Tests: `src/editor/prefabInstanceEdits.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/prefab-instances.spec.ts` (Chromium and WebKit). Not covered: editing in context (the scene greyed out behind the prefab), which the record leaves for later. |

Tests: `src/editor/liveEdits.test.ts`; e2e `tests/e2e/playdebug.spec.ts` (Chromium and WebKit). Not covered: deleting ghosts or nodes scripts still hold (behaves as the game would). |
| [0125](../decisions/0125-npm-packages-type-checks-and-reload-during-play.md) npm packages, type checks before Play, reload during play | Done (Proposed) | **npm packages** (`src/editor/packages.ts`, `projectPackages.ts`, `src/server/packages.ts`): a script's bare imports other than `"myco"` are bundled from the project folder's own `node_modules` with esbuild (`esbuild-wasm` 0.28.2, pinned; in the editor's page its WebAssembly build is loaded on first use in a worker, in Node its Node build). `PackageResolver` (`packageResolver.ts`) does Node resolution through the project's `FileSystem` (Tauri, File System Access or memory): `exports` maps via `resolve.exports` (conditions `browser`, `import`, `module`, `default`), then `browser`/`module`/`main`, extension and `index` probing, nested `node_modules` first, `browser` field replacements (`false` → an empty module); Node built-ins without one are refused. Every specifier is an entry point of one esbuild build with code splitting (shared dependencies once, `"myco"` external, `process.env.NODE_ENV` development in the editor and production in builds). The loader (`scriptLoader.ts`) loads the chunks as module URLs with their imports rewritten (the engine shim for `"myco"`), caches the bundle per project until the specifiers or `node_modules/.package-lock.json` (else `package.json`) change, and reports a missing package ("run npm install in the project folder"), a Node built-in or a bundling error against the scripts importing it only. Game builds write the chunks to `code/deps/` (`compileForBuild` in `src/export/webBuild.ts`; `npm run export` and the editor's Export… pass the project's packages) and the dedicated server loads them like the editor. A preload (`src/server/esbuildPrewarm.cjs`, through `NODE_OPTIONS`) warms up OpenSSL's random generator in esbuild's Node service process, which otherwise fails on Node 26 for Windows. **Package types:** `packageTypes` reads each package's declarations (`types`/`typings`/`exports` types, else `@types/<name>`) and the `.d.ts` files they reference (at most 3000), and the Code panel's TypeScript service gets them at `file:///node_modules/…` (`setPackageTypes` in `code/monaco.ts`); a package without declarations gets `declare module "<name>";`. **Type check before Play** (`scriptCheck.ts`): Play first asks Monaco's TypeScript service (loading it if needed) for each script's syntax and type errors, with the Code panel's strict settings and the text it holds (unsaved edits included); "Checking scripts…" shows meanwhile and a second Play waits. With errors, a dialog (`confirmInEditor` gained clickable items) lists the first 50 as `path:line: message`; clicking one opens the script in the Code panel at that line (`openScript(path, at)`, `revealAt`), **Play anyway** plays, and **Don't check before Play** turns the per-user `checkScriptsBeforePlay` preference off (also in Settings > General). A check that fails to run doesn't stop Play. **Reload during play:** saving a script (or a change on disk) while playing reloads scripts and swaps every World of the game (`swapScriptTypes`/`captureScriptTypes` in `src/engine/editor/`, `World._swapScriptTypes`): each component of a reloaded class is replaced in place by an instance of the class now registered under its id, with every own property copied except the engine's internal state, references to other swapped components re-pointed (directly and in arrays, `Map`s and `Set`s), static properties copied to the new class; the old instance gets `onDisable` and its waits and async flows are cancelled, the new one isn't awoken or started again and gets `onEnable`. Data component stores move to the redefined definitions keeping the shared fields; systems follow the catalog. Types that failed to load keep running their old code, and the status bar says how many components were swapped and which kept their old code. The Editor World rebuilds on Stop. **Tests:** `src/editor/packages.test.ts` (resolution, bundling ESM and CommonJS with a shared chunk, loading scripts that import packages, build output in `code/deps/`, package types and `@types`), `playReload.test.ts` (a headless game: properties, `@field`s, new fields, lifecycle calls, static and cross-component references, data rows, a new system body, a script that fails), `scriptCheck.test.ts` (the dialog's decisions with a stand-in checker). **Checked by hand** in the browser editor (dev server, `openTestProject` with an in-memory `node_modules`): esbuild-wasm bundles in the page, the check lists the one real type error (typed and untyped packages resolve), clicking it opens the script at the line, and saving a sample script during play swaps the Bird and its siblings with the game still running. **Not yet:** an automated test of the Monaco check (it needs a page), the desktop editor with a real `npm install`ed folder, a disk cache of bundles, type-checking in builds, reloading a code bootstrap. |

Not covered: visual checks of the previews and e2e. |

| [0187](../decisions/0187-the-babylon-editor-viewport-is-the-adapter-itself.md) The Babylon editor viewport is the adapter itself | Done | `src/engine/render/babylon/editorViewport.ts`: `BabylonEditorViewport implements ViewportAdapter`. | [0188](../decisions/0188-render-tests-build-renderers-the-way-callers-do-and-the-feature-host-offers-each-service-once.md) Render tests build renderers the way callers do; the feature host offers each service once | Done | **Host** `render/babylon/feature.ts`: `BabylonFeatureHost` without `probeLookup` and `drawPolicy()` (Water uses `probes.lookup`, Sprites `policy().drawPolicy`); `describeDrawn` (the variant report's naming, called by `BabylonRenderer.setVariantReport`). **Removed (test-only):** `BabylonRenderer.syncMeshes` and `meshes`, `MeshesFeatureApi.batchStats`, `BabylonMeshes.batchStats`/`batchSources`/`meshesOf`, `MeshBatches.stats`/`sources`, `SpriteBatcher.stats`, `BabylonWater.meshes`, `heightFogAmount`, `softFade` (their maths is checked again, on the shader code itself: `engine/tests/shaderMaths.ts` runs the GLSL and WGSL snippets `heightFogShader` and `MycoSoftParticlePlugin.getCustomCode` hand out on the CPU, in `heightFog.test.ts` and `particleSoft.test.ts`, 2026-11-16), `_resetGraphTextures`, `_resetTextMeasurer`, `SKY_SOURCES`, `lightSegments`, `AsyncPipelines.compiled`/`skipped`/`busy`, `_setAsyncPipelines`, `BabylonLighting.shadowState`, `BabylonFrame.state`, `KERNEL_BUFFER_USAGE`; `ThumbnailRenderer`'s constructor, `QuadDraw`/`InstanceDraw` buffers and `BabylonSky`'s bake counters are private. Comments describing a three.js backend fixed. **Tests:** `renderFeatureHooks.test.ts` and `babylonEditorViewport.test.ts` build with `forThumbnail`; `instancing.test.ts`, `spriteBatching.test.ts`, `babylonWater.test.ts` read the Babylon scene; `babylonPreviews.test.ts` uses `ThumbnailRenderer.create` with a mocked engine factory (`createEngine`, ADR 0244); light wireframe checks moved from `lightGizmo.test.ts` (deleted) into `babylonEditorDrawing.test.ts`; `asyncPipelines.test.ts` reads the fake engine; `babylonUI.test.ts` loads `ui.ts` afresh per canvas state. `bench/instancing.bench.ts` runs again (renderer `sync`, draw calls derived from the scene). **E2e** `shaderassets`, `shadergraph`, `shadersubgraph` read `diagnostics()` and use the inspector (changed, not run). **Not covered:** height-fog and soft-particle maths (only in a browser; no spec checks them), the `warmUp`-to-warmer glue (NullEngine can't run the frame graph). Left: `MaterialPreview.readCenter` / `window.shaderGraphPreview` (editor half), `pipelineTierKey` exported for its test. |
| [0189](../decisions/0189-environment-and-sky-thumbnails-draw-through-the-render-features.md) Environment and sky thumbnails draw through the render features | Done | **Scenes** `render/babylon/previews/thumbnailScenes.ts` (`ThumbnailScenes.prepare`, `ThumbnailScene`, `ThumbnailOptions`; `prepare`, `forEngine`, `prepareSky`, its own map loader and skybox, `waitFor` removed). **Hook** `BabylonFeature.settled?()` (`feature.ts`): the lighting rig (`BabylonLighting.settled`, `EnvMap.failed`; was `EnvironmentFeature`, ADR 0252), `SkyFeature` (`BabylonSky.settled`, `SkyDrawable.loading`); `BabylonRenderer.contentReady()` waits for it, `beforeStill(null)` while settling runs the bake. `SkyDrawable`/`SkyBaker` no longer exported. **Fixed:** a World's Environment never loaded a `*.cubemap.json` (the lighting asked `Assets.url` of a JSON asset); prefab and scene thumbnails under a sky were captured studio-lit (the bake was never promoted). **Tests:** `engine/tests/thumbnailEnvironments.test.ts` (a sky still is the feature's box alone, fitted per draw; a live sky's chrome sphere and drifting clouds; an environment that fails to load gives up at once; wrong kinds and missing files; no Sky feature, no sky; dispose; a prefab under a baking sky waits for and is lit by the bake), `babylonLighting.test.ts` (cubemap assets load; a missing map still warns), `thumbnailRenderFeatures.test.ts` and `babylonPreviews.test.ts` through `ThumbnailScenes`. **Not verified automatically:** pixels (NullEngine draws nothing; it can't decode images, so a loaded environment map is never shown in a test), the sky at the tier's shader level and the bake size on a live sky's sphere against the old pictures. `THUMBNAIL_FORMAT` not bumped. Material, shader graph, model and particle thumbnails stay on their own scenes (record, Decision). |

`exceptSceneSetting` keeps a scene set to another physics dimension from starting one by its Components; a service's `prepareScene` runs alongside its start) or on a needing Component attaching (`World._addComponent`), a load finishing after teardown starts nothing, reverse-order disposal in `World._destroyAll`, disposal on unregister, no start in editor Worlds by default (`runsIn`); optional per-service hooks `interpolate` (the Engine calls `_interpolateWorldServices` for rendered Worlds) and `raycast` (`_worldRaycast`, the particles' collision rays); `World.service(id)`; public `defineWorldService`/`worldService`. **Render features** `render/renderFeatures.ts`: `spec.after` constraints sorted by `byPhase` (stable, cycle → error), `_registerRenderFeature` (spec + factory loader); `BabylonRenderer` loads eager factories in `create` and appends plugin slots, with `BabylonFeatureHost` and the `fit`/`beforeFrame` hooks. **Activation** goes through the plugin set (ADR 0144, see its row), which drives `runtimeActivator`: player (`player/main.ts`), server (`server/run.ts`, `server: true`), editor boot (`plugins/editorBoot.ts`, for the edit World), project open and Play (`plugins/editorPlugins.ts`). **Public module** `src/myco.ts` (core plus the first-party runtime halves' exports: Sky, Particles, Physics, Navigation, Tilemaps…), used by the engine library, the player's `myco.js`, the `myco`/`tranquility` aliases (tsconfig, Vitest), the editor's script loader and the Monaco declarations (`tsconfig.engine.json` rooted at `src/`; `index.d.ts` re-exports `myco.d.ts`). **Sky** (step 2): `Sky` in `plugins/sky/runtime/Sky.ts`, `SkyFeature` in `plugins/sky/runtime/skyFeature.ts` (loaded on demand, `SKY_SPEC` in `runtime/index.ts`); `BabylonSky` stays in the renderer (thumbnails use its baker) and gets the live sky through a callback; `worldSkyPath`/`readSkyAsset` moved to `engine/sky/skyAsset.ts`; `skyFormat`, `presets`, `skyMath`, `cubemapFormat` stay in `engine/sky/` (lighting, thumbnails and the Environment inspector read them). **Physics 2D, Physics 3D, Navigation** (step 3): plugins `myco.physics2d`, `myco.physics3d` (merged into `myco.physics` plus `myco.physics-rapier` by 0137; see its row), `myco.navigation` (`src/plugins/<name>/{manifest.ts,runtime/}`, runtime halves only), each registering its Components, engine systems and World service. Moved: `Physics2DWorld`/`Physics3DWorld` (since split into the shared core and the Rapier backends, 0137)/`NavigationWorld`, every physics and navigation Component (`TilemapCollider2D` and `Buoyancy` included), the `Physics`/`Physics2D`/`Navigation` facades, queries, event buffers, the nav bake-bytes cache and the wasm runtime wrapper (`runtime.ts`). Needs: 3D by `"physics": "3d"` or its Component ids, 2D by `"physics": "2d"` or its Component ids (a scene saying nothing now starts 2D too), navigation by `NAV_RUNTIME_TYPES` in Worlds that simulate it. Removed: `ensurePhysics2D`/`3D`/`ensureNavigation`, `World.physics2D`/`physics3D`/`navigation`/`_navigationDisposed`, `usesPhysics3D`/`usesNavigation`/`PHYSICS_3D_TYPES`, the `SceneManager.load` scans, `ensureNavigationFor`, the Engine's interpolation calls and `_destroyAll`'s per-subsystem teardown; `engine.PhysicsRestoreInterpolated` split into a 2D and a 3D system. Stays in core: `physics/{types,layers,polygon,shape3d}.ts` (shared types; the tilemap's shapes, gizmos and nav geometry read them), `nav/{settings,geometry,plan,bake,loader,formation,debugGeometry}.ts` and `nav/wasm/` (project settings, the editor's bake and `npm run nav:bake`). `LifecycleHooks`' physics parameters are now core structural types (`Component`, `PhysicsCollision`, `PhysicsControllerHit`); scripts annotate `Collider2D`/`Collision2D` as before. Editor parts (gizmos, nav inspectors, baking, panels) stay in `src/editor/`, importing the runtime halves. Vitest activates `RUNTIME_PLUGINS` in a setup file (`plugins/testSetup.ts`, through a plugin set in `plugins/testPlugins.ts`); `nav:bake` imports `myco` so the plugins' Components register. Tests: `plugins/physics/physics.test.ts` (a RigidBody on a runtime-instantiated prefab starts 3D physics; a World torn down while Rapier loads gets none; scene-scan rules), `engine/tests/worldServices.test.ts`, `engine/tests/renderFeatures.test.ts`, `plugins/runtimeContext.test.ts`, `plugins/plugins.test.ts` (the engine imports no plugin code; only the player, server, the editor's and the tests' plugin sets and `myco.ts` import `RUNTIME_PLUGINS`). A game built on the engine library (`dist/engine/`) gets every first-party runtime half: `Engine.create` activates them through `Engine._defaultPlugins` (set by `src/myco.ts`) unless a host already chose a set (`runtimePluginsChosen`); not covered by a test. **Particles** (step 4 for one feature): `plugins/particles/runtime/` claims `ParticleSystem` and registers the lazy `particles` render feature (`PARTICLES_SPEC`, moved from `features.ts`; `particlesFeature.ts` moved from the renderer); `BabylonFeatureHost` gains `particleHost()` and `particlePreview()`, and the renderer finds the feature by name (`ParticlesFeatureApi`) for the editor preview, warm-up and the variant report. The component class, the effect compiler and `BabylonParticles` stay in core (thumbnails, editor overlays, warm-up). `ParticleSystem`/`ParticlePropertyValue` are exported by `myco.ts` from the plugin, not `engine/index.ts`. **The other render features** (step 4, ADR 0140): Sprites, Meshes and models, Water and UI register theirs from `plugins/{sprites,meshes,water,ui}/runtime/`; Environment and Post-processing claim their Components (see 0140's row). Missing: a render feature deactivated after a renderer started stays in that renderer. |

`assetBrowser.ts` re-exports `assetKind`, `canAddToScene`, `opensInCodePanel`, `AssetKind`. **Core kinds** `src/editor/assetKinds.builtin.ts` (`CORE_ASSET_KINDS`, `registerCoreAssetKinds()`, called by `main.tsx` at boot): scene, prefab (preview `inspector`, `ui/PrefabInspector.tsx`), image and audio (`importSettings`: `ui/ImageImportSettings.tsx` with `PivotRow`, `ui/AudioImportSettings.tsx`), rendertexture (`ui/RenderTextureInspector.tsx` as its `inspector`, newly reachable), json, xml, script, javascript, typescript, text. **SDK** `AssetKindContribution.importSettings` (`ImportSettingsContribution { preview?, view }`, `ImportSettingsViewProps`), `AssetSceneNodes.keepsOwnPosition`. **Plugins**: Particles registers `addToScene` for `particles` (`particleNodeEntry` moved to `plugins/particles/editor/particleAssets.ts`); Meshes registers the model's `importSettings` (`plugins/meshes/editor/ModelImportSettings.tsx`: scale, up axis, normals, pivots, material slots, Extract Materials). **Removed**: `KIND_LABELS` (`ui/Assets.tsx`), `ICONS` (`ui/AssetPreview.tsx`), `BuiltinAssetKind`, `builtinKind`, `builtinExtensionLength`, `CODE_KINDS`, `builtinNodeForAsset`, `canAddToScene`'s list (`assetBrowser.ts`), `previewKind`'s endings (`thumbnails/keys.ts`, now `previewOf`), `FILTERS` (`assetFieldKinds.ts`), `ImportKind`/`importKind`/`INSPECTED_KINDS` (`assetImport.ts`, now `hasImportSettings`), the model, image and audio rows and `KIND_TITLES` in `ui/AssetInspector.tsx` (now the import frame plus a kind's views), the one-line pass-throughs. **Engine** `AssetFieldKind` is `KnownAssetFieldKind | (string & {})` (`core/registry.ts`, `KnownAssetFieldKind` exported from `engine/index.ts`; `ASSET_FIELD_KINDS` unchanged); `assetKindsOf` keeps any name; the Inspector builds a datalist for single non-engine kinds; `assetsForKinds` warns once (Messages) about a non-engine field kind nothing satisfies. Tests: `editor/assetKindRegistry.test.ts` (one fake kind through the module, matching, ties, core kinds, slices, Code panel), `useCoreAssetKinds()` in `editor/testing.ts` used by the asset, code, thumbnail, Inspector and plugin tests, `assetFieldKinds.test.ts` (plugin field kind and warning), `plugins/particles/particles.test.ts` (adding effects), `plugins/renderPluginsEditor.test.ts` (model import settings); e2e on Chromium: `assetbrowser`, `assets`, `code`, `codefiles`, `inspector-fields`, `particles`, `shaderassets`, `spriteeditor`, `three-d` (model import settings), `thumbnails` (@slow), `materials`, `sky`, `tilemap`. Not covered automatically: the render texture Inspector in a browser; a third-party Component's field datalist in a browser. Open: `folder` registering, `rendertexture` moving to a plugin (record's open questions); `modelNodeEntries` still lives in `assetBrowser.ts`. |
| [0144](../decisions/0144-one-plugin-set-module-activates-plugins-for-every-host.md) One plugin set activates plugins for every host | Done | **Set** `src/plugins/pluginSet.ts` (host-neutral): `createPluginSet({ runtime, editor? })` with `HalfActivator` adapters; `apply({ plugins, disabled, host: { server, editor }, keepRuntime })` merges entries by name (runtime halves activate in their entries' order, then editor halves in theirs, so `RUNTIME_PLUGINS`' and `EDITOR_PLUGINS`' orders both hold), resolves enabled once (`resolveEnabledPlugins`), turns `server: false` runtime halves off on the server, deactivates halves no longer wanted or whose module changed (and the same halves of their dependents, dependents first), stubs off runtime halves (the activator's `disableRuntimePlugin` unclaims their Components), activates the rest in `orderPlugins` order, rolls back both halves of a plugin that throws and skips its dependents ("needs X, which failed to activate"), reports cycles, never throws, returns `{ active, problems: [{ plugin, where, message }] }`; a half deactivated outside the set is activated again (`isActive`). `keepRuntime` leaves runtime halves alone (reloads during Play). `throwOnPluginProblem`. **Adapters** `runtimeActivator` (`runtimeContext.ts`: `activateRuntimePlugin`, `disableRuntimePlugin`, `deactivateRuntimePlugin`, all private behind it; the low-level `activateRuntimePlugins` was removed, its tests go through `runtimeActivator`) and `plugins/editorActivator.ts` (`activatePlugin`/`deactivatePlugin` in `editor/sdk/plugins.ts`). **Hosts** player and server: one `activateGamePlugins(thirdParty, { disabled, server })` (`plugins/runtime.ts`) that throws the first problem (ADR 0139's broken-script rule); `myco.ts`'s default activation uses it too. Editor: one set in `plugins/editorPlugins.ts` (`applyEditorPlugins(disabled, { thirdParty, keepRuntime, layout })`, `reportPluginProblems`), applied at boot (`editorBoot.ts`, problems in the status bar), on project open (first-party with the previous project's third-party dropped, then first- and third-party from `loadProjectCode`, problems with the script problems), on a settings change (`reapplyPlugins`, status bar; Play keeps its plugins until Stop through `session.ts`'s `pendingPlugins`), on reload, before Play (`playMode.ts`) and on close (`unloadThirdPartyPlugins` drops third-party; first-party go back to all on). `thirdPartyEditor.ts` keeps discovery, checks, module loading and `toEditorPlugin`; a plugin whose code doesn't load becomes a half that throws, so the set reports it and skips its dependents. Removed: `plugins/editorApply.ts`, `thirdPartyEditor.ts`'s `activeRuntime`/`activeEditor` and `activateThirdParty`, `enabledRuntimePlugins`, `syncRuntimePlugins`. **Tests** `plugins/pluginSet.test.ts` (fake plugins and activators: ordering, per-half orders, diffing, re-apply keeps halves, unlisted plugins go, a changed module re-activates with dependents, `keepRuntime`, `server: false`, rollback of both halves and skipped dependents, never throws on cycles or throwing deactivation, a third-party plugin needing a disabled first-party one is off in editor and player hosts), `plugins/runtime.test.ts` and `server/server.test.ts` (a failing plugin stops the game / the server with its message). Tests activate editor halves through `useEditorPlugins` (`plugins/testEditorPlugins.ts`) and runtime halves through `applyTestPlugins` (`plugins/testPlugins.ts`); the low-level activator tests and the asset-kind tests (ADR 0143's area) still call the activators directly. Open: showing "needs X, which failed" inline in the Plugins category (the record's open question). |
| [0145](../decisions/0145-undo-and-redo-act-on-one-active-history-resolved-by-focus.md) One active history for Undo and Redo | Done | `src/editor/activeHistory.ts` (`UndoHistory`, `activeHistory`, `undo`/`redo`/`stepBack`/`stepForward`, `providePanelHistory`/`usePanelHistory`, `sceneHistory`, `assetDocumentHistory`, focus tracking); `shortcuts.ts`, `menuBar.ts` (`MenuState.history`), `ui/MenuBar.tsx`, `ui/HistoryPanel.tsx` route through it; `assetDocuments.ts` (`undoLabel`/`redoLabel`, `history.back`/`forward`, `KEEPS_HISTORY_FOCUS` with the menu bar), `TextHistory.undoLabel`/`redoLabel`, `EditHistory.canUndo`/`canRedo`; SDK exports in `sdk/index.ts`. Per-panel Z/Y handlers removed from Sky, Material, Shader Graph, Particle Graph (registers a history keeping frames), Formation, Tileset, Project Settings and Worlds (register `EditHistory`), Sprite Editor (registers its snapshots); `graph/history.ts` deleted. Tests: `activeHistory.test.ts` (routing: scene, asset document, panel-local, precedence, menu-bar clicks, nothing to undo), `menuBar.test.ts`, `assetDocuments.test.ts`. Not run for this change: e2e (`shadergraph.spec.ts`, `shadersubgraph.spec.ts`, `projectsettings.spec.ts`, `worldspanel.spec.ts`, `particles.spec.ts`, `navformation.spec.ts` press these keys or buttons). |
| [0146](../decisions/0146-the-editor-shell-reaches-plugins-only-through-contribution-points.md) The editor shell reaches plugins only through contribution points | Done | **SDK** `src/editor/sdk/contributions.ts` + `plugins.ts`: `HierarchyCreateContribution`/`hierarchyCreateEntries`/`registerHierarchyCreateEntry` (`nodeCreate.ts`'s `newNodeKinds()`, `contributedKind`, `NodeKind`; used by `ui/HierarchyMenu.tsx`, `menuBar.ts`, `hierarchyActions.ts`'s `createNodeIn` for `createAsset`), `AssetActionContribution`/`assetActions` (`ui/Assets.tsx`, after Open / Sprite Editor…), `PlayStatsCounterContribution`/`playStatsCounters` (`playStats.ts`'s `WorldStats.counters`, `ui/PlayStats.tsx`), `SceneOpenMigrationContribution`/`sceneOpenMigrations` (`sceneTabs.ts`, after a tab's history attaches), `IdlePrefetchContribution`/`idlePrefetches` (`prefetch.ts`); `ui/panelExtensions.tsx` (split from `ui/panels.tsx`; `ScenePanel.tsx` renders `scene` extensions). **Tilemaps**: `editor/tilesetAssets.ts` (+ test) moved from `src/editor/`; Create Tilemap entry and Create Tileset from Slices action in `editor/index.ts`. **Particles**: `createParticleEffect`/`effectFolder` merged into `editor/particleAssets.ts`; `editor/particlePreview.ts`, `editor/particleMigration.ts` (+ test), `editor/ui/ParticlePreviewOverlay.tsx` + `particlePreview.css` moved; Create Particle System entry, the `myco.particles.preview` viewport tool and `scene` panel extension, the embedded-effect migration in `editor/index.ts`. **Navigation**: the `nav agents` counter. **Physics**: idle prefetch of the core and backends via the new optional `PhysicsBackendRegistration.prefetch` (set by Rapier). **Boundary** `plugins/plugins.test.ts`: shell non-test code imports nothing from a plugin folder, only the listed host modules (with 0144's `pluginSet`, `editorPlugins`, `editorActivator`; and `editorBoot` from `main.tsx`). desktop: `create_editor_window` with an `on_new_window` handler in `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, checked against tauri 2.12.5 and `cargo check`ed on Linux. **Tests:** `layout/model.test.ts`, `layout/saved.test.ts`, `layout/savedStore.test.ts`, `menuBar.test.ts`, `windows/*.test.ts`, `plugins/editorBoot.test.ts`; e2e `tests/e2e/panels.spec.ts` (close, add, copies' own view state, save/switch/restore layouts, plugin off keep/remove, detach and move back), `tests/e2e/childwindow.spec.ts`. **Not covered:** detached windows on Windows (WebView2) and macOS (WKWebView) desktop; | [0251](../decisions/0251-particle-steps-are-written-once-and-the-cpu-simulation-becomes-a-kernel-executor.md) Particle steps written once; the CPU simulation becomes a kernel executor | Done | **Steps catalogue:** `render/particles/gpu/kernelSteps.ts` (`KERNEL_STEPS`: each built-in step's KSL and JavaScript; `KERNEL_GLOBALS_KSL`, `KERNEL_HELPERS_KSL`, `shapeKsl`, `DEPTH_STEP_KSL`), used by `kernelShader.ts` (generated WGSL/GLSL byte-identical, checked by diffing every generated shader for the samples, templates and every block) and `kernelJs.ts`; collider collision on the JavaScript executor (`collidersStart`/`colliders` steps, `KernelSystem.jsOnly`, the ray cast through `BabylonKernelHost`; a GPU-run system warns). **Every system on a kernel (2026-11-20):** `chooseBackend` returns an executor ("cpu" and plain "auto" the JavaScript one, "gpu" and "auto" systems with `needs` the best), and `KernelEffect` runs each system on its own (`executor`; the plan keeps an event child on its parent's); `render/particles/simulation.ts`, `render/babylon/particleMeshes.ts` and the CPU branch of `particleDraw.ts` (`SystemDraw`, `EmitterDraw`, `usesSheet`, `drawsAsSystem`, mesh-draw materials) are deleted; `ParticleProgram` is `spawns` (`SpawnSettings`, read by `KernelSpawner`) plus `kernels` (compiled with it, their problems in `problems`) and `simulation`, its bindings spawn inputs only (`ProgramRunner.spawns()`); `GraphProblem.cpuOnly`, `KernelSystem.unsupported`, `WarmParts.systems`, the variant report's particle-system scan and the `sheet` texture use are gone; `EffectPlayer.points()` (what the thumbnails frame) reads `KernelRun.cpuState`; a seeded restart repeats exactly (`KernelSpawner.restart(true)`). **Material trails:** `materialVertexCode` places a trail output's strip through the history (`k_trail`, `kphist`, `kptrail`), `KernelMaterialDraw` builds the strip, every run hands out its history as a texture in the state layout (`KernelTextures.history`/`frame`; WebGPU copies its buffer a row at a time), soft copies fade, sorted outputs sort. **Compared before deleting** (the record's "Building it"): the three samples' effects on both paths, 20 seeds, alive counts within 2%, positions, speed, size, alpha and age within noise; the JavaScript executor steps them faster (0.14 against 0.25 ms a frame for rain). Tests: `kernelSteps.test.ts`, `particleColliders.test.ts`, `particleKernel.test.ts`, `particleCompile.test.ts` (spawn settings, kernels, wires, events, the runner), `particleKernelMaterials.test.ts` (material trails: geometry, history texture, soft copy, the compiled shader), `babylonParticles.test.ts`, `babylonKernelParticles.test.ts`, `effectPlayer.test.ts` (seeded restarts by `points()`), `particleSoft.test.ts` (was `particleSoftCpu.test.ts`), `warmUpVariants.test.ts`, `babylonPreviews.test.ts`, `particleSeed.test.ts`; `particleSimulation.test.ts` deleted. **Not verified:** material trails on screen on any GPU (the WebGPU history copy compiles in no test), the JavaScript executor's upload cost with drawing in a browser (`npm run bench:particles` runs again since 2026-11-20, through `EffectPlayer`; its WebGL2 rows are simulation only and its WebGPU rows can't run in this container, see `bench/particles/README.md`). **Dormant "needs the GPU backend" wire styling removed (2026-11-20, the record's open question):** `ProblemIndex.gpuWires`, the dashed `.pg-wire.gpu` wires and the `.pg-wired-from.gpu` label are gone (`problems.ts`, `Wires.tsx`, `BlockView.tsx`, `GraphEditor.tsx`, `particleGraph.css`). |

Removed `core/hrirResample.ts`. **Tests** `cargo test -p audio-decode` (`tests/spatial.rs`: SHA-256 of both blobs at 12 rates, recorded from the removed TypeScript; the ABI; refusals; `spatial.rs` unit tests: resampler DC, sine, impulse, lengths, group delay); `decoder/spatialPrepare.test.ts` (wasm, handler, client, crash); `core/hrir.test.ts` (format 2 blob, loader, retarget while preparing, a resend before the blob lands, a failed retarget), `hrirMagls.test.ts`, `hrirBlob.test.ts`; unchanged digests in `audioGolden.test.ts` and `hrtfParity.test.ts`. e2e (coordinator): `audio-parity-hrtf.spec.ts`, `audio-parity.spec.ts`, `audio-spatial-play.spec.ts`, `audio-spatial-replay.spec.ts`, `audio-spatial-settings.spec.ts` (the HRIR and MagLS load through the real decoder worker). **Merged with the Audio Studio** (2026-11-23): decoder ABI **1.6** (the Studio's 1.4 `myco_decode_seam` and 1.5 Opus and `myco_decode_adpcm` beside these exports; `DECODER_ABI_MINOR` 6); the protocol carries both the package messages and the spatial ones. |

| [0333](../decisions/0333-the-websocket-transport-sends-unreliable-messages-over-a-webrtc-data-channel.md) WebRTC data channels for unreliable messages | Done | **Lane** `net/transport/datagramLane.ts` (`DatagramLane`: one `RTCPeerConnection` and an unordered, zero-retransmit channel `"myco-u"`; the server offers; local candidates wait for the description, early remote ones queue; 5 s open timeout; `hostCandidateRewriter` for `publicIp`). **Transport** `net/transport/websocket.ts`: `myco-rtc=1` query ask, signal frames (flag `0x02`, JSON), sequenced unreliable frames (flag `0x04`, 32-bit, serial-number stale check; since [0398](../decisions/0398-each-websocket-path-numbers-its-unreliable-messages-on-its-own.md) each path has its own sequence space and stale check, `PathSequence`, so a message too big for a datagram is delivered when datagrams overtake it), datagrams when open and the message fits `maxDatagramBytes` (1,100), `webrtc` option (`webRtcOptions`), payload size checked after decoding, signal frames up to 16 KiB. `TransportConnection.maxDatagramBytes` (`net/Transport.ts`). **Node** `src/server/websocketServer.ts`: node-datachannel's polyfill loaded lazily for both roles; `udpPort` (ICE UDP mux), `publicIp`, `bindAddress`. **Netcode** `NetPeer.datagramBodyBytes` (`net/session.ts`) sizes snapshot parts (`net/replication/server.ts`, ADR 0334's row); `ListenDefaults.options` carries the dedicated server's settings to runtime listens. **Server** `--udp-port`, `--public-ip`, `--no-webrtc` (`src/server/main.ts`, `run.ts`), stats line `(N on WebRTC)`. **Tests** `src/engine/tests/webrtcTransport.test.ts` (real Node-to-Node lanes: open, close, no-WebRTC client, `webrtc: false` either end, timeout fallback, one UDP port for two clients with `publicIp`, bad options, framing and stale drops over fake sockets; ADR 0398's per-path sequences over a fake peer connection whose lane opens without a network: an oversize socket frame overtaken by later datagrams still arrives, repeats on either path are stale), `sessionWebSocket.test.ts` (snapshots split into datagram-sized parts and 300 ghosts arrive; listen-default options), `src/server/server.test.ts` (flags, stats line). **Not automated:** a browser client (checked by hand: headless Chromium against the Node server, channel open in 215 ms, 50 of 50 datagrams); real packet loss; Safari, Firefox and the Tauri webviews. Browser-to-browser hosting and WebTransport are deferred. |
| [0334](../decisions/0334-snapshots-split-into-datagram-sized-parts-each-acked-on-its-own.md) Snapshot parts | Done | **Amended by [0405](../decisions/0405-late-snapshot-parts-are-read-in-tick-order-and-acked.md)** (late parts read and acked). **Amended by [0402](../decisions/0402-ghost-creation-goes-reliably-apart-from-per-tick-state.md)**: parts carry states only; spawns, despawns, leaves, relayouts and structure changes moved to reliable lifecycle messages (its row), and the ack leads with the lifecycle tick. **Server** `net/replication/server.ts`: `snapshot` writes the tick whole when it fits a part (one datagram body, `NetPeer.datagramBodyBytes`, or `maxSnapshotBytes` without a datagram path), else `pack` fills parts first-fit among the newest `FIT_LOOKBACK` (4): despawns and leaves, pending relayouts and structure changes, then ghosts by priority (a ghost goes with its pending items); `writeWithin` sheds lowest-priority ghosts from a part that runs over; at most `maxSnapshotBytes` per tick and `MAX_SNAPSHOT_PARTS` (1024) parts; `history` keeps each part's `Sent`, nulled once acked (`acked`/`applyAck`); `lastBytes` is the tick's total and `lastParts` its part count. **Wire** a part index first in `net.snapshot`; `net.snapshotAck` is tick, count, first part and gaps (`wire.ts`). **Client** `net/replication/client.ts` reads each part of the newest tick once and acks every part of it read. **Tools** `NetPeerInfo.snapshotParts`; the Network panel shows "… in N packets". **Tests** `src/engine/tests/replicationParts.test.ts` (a test transport over the loopback with `maxDatagramBytes`: parts each within a datagram and every ghost arriving, acks bringing it back to one cheap part, `maxSnapshotBytes` per tick, 30% loss with 200 moving ghosts converging with no read errors (fails if an ack covers every part of its tick), structure changes under loss), `sessionWebSocket.test.ts` (over a real WebRTC lane), `netTools.test.ts`. **Not automated:** the Network panel's "in N packets" text; performance (measured by hand: 2,000 moving ghosts, one client, about 2.7 ms per snapshot in 37 parts against 2.0 ms whole). Parts reordered in the network are dropped by the transport's stale rule. |
| [0335](../decisions/0335-gamepads-come-from-a-native-sdl3-adapter-on-desktop-and-device-state-reaches-worlds-as-entities-in-an-input-phase.md) Native SDL3 gamepads on desktop; device entities in an `input` phase | Done (hardware unverified) | **Phase**: `input` in `core/systems.ts` (`PHASES`), run by `World.step` after the clock and before `start()` (amends 0011); built-ins may be `predicted`. **Snapshot** `input/devices.ts`: keyboard, pointer, gamepads, touch (10 slots, primary, pinch, swipe) and pen (pressure, tilt, twist, buttons), timestamped raw events, pollers, gamepad output (rumble, trigger rumble, LEDs, sensors, `triggerEffect`). **Control paths** `input/controls.ts`. **Adapters**: DOM (`domDevices.ts`, `pointerType`, fingers by `pointerId`), Gamepad API (`webGamepads.ts`, raw state only, mapping wasm loaded on the first pad), Tauri SDL3 (`tauriGamepads.ts` + `src-tauri/src/gamepad.rs`, also in exported desktop games), **WebHID** (`input/webHid/`: DualSense/Edge, DualShock 4 and its adapter, Switch Pro, USB and Bluetooth, factory motion calibration, adaptive triggers, lights; `Input.requestHidGamepads()`; owns its pads, the Gamepad API takes them back on failure). **Mapping crate** `native/input/` (families, web ids, SDL GameControllerDB including sticks composed from two buttons or hat directions; `npm run build:input`, committed wasm ~664 KB, mostly the database; CI `input-native.yml`). **Entities** `input/deviceEntities.ts` (`InputDevice`, `GamepadState`, `TouchState`, `PenState`, `DeviceEvents`), none in editor, server or non-input Worlds. `Input.inject` is the public input end. **Tests** `inputActions`, `inputModules`, `inputWebHid`, `devices`, `uiCore` tests; `cargo test` in `native/input` (16) and `src-tauri` (5). **Manual**: [hardware checklist](../plans/2026-11-input-hardware-checklist.md). Not verified: real controllers, Windows and macOS builds, WebHID byte layouts on real firmware. Since: gamepad touchpad points (`<Gamepad>/touchpad0…`, `gamepadTouch`) and battery (`gamepadBattery`, `PlayerInput.battery`) on SDL, WebHID and `Input.inject`; WebHID Joy-Cons (single, and left+right joined automatically) and Switch HD rumble with SDL's amplitude table, 30 ms write limit and refresh; desktop DualSense trigger effects through `SDL_SendGamepadEffect`, sharing `input/dualSenseEffects.ts` with WebHID; a hardware recorder (`input/recording.ts`, taps in every adapter) and replay through the real adapters (`input/replay.ts`, fixture `src/engine/tests/fixtures/input/`, `inputRecording.test.ts`). Missing: the Joy-Con USB charging grip, per-side motion for a joined pair; the only fixture is constructed (record real controllers with the checklist). |

Since: the debugger's game-view overlay (`editor/ui/InputOverlay.tsx`, shared sampling `editor/inputDebugFeed.ts`), Record/Stop saving recordings, and Bind reading the engine's device snapshot (`watchRawPresses`), so it captures SDL and WebHID pads, touch and pen; the control picker comes from the engine (`namedControlPaths`). Browser specs: recorder and overlay in `tests/e2e/input.spec.ts`. **Samples:** King of Pirates reads its controls as actions with gamepad defaults (`samples/pirates/scripts/controls.ts`; prompts in the HUD, focus navigation in its menu and leave dialog). |
| [0337](../decisions/0337-input-players-are-entities-networked-actions-are-input-streams-and-rebinding-is-data.md) Players as entities, networked actions as input streams, rebinding as data | Done | **Players** `input/players.ts` (join policies in `project.json` `input`, `ControlledBy` copies onto pawns). **Netcode** `input/inputNet.ts` (networked sets are `defineInput` streams, quantized at the source, server pawns get each owner's actions and `ActionEvents`, prediction restores per replayed tick, several local players per connection: 0338). **Rebinding** `input/rebind.ts` (conflicts against every known context; device groups keyboard/gamepad/touch/pen). **Prompts and glyphs** `input/prompts.ts`, `input/glyphs.ts` (`InputGlyphs`: original SVGs generated on use for every family, `asset:@myco/glyphs/…`). **Accessibility** `input/profiles.ts` (hold-to-toggle, invert Y, sensitivity, dead zone, repeat). **Rebinding screen** `ui/controlMapper.ts` (`UIControlMapper`, UI plugin component; mouse, and keyboard and gamepad through the game UI's focus navigation, ADR 0340). **Tests** `inputActions`, `inputEvaluation`, `inputNet`, `inputGlyphs`, `uiControlMapper` tests, e2e `tests/e2e/input-mapper.spec.ts`. |

| [0341](../decisions/0341-the-audio-studio-is-its-own-app-on-the-editors-frontend-and-replaces-the-editors-audio-panels.md) The Audio Studio is its own app | Partly done | **Entry and boot** `studio.html`, `src/studio/main.tsx`, `boot/` (project opening, start query, plugins, lock and watch `studioProjectWatch.ts`, moves `studioAssetMoves.ts`, migration prompt); **runtime** `runtime/studioAudioHost.ts`, `engineRuntime.ts` (internal `audioOnly` World, forced core, `Audio.debug().coreUnavailable` banner), `takeReplay.ts`; **shell** `shell/` (nav, per-screen docks over `layout/layoutStore.ts`, router `route.ts`, keymap, replay store, player bar slots); **screens** Samples, Playlists (+ Sequence view), Mixer, Spatial, Take, Profiler in `src/studio/screens/`. **Editor side** Window > Audio Studio, Open in Audio Studio, double-click opens Studio files (`studioLaunch.ts`, `studioAssets.ts`, `studioHandoff.ts`); `projectWatch.ts`, `projectLocks.ts` (pending moves), `editorProjectWatch.ts`; the editor's audio authoring panels removed (W7-A); Project Settings > Audio points at the Studio. **Tauri** `create_studio_window`, standalone `src-studio-tauri/` (`tauri:studio:dev/build`). **Tests** Vitest across `src/studio/**` and the editor modules; stories beside every view. **Not verified:** any Tauri build or run (no webkit2gtk in the build container; `cargo check` only), the stories' play functions and e2e (run at the end of the build), the live link (later record). |
| [0342](../decisions/0342-a-sample-is-an-audio-file-plus-non-destructive-edits-in-its-sidecar.md) Samples: sidecar edits | Partly done | **Format** `src/engine/audio/sample/` (sample block, derived samples with fork follow/pin, WAV chunk read/write, `wavSample.ts`), sidecar block in `scene/importSettings.ts`; **loudness** `engine/audio/loudness/integrated.ts` (BS.1770); **core** loop programs (ABI 4.3: directions, counts, crossfades, hold points, activations, exits, re-entry), start offsets; **Studio** Samples screen (import with metadata review, timeline, markers, loops and seam, trim, tempo, root, normalise, razor and close gap, file diff/revert/detach, merge review model, Write to file with backup, forks/snippets/duplicates). Derived samples play at runtime: `engine/audio/sample/resolve.ts` resolves a `*.sample.json` to its source file (chains, cycle guards), its edits (a following fork reads its parent's resolved block live) and a snippet's range as trim; the clip resolver plays the source file with them, picking up edits and hot reloads. One-shots, `AudioSource` and `AudioZone` apply the sample plan (trim, normalisation gain, first loop region, held loops released by `AudioSource.release()`; shared in `clipResolver` `samplePass` and `sampleVoice.ts`); `sample.source` (a flattened render) plays in place of the source with its import settings and without the baked gain, trim/loops/markers keeping source time. Flatten (W9): `renderSample` (`engine/audio/sample/sampleRender.ts`) renders the baked chain, its automation and the normalize gain over the source's own timeline (tails appended; trim, cuts, deleted snippets and closed gaps stay runtime edits) via `myco_audio_offline.wasm` (`native/audio/crates/audio-offline`, own 256 KB tripwire); the Studio's Flatten / Re-flatten / Unflatten writes `<name>.flat<n>.wav` and sets `sample.source` undoably. Detection (W11): tempo (spectral-flux onsets, autocorrelation comb, 120 BPM prior, ½×/2× candidates, tempo map on clear changes) and root (YIN, note + cents) in `engine/audio/sample/detect/` on a Studio worker, wired to the existing Detect buttons and the import's "Detect what's missing" (`source: "detected"`, never over file or set values at import); re-import with source versions (`.myco/sample-versions/`, `sample.sourceVersion`, undo restores the audio; keep seconds or scale; merge review; past-the-end flagged); renders on a worker with a content-hash cache in `.myco/cache/samples/`; export renders baked samples without a render (WAV sources); sample automation lanes in the shared curve editor; `entry` markers. **Missing:** a decoder for non-WAV export renders, the loudness cache, full loop programs on clip voices, trim end after a held loop is released, the trim stop on the core instead of the frame, re-import and source versions, tempo/root detection, cue-marker reports from the core, full loop programs on clip voices (one-shots, AudioSource, zones loop the first region without crossfade). Stream voices run loop programs in the decoder worker (sample-accurate wraps, shaped seam crossfades via `myco_decode_seam`, decoder ABI 1.4, counts, later loops, key-off; forward only). |
| [0343](../decisions/0343-playlists-replace-audio-events-and-interactive-music.md) Playlists replace audio events and music | Partly done | **Format** `src/engine/audio/playlist/` (types, parse/write, references, lint, blend weights, picks, switch rules, gate, envelope, `clipDefaults.ts`); **migration** `engine/audio/migrate/` + `npm run audio:migrate`, on open in both apps; **runtime** `runtime/playlists/` (Clip, Queue incl. continuous, Switch with beat/bar/exit rules and transition bridges, Blend 1D/2D, Layer, Sequence playhead with sync/async clips and loop overrides, stingers, markers, `handle.music`, beat callbacks, instance automation, hot reload), `AudioPlaylistHandle`, `onMarker`, `onAudioMarker`, `EventOptions.offset`; **Studio** Playlists screen and Sequence view. **Retired:** the 0158/0166 event and music player runtime (shared host in `runtime/playlists/host.ts`, `ParameterDef` in `engine/audio/parameters.ts`, old formats read-only under `migrate/legacy/`); the runtime, exports and server refuse `myco.audioevent@1`; samples migrated; guide `docs/wiki/guide/playlists.md`. Migration: looping clips put their loop in the sample's sidecar `sample.loops` (held where the old event released it), or in a derived `*.sample.json` when the sidecar has a different loop (`migrate/sampleLoops.ts`). `Audio.debug().music` rows come from playlist instances with a tempo; `handle.music.transition` is filled. Parameter renames: one engine walker in `playlist/references.ts` (`playlistParameterReferences`, `renamePlaylistParameter` global/local) used by both Studio renames. **Missing:** the CLI's wording/count for sample files (they print as playlist writes), regenerable seam goldens (writer deleted), group priming of streamed sync clips. **Samples:** King of Pirates plays every sound as a playlist (`samples/pirates/sounds/`, a shared attenuation preset) on its `audio.json` mix (Storm parameter automation, ducking, a code-started snapshot). |
| [0344](../decisions/0344-the-mix-moves-to-audio-json-with-returns-vcas-and-blending-snapshots.md) The mix in `audio.json` | Partly done | **Format** `engine/audio/project/` (`audio.json` parse/write, migration `legacy.ts`, `runtimeView.ts`, shared snapshot model `snapshots.ts`/`snapshotPlayer.ts`); **runtime** mixer runs the model natively (VCA lane, mute, main-side returns, blending snapshots with fades/hold/priority/add and effect overrides, project live ops `projectOps.ts`), `Audio.snapshot(name)` handle; hosts read `audio.json`, editor migrates `project.json` on open; **Studio** Mixer screen and Global parameters panel. Core bus-to-return sends `BUS_SENDS` (ABI 4.7: 4 post-fader sends a bus, ramps, core-side send muting). Returns run on the core (W9): each return is a bus of `coreSectionOf` after the buses (output, fader, mute, inserts); bus sends are `BusSettings.sends` written as `BUS_SENDS` with ramped levels, `BUS_SEND_MUTED` for a muted bus, replayed after a rebuild, removed sends fading out; return lanes, snapshot offsets, insert overrides, `fx:` automation and ducking (level rules) as on buses; `sendsPerBus` rule (4). ABI 4.10: every automation target runs in the core, keyed on the parameter's smoothed value — bus and return effect parameters by catalogue index (log2 curves for frequencies) and send dB (BUS_AUTOMATE fx/send, 16 bindings); snapshot overrides hold their parameters against automation; snapshot fades are one `Decibel` BUS_RAMP; the Babylon fallback warns once that returns, sends and bus effects need the core; Studio undo/redo of live moves carry undo/redo MIXOP origins. **Missing:** slots beyond 32 shared by buses and returns (a reverb return that doesn't fit still runs as the shared reverb send). |
| [0345](../decisions/0345-one-effect-catalogue-runs-at-four-levels.md) One effect catalogue at four levels | Partly done | **Catalogue** `engine/audio/effects/catalogue.ts`; **core** instance submixes (ABI 4.5: 64 slots, low-pass/high-pass/compressor chains for root playlist effects), voice pan and high-pass targets (ABI 4.4); **Studio** EffectChain/EffectCard at every level. **DSP** `native/audio/crates/audio-dsp/src/fx/` (ABI 4.6): every catalogue kind except the offline-only ADSR, noise reduction and reverse, smoothed parameters, bypass keeps state, large state in the core's `FxMem` region (20 s stereo); bus chains run 8 effects of any kind, no compressor limit, sidechain compressor keyed by a bus; instance submixes run every kind except the sidechain compressor. Per-level submixes (ABI 4.9): nested playlists', sequence tracks' and clips' chains each run on a submix of their own (keyed in the instance, reused per pass), feeding their parent level's on the same bus (8 deep), voices named by ROUTE; tails ring after the instance ends; a full pool frees the nearest-ending tail, then refuses (warned once); gain runs at the instance level. Instance submix parameter automation (SUBMIX_AUTOMATE, ABI 4.10; global, local or `sequenceTime` inputs). Starved effect memory is reported (STATUS `fxStarved`/`fxStarvedOwner`, `Audio.debug().mix`, Studio status bar). **Missing:** automation of per-level submixes, `voiceTime` lanes on an instance submix, true per-voice chains (ADSR), (sample level renders offline through the shared kernels, with fades, a spectral-gate noise reduction and the ADSR in `audio-offline`; reverse is native but left out of renders because it would move source-time loops and markers), golden scenes for the catalogue kernels. |
| [0346](../decisions/0346-one-curve-model-with-bezier-keys-and-time-as-a-core-parameter.md) Bézier curves, time parameters, one curve editor | Done | **Curve** `engine/core/curve.ts` Bézier keys with the Rust evaluator and parity (ABI 4.1), 64-key limit `audioCurveLimit.ts`; **time parameters** voiceTime/clipTime/sequenceTime in the core (ABI 4.2) used by the playlist runtime; **editor** `src/editor/ui/curves/` (model, dialog, inline field, SDK exports), used by particles, Mixer automation, Playlists voice curves, Sequence and Spatial attenuation. **Not built:** And by (by design), a clock selector. Bindings carry a time origin (AUTOMATE `offset`, ABI 4.9): time-keyed lanes bind one curve id per curve whatever the voice's start. |
| [0347](../decisions/0347-attenuation-curves-live-on-playlists-audio-zones-and-spatial-test-scenes.md) Attenuation curves, audio zones, test scenes | Partly done | **Format** `engine/audio/attenuation/`, `engine/audio/spatial/testScene.ts`; **runtime** attenuation curves on the playlist instance (main thread), `AudioZone` component + editor Inspector/gizmo, `dopplerAmount`, four reverb presets + `levelDefault`, test scene player `runtime/testScene/player.ts`; **Studio** Spatial screen. Per-voice spread in the core (ABI 4.11: SET_VOICE_FX `spread`; point part plus a decorrelated diffuse part; interaural correlation 1 − s/360; stereo keeps its image) from `spatial.spread`, the attenuation spread curve and zones; voices past `audio.bed.beyond` and inside an audio zone go to their bus's bed (panner pick); the four ADR 0347 presets have their own responses (smallStone 1.1 s, canyonHall 4.8 s, metalTunnel 2.8 s, forest 1.5 s; loopback T20 within 2%). **Missing:** a metered spread scene; on the Studio side nothing (the Inspector's Scene section, with nothing selected, picks the base game scene from the project's `*.scene.json` files, `setBaseScene`; base-scene emitters and zones appear in the Voices table and rays; rays follow moving emitters). **Moving emitters (W11):** the Studio's Engine steps its World each frame, so route movers advance and the core's Doppler is heard (loopback-measured 1.0617 / 0.9451 at 20 m/s, matching c/(c∓v) within 1e-4; project `doppler.amount`, playlist `spatial.doppler` and a dragged listener all apply); `engine/audio/spatial/routeTravel.ts` carries velocity across a near-closed loop's wrap and jumps a long one (teleport guard zeroes it); the plan draws live position and heading, the Inspector live speed, distance and Doppler ratio. **Added (W10):** base game scene loading (`engine/audio/spatial/baseScene.ts` imports audio components and 3D colliders from the resolved scene view; `runtime/testScene/baseScene.ts` loads it read-only through `SceneManager`; plan layer and banner), 3D physics occlusion in the Studio (the Studio activates the physics and Rapier runtime halves). |

PanelFields (editor kit), FadeShapeSelect and Filmstrip (Studio kit). |
| [0350](../decisions/0350-playlists-shape-blends-fades-tails-and-quantised-automation.md) Playlists shape blends, fades and tails, and quantise automation | Partly done | **Format** 1D blend regions, weight curves (1D and 2D), one fade-shape set (`playlist/fadeShapes.ts`) for transitions, Queue overlaps, sequence loop exits and regions, Exit-marker tails (`ChangeHow.tail`, on by default), loop tails, `entry` markers (pre-entry), bridge fades, per-lane `quantize`; lints. **Runtime** shaped stamped fades, ring-out, wrap tails, pre-entry, quantised gain ramps on the musical clock (`quantizedLanes.ts`). **Migration** restores the Sound Lab engine, music-loop, music-world and music-intensity. **Studio** shape picker, Switch fades, tail and bridge, region trapezoids, weight-curve presets and editor, 2D influence tint, lane quantize, tail shading. Samples have `entry` markers read by pre-entry; the Samples waveform shades the post-exit tail and pre-entry. Quantise (ADR 0353) also covers pitch, pan and filter lanes and entry voice curves (voice-time binding stepped at the boundary, within one quantum); an unstamped sequence waits for its clips' data so playhead and first sound agree. Core seam and exit crossfades take the fade-shape set (LOOPS `shapes`, ABI 4.9; `LoopBehaviour.crossfadeShape`/`exitShape`). **Missing:** a Samples-screen loop shape picker, quantise on other effect parameters, a Studio control for entry-curve quantise, post-exit ring inside a continuous Queue, separate fade-out/in lengths and offsets. |

`--platform`, `--no-audio-convert`; games ship `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`; loose ADPCM falls back to Vorbis. **A5:** `Audio.package(name)`/`Audio.packages`, ref-counted loads and clips, unload with fade or `finish`, strict `notLoaded` (editor Play too), `AudioPackageLoader` and scene preload, `Audio.debug().packages`, capture `PKG`, `Assets.platform` android. **A4:** clip format 6 (IMA ADPCM) held compressed in the arena and decoded per voice each quantum, bit for bit with int16 reads (`audio-core/adpcm_voice.rs`, ABI 4.12; `backend.codecs`. **A7:** libopus fixed point in both decoder builds (ABI 1.5; pre-skip, end trimming, 0.5 s seek pre-roll), symphonia `adpcm` for WAV IMA; decoder tripwires 1,000/1,350 KB. **Integration (W12):** packages load into the decoder (`loadPack`, re-fetched from the build URL on a worker restart, so main keeps no copy); `decode` and ADPCM `compressed` entries and `stream` heads are committed to the arena at load (`AudioBackend.packs`), so progress, `memoryBytes` and preload reflect it; unload releases holds and the pack; compressed Vorbis/Opus stream from the worker; `stream` sounds start on their arena head and hand over to the ring through two stamped voices (ADPCM 37.6 dB; seam within codec error). **Missing:** heads for compressed Vorbis/Opus (needs `prefetchMs` in the TOC), packaged music stems, a core head-to-ring voice if pitch or pause during a head must keep the seam, span clips and loop programs of ADPCM held compressed, music groups from packs, the capture memory section, A8 docs/e2e, the Samples render worker loading plugins, derived samples' own `encoding` block (read-only in the Studio), Hear as preparing a playlist's or take's sounds itself, Convert now rendering unflattened baked effects, a hint for scene-named sounds in no package. **A6 (W12):** Samples Inspector Encoding section (per-platform values with origins, overrides and resets per key and platform, matches, estimated and exact sizes and memory via on-demand encodes cached in the export's layout); lint of `Audio.package` names; Profiler memory track (arena from STATUS, package memory from PKG events, memory argument added within capture format 2); editor-Play glob membership without a file list, a derived sample's `encoding` write-back. |

a document shows the text without tags); line breaking and direction work across a text's runs. **0423 wave D (2026-11-26):** T3's `t:` attributes (per-row keys, `ui.data` arguments, content fallback, button labels) went with UI documents; components use `t()`/`tHtml()` from `myco` (reactive, 0423 wave X2) and scripts `Locale.t`; `:lang()` stays.
